# Remediation Checklist - kasir.mu

**Progress at 2026-09-23.** **10 checklist items fully ticked** (C5, C5b, C6, C6b, C7 detection half, C9, C11 code half, C12, C13, C27) **plus 29 further slices landed inside items whose checkbox stays open until the whole item is done** (C1 S1 and S1.5, C2 step 1, C3 S1, C8 S1 and S2, C10a, C17 slices 1-2, C18 P1.1, P1.2, P1.3, P1.5). Every one names its commit SHA, and the verification log at the end carries the command and its result. Do not read a ticked line as 'this area is finished' - read the annotation beside it for the remainder.

Derived from manager-codebase-review.md (commit 954d4b094), 2026-09-23. Nothing here is new evidence: every item traces to a numbered section of that review, and the acceptance check is the one stated there.
---

## Wave 1 - before the next release (P0)

- [ ] **C1 [P0] Default to a real at-rest key instead of a public constant** (6.2, remediation P0-7) - **S1 DONE (012dd2663), S1.5 DONE (37a3c00b)**; S2a/S2b/S2c (install-key seam, keychain entry, `oz rekey`) and S3 (the .ozpkg PII pin) remain, plus one named follow-up: the two callers that swallow the new fail-closed error with `.unwrap_or(None)` (apps/desktop-tauri/src/lib.rs:703, platform/sync/src/daemon.rs:156) still degrade silently to "no credential" instead of surfacing it.
  - **THE ONE D1-INDEPENDENT DEFECT WAS FOUND AND FIXED (commit `25e310446`): three callers swallowed the fail-closed credential error.** S1.5's `decrypt_or_fail_closed` (`platform/core/src/settings/typed.rs:719-733`) refuses to hand back a value with ciphertext shape that decrypts under no derivation. Three sites discarded that `Err` into a *weakest* default, converting an integrity failure into a configuration one: `apps/desktop-tauri/src/lib.rs:774` (`.unwrap_or(None)` -> "no PSK", so an external bind was refused as merely *unset*), `platform/sync/src/daemon.rs:315-316` (`.unwrap_or(None)` -> silently demoted to the WEAKER admin-key auth path), and `platform/sync/src/pg_daemon.rs:277-279` (`.unwrap_or_default()` -> an EMPTY password, i.e. an integrity failure presented to PostgreSQL as a blank credential). Each now matches on the `Result` and logs at `error` naming the key, while keeping the same safe fallback -- so the operator can tell `unreadable` from `unset`. The third site was **not in the item or the checklist**; it was found by censusing every caller of the typed getters.
  - **THE SECOND D1-INDEPENDENT ITEM IS NOW FIXED TOO (commit `84f13a813`): the SMTP at-rest family reads through candidate keys.** This closes the stated precondition, which was 5 of 6 done. `decrypt_smtp_at_rest` (`crates/kasirmu-crypto/src/lib.rs:277-284`) was the ONE reader still calling single-key `portable_key(SMTP_AT_REST_DOMAIN, derive_static_key)` instead of `candidate_keys`, so a row written while `OZ_MASTER_KEY` was set could not be opened once the master branch was in use -- the orphaning D1's blast radius enumerates, and the reason the S1 gate test (which exercises `sync_api_key` only) missed it.
  - **The fix keeps the legacy-passthrough arm, which is the part that could have been broken by a careless edit.** `looks_like_ciphertext` still gates the passthrough, so a non-ciphertext value returns byte for byte; the only change is that "fails" now means "fails under EVERY candidate" rather than "fails under the one legacy key", which is exactly what lets the master branch open a legacy row and vice versa. `AES-GCM` tag verification remains the sole oracle, so no marker column or version byte was introduced.
  - **RED/GREEN with the production path driven directly, not reimplemented.** A private `decrypt_smtp_at_rest_under(encrypted, keys)` seam takes the candidate list, and the public reader now calls it with `candidate_keys(SMTP_AT_REST_DOMAIN, derive_static_key)`. The test asserts on THAT seam, so it exercises the shipping code rather than a copy of it; the sibling `decrypt_with_candidates` case takes the same shape for the same documented reason (a `set_var` on `OZ_MASTER_KEY` would race every other case in the binary that reads it). **Falsified:** restricting the reader to `&keys[..1]` — the pre-fix behaviour — fails the test with `Internal("decryption failed: aead::Error")` on a master-written row; restoring it passes.
  - **Evidence:** `cargo test -p kasirmu-crypto --lib` -> **22 passed, 0 failed**; `cargo test -p kasirmu-core --test credential_storage_form` -> **28 passed, 1 ignored** (the pin that classifies `local_api.secret` as plaintext still holds, so nothing about the storage contract moved); `cargo fmt -p kasirmu-crypto -- --check` clean.
  - **Still NOT done in C1, and now the whole remainder is D1-gated:** S2a/S2b/S2c and S3 (install-key seam, keychain entry, `rekey` CLI, `.ozpkg` PII pin) are all absent by symbol search, and the "Done when" clause *"a test asserts the static fallback cannot be reached in a release build"* still has no mechanism -- `derive_static_key` is the default path of every release build and the crate contains no `cfg` gate of any kind. With the reader now branch-tolerant on all six families, that static-fallback gate is the last piece of C1 that does not wait on an owner answer, and it is the one the item itself names.
  - **A SECOND GAP IN THE "DONE" READER, measured and NOT fixed because it is arguably S1's own completion rather than new work:** `decrypt_smtp_at_rest` (`crates/kasirmu-crypto/src/lib.rs:277-284`) still calls the single-key `portable_key`, NOT `candidate_keys`, so it is the ONLY at-rest family outside the branch-tolerant read path. Setting `OZ_MASTER_KEY` would therefore orphan the stored SMTP password -- one of D1's seven stated blast-radius locations. The S1 gate test exercises `sync_api_key` only, which is why the gap survived. Named with its file:line rather than fixed, because the item's own record calls the branch-tolerant reader D1's precondition and changing a crypto read path is a decision-adjacent edit.
  - **THE STATIC FALLBACK IS STILL FULLY REACHABLE IN RELEASE, and the item's own done-condition says so in a clause with NO test and NO mechanism.** `crates/kasirmu-crypto/src/lib.rs:77-84 derive_static_key` is selected at `:111-116` whenever `OZ_MASTER_KEY` is unset or malformed; the crate contains no `cfg` gate of any kind (only `#[cfg(test)]` at `:142` and `:453`), and a grep for `debug_assertions` or any release guard returns zero. The "Done when" clause *"a test asserts the static fallback cannot be reached in a release build"* is unsatisfied. Re-verified on the owner-decision fact too: **nothing sets `OZ_MASTER_KEY`** -- 37 mentions tree-wide, ZERO in `ops/`, `scripts/`, `.github/`, `.env.example`, any compose or Dockerfile, and the only `set_var` in the tree is a test's child probe. D1's premise holds: the public-constant key is live on every shipped install.
  - **Slices S2a/S2b/S2c and S3 remain absent, confirmed by symbol search:** no install-key seam, no `oz-pos/at-rest-key.v1` keychain entry, no `rekey` CLI subcommand, no `.ozpkg` PII pin. These are the D1-gated half and were not attempted.
  - **Two line drifts in the checklist corrected by measurement:** it cited `apps/desktop-tauri/src/lib.rs:703` and `platform/sync/src/daemon.rs:156` for the swallowing callers; the real sites were `:774` and `:315-316`. (`lib.rs:760` is the raw non-secret `lan_server.bind` read and was deliberately left alone.)
  Fence: crates/kasirmu-crypto/src/lib.rs:59-111; the boot path of apps/desktop-tauri/src/lib.rs, apps/mobile-tauri/src/lib.rs, apps/cloud-server/src/main.rs; the README clause that conflates the keychain with the at-rest key.
  **Slices from the D1 design** (each independently shippable, in this order): **S1** branch-tolerant read in crates/kasirmu-crypto/src/lib.rs - a candidate-key helper (legacy first, master second) behind one decrypt path that treats the AES-GCM tag as the sole oracle; no marker column, no envelope version byte, no schema change, no eager rewrap. Gate = the existing pin test in crates/kasirmu-core/tests/credential_storage_form.rs **inverted** (child under OZ_MASTER_KEY decrypts the parent's legacy row; a master-written row still fails in the parent). **S1.5** fail closed on shaped-but-undecryptable values at platform/core/src/settings/typed.rs:334, :361, :437, :553, :639 - today they `unwrap_or(v)` and hand the ciphertext back as the credential. **S2a** dormant install-key seam in crypto. **S2b** new keychain entry oz-pos/at-rest-key.v1 (generate-once-if-absent) wired at startup - do NOT reuse oz-pos/encryption-key, whose rotation archives the old key without re-wrapping any row. **S2c** opt-in `oz rekey` CLI that copies the DB first and prints counts, never values. **S3** a pin test that .ozpkg user JSON never carries national_id or monthly pay.
  Done when: a per-install key is held in the OS keychain, a test asserts the static fallback cannot be reached in a release build, and a row written under the legacy derivation still decrypts after the key exists. **Precondition, and it is most of the work: make the reader branch-tolerant first** (see D1) - simply setting the environment variable, or refusing to boot without it, silently bricks five credential families and both PII columns on any existing install, including two families that have no product setter to restore them.

Signature verification and the operator grant store remain - they are the part your D7 answer decides.

THE WARNING IN THE DESIGN WAS RIGHT: the miss was real and lived in a file the fence did not name - apps/cloud-server/src/sync_store.rs holds TWO MORE server INSERT column lists, the per-item fallbacks that run whenever the multirow fast path errors, and touching only pg.rs/sqlite.rs would have silently dropped the field for exactly those batches. The worker found it, included it, and added a discriminating test that poisons an id to force the fallback and was verified to fail when the fix is reverted. Declared open item: the applier still writes a NULL effect_key, so the partial index is inert until the receipt call is switched (S3a: BLOCKED on a fence conflict - the C4 lifetime-spend worker held queue.rs, so S3a stopped with zero edits rather than sweep foreign work. Its researched handoff is in the journal: the receipt writer is queue.rs:868 and the arms must change signature to yield Option<String>, with the effect keys per arm listed there, and two arms that cannot carry a key at all - stock.adjusted (no id in its payload) and stock.movement under the crdt_delta envelope (two effects in one item). Re-dispatch S3a once queue.rs is clean.). **CORRECTED 2026-09-25: "S3-S7 remain" was a plan sketch that never itemised S6 or S7 - a tree-wide search finds no definition of either. S3a, S4, S5a and S5 ALL LANDED (log rows below, commits 359f3b85 / c8486bb2 / a15c5b68 / b4c489c4), and the item's own acceptance criterion is met and independently re-verified this session: the push-pull round trip passes (`a_terminal_does_not_reapply_the_sale_it_pushed`, falsified by neutering the gate), the server-side self-exclusion is tested (`sqlite_pull_excludes_rows_originated_by_the_calling_terminal`, including the NULL-origin legacy case), and platform-sync is 428 passed / 0 failed. Re-measured: `unsupported remote sync action` survives only as the `_` fallback (:1523), no longer reachable from these three.

- [x] **C6 [P0] Make the report timezone contract match the write contract** (8.1, P0-5) - **DONE 2026-09-23, commit 7559a3f6b** (the provisioning-validation half is split out as C6b)

- [x] **C7 [P0] Turn tenant isolation on for real - as a role change, not a schema edit** (7.1, P0-6) - **detection half DONE (e7aec886)**: a pure RlsPosture::from_facts verdict (Enforced / BypassedBySuperuser / BypassedByOwnerRole / PartiallyEnforced / NoProtectedTables) with 9 unit tests, an ERROR-level boot report, and rls_posture on /health plus its published schema. The protected-table set is read from pg_policies rather than pinning the generated 34 in Rust. The role cutover itself remains an owner/ops action. **THE SQL IS NO LONGER CODE-REVIEW-COVERED ONLY - IT IS MEASURED, commit 587aa0c88 (2026-09-23, `test(cloud): verify the RLS posture against a live PostgreSQL`, apps/cloud-server/src/db_tests.rs +345, db.rs UNTOUCHED - the shipped logic was tested, not rewritten).** The live verdict on the dev container is **`bypassed_by_superuser`**: `role=postgres superuser=true forced=0 protected=34`. Independent ground truth was taken BEFORE trusting the Rust code - `docker exec ... psql` on the same query returned `postgres|t|0|34` - and the test then corroborates all three facts against separate catalog reads (`current_user`, `pg_roles.rolsuper`, a `pg_policies` count) rather than against itself. **So the documented blocker is now a measured fact: the profile connects as a superuser and all 34 `tenant_isolation` policies are inert.** Negative controls in both directions: in-process, four distinct verdicts are asserted with `assert_ne!` between them (superuser+all-forced -> BypassedBySuperuser, non-superuser+0-forced -> BypassedByOwnerRole, non-superuser+all-forced -> Enforced, zero protected -> NoProtectedTables); and LIVE, `SET ROLE oz_app` on the test's own session CHANGES the verdict, with the result captured before asserting so a panic cannot return a pooled connection still wearing `oz_app`, and `RESET ROLE` restoring it. `/health` was proven end to end at the HTTP layer through the real `build_router` with a real PG pool, carrying `"db":"postgres"`, `"db_connected":true` and `"rls_posture":"bypassed_by_superuser"`. The reachability control is the strongest form available: against a DEAD port the suite printed **7 skip messages in 321.82s**, against the live port **0 skips in 61.20s**, both 36 passed - so a green run is proven to have RUN rather than skipped. **One finding worth keeping from getting there:** a first run reported `rls_posture: "unknown"`, `db_connected: false`, `db_latency_us: 2003217` because the health handler's own 2s pool-checkout bound fired on a loaded container - the health path was correct and the TEST was starving it. Pre-warming the pool dropped latency to 6511us. So **on a loaded container `/health` can report `unknown` for connection-establishment reasons unrelated to posture** - a property of the 2s fail-fast design, not a defect, and the pre-warm is the knob if that assertion ever flakes (the ORIGINAL claim that follows was false and is kept visible for the record): this environment has no PostgreSQL
  Fence: scripts/rls-cutover.sql:7-10, :33-42, :56 (its comment claiming 19 tables is stale against its own 34-table array); the connection configuration that carries DATABASE_URL.
  Sequence (see D2): create the oz_app role, grant DML on the 30 tables, point DATABASE_URL at it **and set OZ_APPLY_SCHEMA=0 in the same step**, verify, and only then consider FORCE as the follow-up. Keep **both** BYPASSRLS roles (webhook resolution and email discovery/prune) and the /status tolerance. Note the blocker this review first missed: the shipped PostgreSQL profile connects as a **superuser** (docker-compose.pg.yml sets POSTGRES_USER and DATABASE_URL from one variable, :21 and :39), and superusers bypass RLS even with FORCE on - so the cutover alone enforces nothing. Add a boot-time rolsuper / relforcerowsecurity assertion. Never add FORCE to the generated migration while the app connects as the table owner - every query would return zero rows on deploy.
  Done when: a tenant-table query without the tenant GUC returns zero rows and a write is rejected, a query with the GUC returns only that tenant's rows, and the reversal (NO FORCE plus DROP ROLE) is written down and rehearsed. Decide D2 first: if no deployment has run the cutover, this is live.

- [ ] **C8 [P0] Make recovery real: restore in the app, a backup that survives, verification on both sides** (14.1, P0-9) - **ACCEPTANCE CRITERION MET AND FALSIFIED 2026-09-25 (`8c75a5537`).** The item's own "done when" named a `restore_roundtrip` test, and no such test existed: the pieces were pinned in isolation (a valid candidate is accepted, a corrupt one is refused, a failed swap leaves the original byte-identical) but nothing backed up FIRST, corrupted the LIVE file, restored, and asserted business data came back. `recovery_roundtrip_restores_business_data_from_a_backup_of_the_live_db` now does exactly that flow, through `Store::backup` (the call the app's command uses) and `restore_from` (the app path), asserting `check_integrity()` is Ok and a real product row reads back with its values intact - not a marker setting. FALSIFIED: neutralising the `fs::rename` that performs the swap makes it fail at the integrity assertion, then `recovery.rs` was restored byte-identically via git. NOTE the remaining work on this item is NOT recovery correctness: S1-S4b, S5a, S7 and the runbook all landed, and all 11 recovery tests pass. What is left is **D5 - whether in-app restore is a product feature or an ops procedure**, an owner decision. **slice S1 DONE (6814222)**: backups are copy -> verify (integrity_check on the snapshot) -> rotate 3 generations -> atomic rename; a failed copy leaves the previous snapshot byte-identical; remove_destination_for_backup deleted with the directory-target typed error preserved. **S2 DONE (3add5b27)**: validate_candidate returns a typed verdict (Acceptable / OlderButAcceptable / NewerThanThisBuild / Corrupt plus a reason - never a bare bool), restore_from validates, snapshots to <db>.pre-restore.db, deletes the sidecars only AFTER validation, stages and re-verifies before an atomic rename, and rolls back on failure; the CLI now delegates to it so one validator and one swap serve both lanes (9 recovery tests pass, plus the 21 backup_restore_integration tests). A candidate with no schema_migrations table is OlderButAcceptable, which is what keeps a pre-migration snapshot restorable. **S3 DONE (9b551e76)**: list_restore_candidates reports each generation with its verdict (a corrupt one is LISTED as Corrupt, not omitted), restore_prepare gates on SETTINGS_EDIT then validates then requires the store name read from the CANDIDATE itself before writing <db>.restore-request.json, and restore_status reports a pending request (an unparsable one reports pending WITH an error, never 'nothing pending'). Nothing is restored by this slice - the boot consumer is S4. S4-S7 remain.
  Fence: crates/kasirmu-cli/src/commands/backup.rs:62-98 (the only restore); crates/kasirmu-core/src/db/mod.rs:264-272 and :281-305 (destination deleted before it is written) and :319 (check_integrity, no production caller); crates/kasirmu-bridge/src/data.rs:146-150 and :330-348; apps/desktop-tauri/src/commands/data.rs; ui/src/api/data.ts; ui/src/app/UpdateBanner.tsx:143-155 and :179-189.
  Approach (see D5): a **safe-mode boot flag** rather than an in-process restore - the live connection is an Arc shared with twelve detached daemons that cannot be joined, and there is no restart primitive. Done when: a restore_roundtrip test backs up through the same command the UI calls, corrupts the live database, restores from the app path, and asserts check_integrity() is Ok with a known sale reading back; a second case asserts a corrupt backup is refused and the live file is left byte-identical. Backups write to a temporary name and rename over the previous only on success; at least two generations are kept; the app either quiesces or refuses a restore while another process holds the database.

  **Slices from the D5 design** (each independently shippable; ship S1-S2 before S3+): **S1** backup durability in crates/kasirmu-core/src/db/mod.rs - tmp file in the same directory, check_integrity on the tmp, atomic rename, rotate three generations, delete remove_destination_for_backup. **S2** validate_candidate + restore_from (validate, pre-restore snapshot to `<db>.pre-restore.db`, delete -wal/-shm, tmp+rename, re-verify, roll back on failure), with the CLI's run_restore refactored onto it. **S3** bridge: list_restore_candidates / restore_prepare (SETTINGS_EDIT plus a typed store-name confirmation, writes `<db>.restore-request.json`) / restore_status. **S4** boot: a recovery module in both shells called BEFORE AppState::new, so no live connection or detached daemon is involved; a `<db>.restore.lock` made with create_new guards a double boot. **S5** IPC plus a Safe Mode screen with locale strings. **S6** the updater writes `<db>.restore-candidate.json` after its pre-update backup, because updater.last_backup_path is unreadable exactly when it is needed. **S7** runbook and CLI help. Schema gate: an OLDER candidate is acceptable (forward re-apply), a NEWER one is refused.
- [x] **C9 [P0] Decide what qris-core is, and make the manifest say it** - DONE 2026-09-23, commit 57e0f8c84 (14.4, P0-10)
  Fence: crates/qris-core/Cargo.toml:6, :7, :9 (publish = true, MIT OR Apache-2.0, repository YOUR_ORG); deny.toml:83-268 (no entry for it, while MIT and Apache-2.0 are already allowlisted so cargo deny passes); root Cargo.toml:40 and :42.
  Done when: either it is proprietary like everything else (publish = false, license.workspace = true, a deny.toml clarification entry) or it is genuinely dual-licensed (LICENSE-MIT and LICENSE-APACHE committed, a real repository and authors, the deny.toml entry). cargo deny must no longer be blind to the difference. Owner decision D4: recommendation is to make it proprietary - zero dependents and no offline QRIS role today, while the code is worth keeping for the planned notification listener.

The unconditional constraint contradicts a shipped feature.

  Done when: (a) all seven sites use TransactionBehavior::Immediate; (b) the CHECK lands **after** a backfill that clamps or quarantines existing negatives, or it bricks startup; (c) fresh_db() carries busy_timeout 5000 and the loser of the concurrency test fails with a lock error rather than an unexamined one. **Prerequisite: C11.** Do not hunt for a reproduction of an oversell - the defect is the false invariant and the untested error path.

- [x] **C11 [P1, prerequisite] Snapshot before every migration** (14.2) - **code half DONE 2026-09-23, commit 9c6d76e1**; the runbook/pre-flight half is split out as C11b
  Fence: platform/core/src/database/migrations.rs:112-121 (drift is repaired by re-running SQL on live data) and :424-446 (per-statement fallback loses per-file atomicity); docs/operations/ (no pre-upgrade procedure for the desktop file); scripts/backup-db.sh.
  Done when: a test asserts a snapshot exists after migrations::run, a migration that fails midway leaves both the database and the snapshot intact, and the runbook names the four pinned un-re-runnable migrations (migrations_tests.rs:361-366) as the reason.

- [x] **C12 [P1, prerequisite] Variance report for stock already double-deducted** (5.1 remediation) - **DONE 2026-09-23, commit bdaffa47**; wiring it to an operator surface is C12b
  Fence: a new reconciliation over stock_summary against stock_movements per (item, location).
  Done when: the report exists, has a test on a seeded double-deducted fixture, and an operator can accept or adjust each difference. Never write a blanket qty = SUM(deltas) update - it destroys legitimate manual adjustments.

  - **RE-VERIFIED 2026-09-26: both remaining clauses CONFIRMED OPEN, and the item's two citations are STALE.** (a) WRITE SITES: `crates/kasirmu-local-api/src/lib.rs:340 load_or_create_secret` and `:353 rotate_secret` both call the plain `kasirmu_core::Settings::set` (`platform/core/src/settings/raw.rs:37`), and the crate imports no crypto at all (`grep encrypt|decrypt` over `kasirmu-local-api/src` -> 0 hits). (b) DOUBLE-PURPOSE: one value cloned into two capability tiers at `:425` (`admin_key`, the `x-admin-key` operator header checked in `routes/tokens.rs:87,:272` and `require_admin_write` across products/tax_rates/exchange_rates/users/terminals/settings/plans/memos) and `:428` (`api_secret`, the HS256 signing key at `kasirmu-api/src/auth.rs:249`). So possession of either string grants the other capability.

The existing ruling, `docs/security/security-audit-completion.md:29-38`, already says so: "CODE FINDING, recorded and not patched ... deny-listed from the IPC surface (C-2) but not encrypted at rest (H-5). **Whether that is acceptable is a call for whoever owns the local API**". The JSON export drops it (`data.rs:294-299` via `IngestPolicy::PortablePackage`), but the whole-file `.backup.db` copy does not (`data.rs:250-253`, `:1021-1041`).
  - **TWO STALE CITATIONS FIXED IN THIS PASS, because a fenced line that no longer points at the code sends the next reader somewhere wrong.** The item and `security-audit-completion.md:31` both cite `apps/desktop-tauri/src/local_api.rs:197`/`:210` as the write sites; that file is now a 10-line re-export shim (`pub use kasirmu_local_api::*`), and the real writes are `crates/kasirmu-local-api/src/lib.rs:340`/`:353`. The item's backup cite `bridge/data.rs:177-195` is now a doc comment; the snapshot path is `data.rs:250-253` / `:1021-1041`. Both corrected in `docs/security/security-audit-completion.md`.
  - **Owner decision status, stated plainly: (a) is BLOCKED ON D1** -- the same key-management call as C1, exactly as the item says. D1 remains open, and `manager-codebase-review-decisions.md:23-42` carries the options (recommendation D: per-install OS-keychain key with a branch-tolerant reader). Encrypting this secret before D1 is answered would choose a key scheme in code that D1 exists to choose deliberately.
  - **(b) has NO listed owner decision, and it is NOT mechanical -- contradicting any reading of the item as a quick fix.** Separating the two roles needs a distinct admin-key value, a signing key that stays stable so already-minted JWTs keep verifying, and a migration for operators currently holding the secret as `X-Admin-Key` (both `scripts/generate-local-api-key.bat` and the rotate flow `ui/src/features/settings/sections/LocalApiSection.tsx:299`/`:329` assume ONE value). D1-D8 do not cover it. The item's own line 74 says "Both are decisions, not mechanical edits" -- re-verified as correct.
  - **Neither clause is remotely reachable today, which is why the P-level is not higher:** the local API binds 127.0.0.1 only with empty CORS, and zero of the Tauri IPC commands are exposed through it. No partial fix exists for either clause, and none was attempted here.
  - **The defect was real and exactly as described.** The IPC door `kasirmu-local-api::mint_token` clamped to `1..=MAX_TOKEN_HOURS` (8760h) at lib.rs:370-372, while the HTTP door called `create_token_full` **directly** and passed the caller's `expiry_hours` straight through (routes/tokens.rs:236, :324). The same requested value produced a bounded token on one path and a 10-year token on the other; `scripts/generate-local-api-key.bat:15` asked for `87600` and printed "valid for 10 years".
  - **THE FIX IS AT THE FUNNEL, NOT AT EITHER DOOR.** `create_token_full` is the single function all four mint paths reach -- `create_token` and `create_token_scoped` delegate to it (auth.rs:184, :197), the IPC door calls it (local-api/lib.rs:378), and both HTTP routes call it directly. Applying `.min(MAX_TOKEN_HOURS)` there is what makes the two doors structurally unable to disagree, rather than two clamps that must be kept in step by hand. `MAX_TOKEN_HOURS` is now defined once in `kasirmu-api` as the contract value.
  - **ONLY THE CEILING, DELIBERATELY -- and my first attempt got this wrong in a way worth recording.** I first wrote `clamp(1, MAX_TOKEN_HOURS)`, mirroring the IPC door. That **broke `expired_token_is_rejected`**, an existing test that mints with `Some(-1)` on purpose to build an already-expired token. The floor is not the security property -- an over-long token is the risk, a short one is not -- and `create_token_full` is a primitive whose callers legitimately construct edge cases. The floor therefore stays where it already was, on the operator-facing IPC door. A test now pins the **pass-through** so a future hardening pass cannot clamp the floor back in and silently break that premise.
  - **TESTS: 5 new.** A 10-year request clamps to exactly `MAX_TOKEN_HOURS`; a negative expiry is **not** raised here (so expired tokens stay mintable); in-range values (1, 12, 24, 8760) pass through unchanged; the default applies when omitted; and both mint doors yield the same expiry for the same input -- the defect in one assertion. **FALSIFIED:** deleting the `.min()` fails `an_out_of_range_expiry_is_clamped_not_honoured` and `both_mint_doors_clamp_to_the_same_bound`, while the two pass-through tests correctly still pass.
  - **Also corrected so the docs stop lying:** the OpenAPI `expiry_hours` schema said only "default: 24" and now states the 8760 ceiling (a silently-shortened token is otherwise indistinguishable from a bug); the route description says the server enforces the ceiling; and `generate-local-api-key.bat` now requests 8760 and prints "valid for 1 year" instead of promising 10.
  - **Evidence:** `cargo test -p kasirmu-api` -> **342 passed, 0 failed** (+1 doctest); `cargo test -p kasirmu-local-api` -> **19 passed**; `cargo fmt -p kasirmu-api -- --check` exit 0; `cargo clippy -p kasirmu-api -p kasirmu-local-api --all-targets` silent.
  - **STILL OPEN in this item (the two clauses that give it its name):** (a) **the secret is stored in plaintext** through `Settings::set` with no crypto import in `kasirmu-local-api` (lib.rs:202-227, :48-60) -- already recorded in `docs/security/security-audit-completion.md:30`, and it rides every unfiltered .db/.backup.db snapshot (bridge/data.rs:177-195). This is the same class as **C1's at-rest key, which is owner-blocked on D1** -- encrypting it needs the same key-management decision, so it should be done with C1 rather than before it. (b) **one value doubles as the JWT signing key and the operator `X-Admin-Key`**; separating them is a wire-contract change (an operator holding the admin key can currently mint tokens, and vice versa) and needs a migration path for already-issued admin keys. Both are decisions, not mechanical edits. **The item's own reported reach:** the local API binds 127.0.0.1 only, auth is JWT plus constant-time admin-key check, CORS is empty, and **zero of the 453 Tauri IPC commands** are exposed -- so neither open clause is remotely reachable. That is context for sequencing, not a reason to skip them. - [x] **C15 [P1] DONE 2026-09-26. The decision was ALREADY MADE by the tree's own record, not by me: `sale.total` now includes tip + service, and the validator compares like with like.**
  - **This looked like a product decision (its own brief says "decide whether tip and service belong in sales.total_minor") and it is not one -- it is an UNFINISHED fix with the intent written down twice.** Migration `20260822_sale_charges.sql:1-4` states the defect verbatim: tip and service "were collected by the frontend in the payment total but never persisted -- **the recorded sale.total understated collected revenue**". Commit `d8bdf07ea` repeats it. The fix that landed added two COLUMNS and never added the term to `total`, so the understatement both records name is still live. Where the tree has already decided and only the code lags, deciding again would be re-litigating.
  - **The decisive precedent is three lines above the fix, in the same function.** `compute_sale_tax_for_location` already adds EXCLUSIVE TAX to `sale.total` for exactly this reason (`sales_tax.rs:304-318`, TAX-06): the comment says it makes `sales.total_minor` "the true collectible amount, matching the receipt's grand total contract", because the customer pays subtotal + tax. Tip and service are the same shape and were simply missing from that same adjustment. The change is that adjustment extended, not a new rule.
  - **Both directions of the divergence verified in code before touching anything.** Loyalty (`sales_lifecycle.rs:51` `earn_total = base_total_minor.unwrap_or(total_minor)`), revenue (`daily_summary.rs:81,127` `SUM(s.total_minor)`) and the drawer (`shifts.rs` `expected_cash`) all read the tip-EXCLUSIVE total, while the same shift's payment breakdown (`shifts.rs`, `get_shift_report`) reads `SUM(payments.amount_minor)` and the UI drives the splits from a tip-INCLUSIVE total (`usePosState.ts:234-244`). One sale, two numbers, diverging by exactly tip + service.
  - **The blast radius was measured before the change, not after: zero.** A tree-wide search for a fixture with a non-zero `tip_minor`/`service_charge_minor` returns **nothing** -- no test anywhere set one, which is both why the defect survived and why the change could not break an existing assertion. `grep -rn 'tip_minor: [1-9]'` across the repo: 0 hits.
  - **RED before GREEN, then falsified.** `compute_tax_adds_tip_and_service_to_the_sale_total` failed first at 700 vs 850 and `compute_tax_adds_tip_and_service_on_top_of_exclusive_tax` at 770 vs 920. **Falsification:** disabling the new block with an `if false` probe turns all three charged-total tests red (850->700, 920->770, and the end-to-end test's payable total 850->700); removing the probe returns them green. The end-to-end test was run against the probe too, so it is proven to discriminate rather than merely to pass.
  - **The like-with-like half is proved END TO END, not asserted.** `complete_sale_deduction_compares_charged_total_like_with_like` runs the real checkout door: an 850 payable sale (700 cart + 100 tip + 50 service) is now correctly REFUSED a 700 tender -- which previously covered the tip-exclusive total and would have been accepted -- and completes on 850, with the stored `sales.total_minor` reading 850. Both validator call sites (`sales_checkout.rs:431`, `sales_lifecycle.rs:416`) pass `sale.total.minor_units`, which now carries the charges, so the two sides of the comparison are the same base.
  - **A contract that the fix depends on is now pinned rather than assumed.** The charges are read from `tip_minor`/`service_charge_minor`, never from the running `total`, so a SECOND call re-adds them. That is safe because every caller passes a sale freshly built by `Sale::from_cart`, and `compute_tax_reads_charges_from_the_sale_not_from_its_own_output` measures the doubled result (850 -> 1000) so a future caller that feeds back an already-adjusted sale goes red. Finding this required writing the test on the assumption and letting the measurement correct it.
  - **Evidence:** `cargo test -p kasirmu-core --lib` -> **3368 passed, 0 failed** (3364 baseline + 4 new); `cargo test -p kasirmu-bridge --lib` -> **1406 passed, 0 failed**; `cargo test -p kasirmu-reporting --lib` -> **78 passed, 0 failed**; `cargo check -p kasirmu-mobile --lib` clean (the tablet sets tip/service then calls the same tax door, so it inherits the fix); `cargo fmt -p kasirmu-core -- --check` reports no diff in either touched file; clippy reports no finding in either.
  - **What is NOT covered, stated so the tick is not read as more than it is:** (a) tip and service are added AFTER tax and are absent from `subtotal` and `tax_total` -- if a jurisdiction ever taxes a service charge, that needs its own decision, and the code comment says so rather than guessing. (b) `refunds` still has no tender column, so a refund's cash-ness is still inferred from the original sale's tender (the C16 finding). (c) `base_total_minor` -- the loyalty earn basis -- remains client-supplied with no server re-derivation; the review lists that in 9.2 and it was not addressed here.

- [x] **C16 [P1] DONE 2026-09-26. Both halves fixed and falsified: split-tender cash is now in the drawer, and a refund belongs to the shift that PROCESSED it. The house-account half of the fence was already correct.**
  - **Both premises verified in code before touching anything, and one was wrong in a way that mattered.** (a) `close_shift` classified tenders from `sales.payment_method` (`:162-164`), and `bridge/pos.rs:1009` stamps a split sale `split` via `SPLIT_MARKER` -- matching neither `cash` nor `card`, so it fell into `total_other` and the cash leg vanished from `expected_cash`. Confirmed, and the shift REPORT already summed `payments.amount_minor` (`:383-389`), so close and report did disagree by construction. (b) The refund claim is HALF right: `refunds.processed_by` exists (`20260813_init.sql:553`) and was ignored, but the second clause -- "refunds are typed by the original sale's tender rather than the refund's own" -- describes a column that **does not exist**. `refunds` has no tender field and nothing anywhere records how a refund was paid out, so there is no "refund's own tender" to read. Stated in the code rather than papered over.
  - **A wrong fix was written, caught by its own test, and replaced -- recorded because it is the trap this item sets.** The first attempt classified a split sale by `EXISTS (payments ... method='cash')`, which makes the sale COUNT as cash while still summing the whole `total_minor` -- my own test went from `left: 0` to `left: 2000, right: 1000`. The bucket must hold TENDER AMOUNTS, not sale totals, so the query was rewritten over a `LEFT JOIN payments` view where a split sale contributes one row per payment with `p.amount_minor`, and a non-split sale contributes `s.total_minor` under its stamp. **No intermediate result was committed.**
  - **Why the tender buckets are a narrow fallback and not a wholesale switch to `payments`.** Both checkout doors write `payments` ONLY when `payment_splits` is non-empty (`sales_checkout.rs:606`, `sales_lifecycle.rs:546`) -- a plain one-tender sale keeps its method on the sale alone. So the view derives each sale's tender from its OWN payment rows when it has any and falls back to `payment_method` otherwise. A blanket join would have driven every simple cash sale's contribution to zero, which is a worse bug than the one being fixed.
  - **RED before GREEN, and both halves falsified independently against the committed fix.** `close_shift_sees_the_cash_leg_of_a_split_tender` failed first with `left: 0, right: 1000`; `close_shift_attributes_a_refund_to_the_shift_that_processed_it` failed first with `left: Some(4000), right: Some(5000)` (user-2's refund leaking into user-1's drawer). **Falsification 1:** restoring the stamp-only query fails the split test at 0 vs 1000. **Falsification 2:** restoring `s.user_id` in both refund queries fails the attribution test at 1000 vs 0. Each probe was run alone and reverted; both fixes restored and green afterwards.
  - **A third test pins the INVARIANT the item is really about, not just the two symptoms.** `close_shift_and_report_agree_on_cash_for_a_split_tender` reads `get_shift_report`'s own `payment_breakdown` cash total and asserts `closed.total_cash_minor == report_cash`. The review's charge was that the two disagree by construction; that assertion is now the thing under test, so a future change to either end cannot silently re-open the gap.
  - **Evidence:** `cargo test -p kasirmu-core --lib` -> **3364 passed, 0 failed** (3361 baseline + these 3); `cargo test -p kasirmu-core --lib shifts` -> 42 passed; `cargo test -p kasirmu-bridge --lib shift` -> 15 passed; `cargo fmt -p kasirmu-core -- --check` clean. `close_shift` is SQLite-only (`grep close_shift` in `kasirmu-api` finds nothing), so there is no PG mirror and no schema drift -- no migration touched.
  - **What is NOT covered, stated so the tick is not read as more than it is:** the item also asks for a `close_shift` test for HOUSE-ACCOUNT credit. That path is already correct and the review says so (`expected_cash` has never counted a non-cash tender), and no `house_account` tender string exists in the tree to build a fixture from -- so the requested test was not written, and the absence is named here rather than silently skipped. Cash-refund DETECTION still keys off the original sale's tender for the reason in the second bullet.

Add the missing close_shift tests for split tender and house-account credit.

All three remaining sub-items were verified against the code: TWO of them are already satisfied (the review's recommendation in one is wrong, its line range in the other is stale) and the third is enforced mechanically by a drift pin. No code change was needed, and that is the finding.**
  - **Item 1, "delete the renderer-supplied user_id variant of settings::set_setting in favour of the scoped twin" -- THE RECOMMENDATION IS WRONG. The unscoped variant is LIVE and the scoped twin is NOT a substitute.** Verified independently and then re-verified by me. The unscoped `set_setting(key, value, user_id, state)` exists at `apps/desktop-tauri/src/commands/settings.rs:237-253` (line reference correct) and is: registered (`lib.rs:1175`), called from live UI (`UpdateBanner.tsx:172` via `ui/src/api/settings.ts:311-312`), and mounted unconditionally (`AppLayout.tsx:385`). **It is not a drop-in swap because the two write DIFFERENT DATABASES:** the unscoped path locks `ctx.db` -- the GLOBAL identity DB -- and checks `require_permission_for_user` (`kasirmu-bridge/src/settings.rs:1260-1278`), while the scoped twin resolves the session and writes the STORE db (`:1308-1342`). The UI's own API doc says so in words: "The two are NOT interchangeable for device-global keys: repointing a call changes which database is read, so what one writes the other never sees" (`ui/src/api/settings.ts:297-309`). The call site is explicitly pre-login -- the banner "renders before login in some routes" (`UpdateBanner.tsx:164-169`) -- and `setSettingScoped` hard-rejects a null token (`settings.ts:326-328`), so deleting the unscoped door would break updater persistence on the pre-login path.
  - **The unscoped door is also a DECLARED gate exception, not an oversight.** Both `registration_gate_debt.generated.rs` files carry `("settings::set_setting", "no_session_resolution")` (desktop `:87`, tablet `:92`) -- so the registration-gate ledger already records this as measured, classified debt with a mechanical reason. The item's framing ("delete it") would delete a door the gate has already accounted for.
  - **Item 4, "gate create_backup or document it as a deliberate unauthenticated filesystem write" -- ALREADY DOCUMENTED, so the second half of the item is satisfied. Its LINE RANGE is stale.** `create_backup` is at `crates/kasirmu-bridge/src/data.rs:433-470`, not the review's `:330-347` (that range is now `gate_import_product_batch`/`gate_import_user_batch`). The function takes NO token, emits `tracing::warn!(event = "backup_ungated_no_session", skipped_permission = DATA_EXPORT, ...)`, and delegates to `create_backup_direct`. Its doc comment (`:433-440`) says it in full: "Create backup -- UNGATED entry point ... checks no permission whatsoever: no token is presented, so there is no identity to authorize. Reachable today from the Data screen with no workspace session ... One event per ungated call." It is additionally recorded in `registration_gate_debt.generated.rs:22`, `docs/records/audit-open-findings.md:214-219` (BR-S1 [PBD]) and the `verify-scoped-coverage.sh` allowlist.
  - **Bounded risk, measured rather than assumed -- and it is narrower than "unauthenticated filesystem write" suggests.** The destination is NOT caller-controlled: `default_backup_path` (`data.rs:250-254`) swaps the extension on `state.db_path` from `AppState`, not an IPC argument, so a renderer can neither choose the path nor the filename. The write is confined to `<db>.backup.db` plus two rotation siblings (`BACKUP_GENERATIONS = 3`). It returns the absolute path but cannot read rows back. So the exposure is: an unauthenticated backup + rotation of up to two prior generations, reachable by any renderer JS including XSS -- not arbitrary-path write and not data exfiltration.
  - **Item "lower the two generated ceilings, never raise them" -- MECHANICALLY ENFORCED ALREADY, which is why nothing was changed.** `apps/mobile-tauri/src/commands/registration_gate_debt.generated.rs:367` `DEBT_CEILING: usize = 92` is pinned by `drift_pin_debt_ceilings_only_shrink` in `registration_gate_tests.rs`, and the generator comment records that "the floor, this total and `DEBT_CEILING` all move together; the generator writes only this number". I did not lower them **because there is nothing measured to lower them to** -- a ceiling must track a real reduction in debt, and this pass retired no debt row. Lowering it without retiring a row would red the pin, which is the pin working.
  - **Evidence:** `cargo test -p kasirmu-mobile --lib registration_gate` -> **12 passed, 0 failed** (including `drift_pin_debt_ceilings_only_shrink` and the floor pin); `python scripts/verify-ipc-parity.py` -> gate OK; the two claim verifications were performed by delegate agents and each key claim was then re-read by me in the file it cites (`settings.rs:237-253`, `UpdateBanner.tsx:164-176`, `api/settings.ts:297-330`, `data.rs:433-470`).
  - **What this tick does NOT claim:** it does not say C17's debt is paid -- 92 tablet rows and 10 desktop entries remain ungated, and the item's own premise that the debt should be worked down still stands. It says the three NAMED remaining sub-items are done: two were already satisfied and the third is enforced. A future pass that retires real debt rows should lower the ceiling in the same commit, as the generator demands.

- [ ] **C18 Wrap the money-path autocommit writes** (4.4) - **ALL MEASURED REMAINING WORK IS NOW DONE: two fixable functions, both fixed and both falsified (commits 57d913a59, cdc14968a). What remains is one FEATURE DECISION.** - ~~138 conn.execute sites in db/ outside tests~~ **CORRECTED BY MEASUREMENT 2026-09-23: 131 sites across 40 non-test files, of which 99 sit in functions with exactly ONE execute - already correct, because on a single shared `Store` connection a bare statement joins an open transaction implicitly, so a wrap there is a no-op and a test for it cannot fail. The real work is ~12 multi-statement functions. See the P3 note below and C61.** Original: make update_sale_status a conditional in-transaction update like void_sale already is (sales_crud.rs:558-600 versus sales_lifecycle.rs:787-798). **Slice status:** P1.2 DONE (b0e78e69) - create_cash_payout is now a compare-and-set inside the write, with a deterministic race test that was proved to fail against the unfixed SQL before shipping; P1.1 (update_sale_status) and P1.5 (fiscal numbering) in flight.
  - **THE SECOND AND LAST FIXABLE FUNCTION WAS `update_kds_status`, AND IT WAS A LOST UPDATE, NOT A MISSING WRAPPER (commit `cdc14968a`).** It is a CHECK-THEN-WRITE: the function reads the current status, validates the transition against it, then issues `UPDATE kds_orders SET ... WHERE id = ?` -- **unconditional on the status it just validated**. A competing transition landing in that gap was overwritten and the call reported success: a lost update on the column that drives the kitchen board and the prep timer. Fixed as a compare-and-set inside `TransactionBehavior::Immediate` (`WHERE ... AND status = ?4`), with `rows == 0` -> `CoreError::Conflict`, mirroring the already-fixed `update_po_status` (`purchase_orders.rs:354-365`) that the code's own comment claimed to copy.
  - **RED/GREEN with a deterministically-interleaved two-connection race, and the falsifier is the interesting part.** `update_kds_status_race_cannot_overwrite_a_competing_transition` uses the busy-handler technique (not a timer, which is load-dependent and can fail a correct implementation). Pre-fix it fails on `left: "cancelled", right: "ready"` -- B committed `ready` and A's transition overwrote it. **Falsified:** replacing the predicate with `AND ?4 = ?4` reproduces the identical failure; `git checkout --` restores green.
  - **Two setup mistakes of mine, recorded because they are the failure modes of this test shape.** (a) My first draft wrote the thread body with a bare `}` instead of `})`, so the file would not parse -- a range/brace error, caught by the compiler rather than by review. (b) My first version had the rival target a status that made the interleaving unreachable; the fix was choosing a transition legal from BOTH directions. A race test whose setup is invalid fails by TIMEOUT, not by assertion, which is worth knowing before debugging one.
  - **THREE AGENTS WORKED THE SAME LINE AT ONCE, AND THE COORDINATION IS WORTH RECORDING BECAUSE NO ONE ERRED.** My test was written, applied, fmt'd, then **reverted out of the working tree by another lane's `git checkout --`** before I committed it -- so for a period the fix existed in `kds_orders.rs` with no test at all. The other lane (`.agents/reviews/crate-review-ledger-24-09-26.md`, pass 101 / MSL-80) had independently written its own race test for the same CAS, correctly detected that my `kds_orders.rs` edit was in flight (40 changed lines at a file mtime 5 minutes before its own), correctly refused to sweep it under its message, and withdrew its test instead. **Its analysis independently confirms both the defect and the fix** ("deleting its `AND status = ?4` predicate would leave the suite green while a rival transition was silently overwritten"). The lesson is not "be more careful" -- it is that an UNCOMMITTED fix is invisible to a peer's `git checkout` and can be erased by it; **commit before falsifying**.
  - **Evidence:** `cargo test -p kasirmu-core --lib` -> **3396 passed, 0 failed**; `cargo test -p kasirmu-core --lib update_kds_status` -> 9 passed; `cargo fmt -p kasirmu-core -- --check` clean; clippy reports no finding in either touched file; gates dead-refs 0, architecture 0, ipc 0 after the change.
  - **What remains in C18, stated so this is not read as "C18 done":** the census found no other multi-statement unwrapped function, and the 99 single-execute sites are deliberately left alone. The only outstanding item is `Decision::AutoMerge` under C19(a), which is a FEATURE DECISION (implement a cross-terminal delta merge, or delete the computation -- the checklist forbids the latter), not an unwrapped write.
  - **The census reproduced the 2026-09-23 correction almost exactly, so the premise that produced it was sound.** Current measurement: **129 `conn.execute` sites across 39 non-test files** (recorded 131/40; the difference is the already-landed P1 wraps), of which **99 sit in functions with exactly ONE execute** -- the population that must NOT be wrapped, because a bare statement joins an open transaction on the shared connection and a wrap there is a no-op. **11 functions carry two or more**, against the recorded 12.
  - **10 of those 11 are correctly left alone, and the classification is the important part.** Three are already wrapped (`kds_ops.rs:85`, `kds_lines.rs:336`, `profile.rs:992`). Five are `&Connection` helpers whose EVERY caller supplies a transaction -- `assignments.rs:457`, `loyalty.rs:747`, `loyalty.rs:908` (which carries its own explicit "NOT wrapped, and deliberately so" verdict), `staff.rs:506` and `staff.rs:615` (both `_in_tx` by contract). One is self-atomic via SAVEPOINT (`downgrade.rs:64`). One has mutually exclusive branches with exactly one write per call (`image_refs.rs:222`). So the recorded work list is genuinely exhausted except for the one below.
  - **THE ONE REMAINING FUNCTION WAS WORSE THAN "MULTI-STATEMENT": `staff.rs record_login_attempt_scoped` ran a prune, THREE limit checks, an INSERT and a re-check as separate autocommit statements.** A failure after the prune committed the DELETION while recording no attempt at all -- the stored history destroyed AND the attempt invisible to every limit, which is the precise opposite of what a rate limiter may do under failure. The four callers (bridge `auth.rs:366`/`:802`, desktop and mobile `commands/auth.rs`) all call it unwrapped.
  - **RED first, with a deterministically-failing falsifier:** `record_login_attempt_is_all_or_nothing_when_the_insert_fails` seeds a stale row, forces the INSERT to fail with a trigger, and asserts the prune did not survive. Pre-fix: **`left: 0, right: 1`** -- the stale row was gone and the attempt was never recorded. **Falsified:** reverting the transaction with a probe reproduces the identical failure; restoring it returns green.
  - **The fix mirrors the file's own established idiom (`is_autocommit()` owns-or-joins), and the commit points are deliberate:** the prune COMMITS on the three limit-breach paths (pruning stale rows is a legitimate effect independent of refusing the attempt, and no attempt is recorded there), and commits together with the INSERT on the success path. Only a genuine error rolls it back, which is the case the test pins.
  - **A NEW CANDIDATE FOUND WHILE MEASURING, not fixed and not claimed:** `kds_orders.rs:387 update_kds_status` reads status at `:398` and writes at `:465` with no transaction -- an unwrapped CHECK-THEN-WRITE of the same shape as the already-fixed P1.7 `update_po_status`. It is branch-exclusive (one write per call) so it is not a multi-statement wrap, but the read-decide-write is not atomic. Recorded so the next pass starts from it rather than re-deriving it.
  - **Evidence:** `cargo test -p kasirmu-core --lib` -> **3385 passed, 0 failed** (3384 + the new test); `cargo test -p kasirmu-core --lib staff` -> 93 passed; `cargo fmt -p kasirmu-core -- --check` clean in the touched file.
  **Ordered P1 list** (money and stock first, from the inventory in the journal): P1.1 sales_crud.rs:591 update_sale_status - dispatched (compare-and-set; its Conflict branch is unreachable today); P1.2 cash_payouts.rs:51 - dispatched (check-then-act race against the shift); P1.3 shifts.rs:80 open_shift (**DONE 4518a2b8**, proven RED against the unfixed SQL - the second open won and persisted a duplicate); P1.4 gift_cards.rs:635/:668 freeze/unfreeze; P1.5 fiscal.rs:213 statutory numbering (**DONE 2243cf096 - but reclassified: the CLAIM path at fiscal.rs:399 was already atomic inside the new IMMEDIATE transactions, so this was a house-rule fix on the bare autocommit upsert, not a race, and no test can fail against the pre-fix code; the worker proved that by running its race test against the unfixed code and watching it pass**); P1.6 payables.rs:63 (**DONE 3abd6e67**); P1.7 purchase_orders.rs:316 (**DONE 291b9065**); P1.8 products_crud.rs:555/:576 (**DONE 4fea3894**, honest partial - no discriminating test exists); P1.9 products_stock_query.rs:107 (wrap at its sync caller platform/sync/src/queue.rs:666, whose sibling arm already does); P1.10 stock_counts.rs:61-332; P1.11 stock_transfers.rs:327/:370 (its delete-then-compensate must be one transaction); P1.12 tables.rs:300/:320; P1.13 products.rs:248/:276.
