  **P2:** **audit.rs:369 log_audit DONE 2026-09-23, commit d76527e429** (`fix(core): make log_audit join an open transaction and own one otherwise`; 3 files, +160/-26) - the decision is (b) with a deliberately (c) in-transaction arm: outside a transaction `log_audit` opens and COMMITS its own, inside a caller's it JOINS and the row is LOST if the caller rolls back. That semantic existed by accident before and was documented nowhere; it is now stated in the method's docs and pinned by four tests. `cargo test -p kasirmu-core audit` -> **115 passed, 0 failed**. **The negative control found more than predicted**: forcing the autocommit arm to always open `unchecked_transaction()` fails not only the new test but a REAL production caller - `db::sales::tests::void_sale_changes_status_and_logs_audit` (sales_tests.rs:697) dies with `cannot start a transaction within a transaction`. So the branch is load-bearing, not defensive, and the naive wrap is the fix that would have shipped without this slice. Honest note carried from the author: **no test discriminates pre-fix from post-fix** (a bare `execute` was already atomic in autocommit and already joined an open transaction), so the discriminating evidence is against the naive wrap, and the commit body says so rather than claiming a HEAD failure that does not exist. Remain in P2: loyalty.rs:126/:651; promotions.rs:242 (latent, no production caller).
  **P3 - RE-SCOPED BY MEASUREMENT, 2026-09-23: this was NOT 89 correct sites of defect, and ~87% of it is a house-rule restatement rather than correctness work.** The original wording here was 'P3: 89 sites - batch one transaction per public fn at the boundary, personal-data subset first (customers.rs:196/:250, staff.rs:664/:698/:723)', and a read-only premise check has now shown what that population actually is. **THE REVIEW HAD ALREADY HEDGED, IN THE SAME SENTENCE IT COUNTED:** its heading is 'Writes that are not in transactions, and balances with more than one writer' and its body says *'There are 138 autocommit-capable conn.execute sites in db/ outside tests ... **Some ride an outer transaction, some do not.**'* - so it flagged the population BY A GREP COUNT, not by behavioural analysis, and the two concrete defects it actually named were `update_sale_status`'s unreachable Conflict branch (class b, done as P1.1) and the multi-writer loyalty balances (class b). The checklist then restated the count as a work list, which is where a house rule became a correctness mandate. **THE STRUCTURAL FACT THAT DECIDES IT: on a single shared `Store` connection, a bare statement issued while the caller's transaction is open ALREADY JOINS IT IMPLICITLY** - which is why the C18 P1.9-P1.13 worker found only one of nine rollback tests discriminating, and why the landed fixes' own doc comments justify themselves as CALLER-COMPOSABILITY (*'correct in isolation ... but it left the caller unable to make the ledger row commit or roll back with its own work'*) rather than as data-integrity repairs. **MEASURED CLASSIFICATION:** grouping every `conn.execute(` in `db/` (excluding `_tests.rs`) by enclosing function gives **131 sites across 40 files - 99 in functions with exactly ONE execute, and 12 functions holding 2 or more**; of those 12, ELEVEN hold no transaction (only `staff.rs@448` does). Class (a) single-statement = already correct, and a wrap plus a test for it manufactures coverage that cannot fail. Class (b) multi-statement = the REAL work. Class (c) hand-rolled `BEGIN` = the only class that falsifies cleanly, and it had exactly one live site (`stock_counts.rs:106`), already fixed by P1.10.
  **THE HONEST REMAINING WORK IS ~12 FUNCTIONS, NOT 89 SITES: `assignments.rs@436`, `kds_ops.rs@77`, `kds_lines.rs@315`, `image_refs.rs@222`, `downgrade.rs@64`, `loyalty.rs@696`, `loyalty.rs@892`, `profile.rs@988` (the `:995` UPDATE + `:999` INSERT pair), `staff.rs@608` (the `:632` UPDATE + `:678` upsert pair), `staff.rs@872`, `terminal_overrides.rs@63`, plus `soft_delete_user` (UPDATE then `persist_over_quota_markers`).** Three previously parked items are CLOSED BY THIS MEASUREMENT and should not be dispatched: `loyalty.rs:126` (single `INSERT OR IGNORE`, atomic by UNIQUE - its own comment says so), `loyalty.rs:651` (single UPDATE) and `promotions.rs:242` (single INSERT) are all **class (a), documentation at most** - the review's real loyalty complaint was never these but `loyalty_accounts.points` having three writers and `customers.loyalty_points` being a projection written from five sites, which IS class (b) and is in the list above. And `sales.rs:437` is **not a write at all** - it is a function parameter (`bill_type: &str`), so the 'check for dead code' note was warranted and the answer is that there is nothing there.
  **THE STYLE-RULE HALF NEEDS A DECISION, NOT A SWEEP:** if 'every public write fn owns or joins a transaction' is a house rule worth enforcing, enforce it with a MECHANICAL CHECK over the whole corpus rather than 89 hand-wrapped sites - and do NOT write tests for class (a), because they cannot fail. **Cheap discriminators, expected outputs verified this round:** (1) class (b) real work: `grep -rn --include='*.rs' 'conn\.execute(' crates/kasirmu-core/src/db | grep -v _tests.rs` grouped by enclosing `fn` -> 131 sites, 99 single-execute, 12 functions with 2+; (2) class (c): `grep -rn --include='*.rs' -E 'execute_batch\("BEGIN|BEGIN IMMEDIATE' crates/kasirmu-core/src/db` -> 1 live site (`stock_counts.rs:114`, now guarded by `is_autocommit()` at :112), every other hit a doc comment; (3) class (b) by shape: `grep -rn --include='*.rs' -B3 'conn\.execute(' crates/kasirmu-core/src/db | grep -E 'query_row|\.prepare\('` -> the guard-read-then-write pairs.
  **UNVERIFIED and stated:** the exact derivation of the '89' (that number's base could not be reproduced - the measured base is 131 sites / 138 per the review, the gap being the P1 fixes already landed), and whether the 12-function class-(b) list is exhaustive, since the grouping used an enclosing-`fn` heuristic rather than reading all 131 sites.
- [ ] **C19 Enforce conflicts, or stop pretending to** (5.4, 5.5) - **FIVE OF SIX SUB-ITEMS NOW CLOSED 2026-09-26, AND THE LAST ONE IS NOW CLEARLY A FEATURE DECISION RATHER THAN AN OVERSIGHT**: the `sale` action was missing from the money-entity classifier, so a completed sale auto-merged by last-writer-wins. FIXED AND FALSIFIED.**
  - **THE LOSING PAYLOAD CLOBBER IS FIXED (commit 87fff8eff), AND IT WAS THE WORST OF THE REMAINING THREE.** `classify` returns `Decision::LastWriterWins { winner_is_remote }` (`conflict_resolution.rs:246-249`) -- the decision NAMES the winner -- and `detect_conflict` IGNORED that field, always persisting `incoming_payload` (`conflicts.rs:293-298`). So when the stored side won the tie-break its body was overwritten anyway, while the call reported a successful resolution: the silent one-side loss the whole policy table exists to prevent, and the exact failure the readme-adjacent comment promises not to do.
  - **RED first, and the failure message IS the proof:** `sqlite_last_writer_wins_keeps_the_winner_and_never_clobbers_it` (a `catalog.updated` pair, `{t1:2}` stored vs `{t2:1}` incoming, so the stored side wins) failed with the stored body reading `{"_terminal":"t2",...,"name":"loser"}` -- the winner's `name` replaced by the loser's. **Falsified:** restoring the unconditional `incoming_payload` write reproduces the identical failure; the fix returns green.
  - **The `AutoMerge` arm is now NAMED rather than left to a `_` fallback, which is what makes C19(a) actionable.** Every arm of the payload table is explicit, and the comment states that `AutoMerge` is the one arm whose merge is not implemented anywhere while `last_payload` has no reader outside this module -- so closing C19(a) lands in that arm rather than in a default.
  - **C19(a) is therefore re-classified by this pass, and the re-classification is the finding: it is a FEATURE DECISION, not a defect.** `Decision::AutoMerge` is still produced (`conflict_resolution.rs:230`, `:241`) and consumed by nobody. Consuming it means IMPLEMENTING an additive-delta merge for `stock.*`/`inventory` entities -- new behaviour with its own correctness surface -- while the checklist's own "do not do these" list FORBIDS deleting the computation. Between "build a merge" and "delete the computation" there is no third mechanical option, so this needs an owner ruling on whether cross-terminal stock merging is a product feature. Recorded as such rather than left looking like unfinished work.
  - **Evidence:** `cargo test -p kasirmu-cloud --bin kasirmu-cloud` -> **393 passed, 0 failed, 4 ignored**; `cargo test -p kasirmu-cloud --bin kasirmu-cloud conflict` -> 29 passed; `cargo fmt -p kasirmu-cloud -- --check` clean; clippy reports no finding in either touched file.
  - **THE `'delivering'` STATE THE CHECK ALLOWED IS NOW WRITTEN, AND THE GAP WAS LARGER THAN A MISSING STATUS STRING.** `20260902_outbox.sql:23` permitted `pending | delivering | delivered | failed | dead_letter` and the module doc promised `pending -> delivering -> delivered` (`outbox.rs:21-25`), but NO code path ever wrote `delivering` -- the SQLite drainer SELECTed `pending`, RELEASED THE CONNECTION, delivered, then settled. In that window a second drainer selected and delivered the SAME row. `drain_pg` was always safe here (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` in a held transaction, `:244-252`); the SQLite backend had no claim at all, so the two backends did not have the same guarantee despite sharing a doc comment.
  - **The fix IS the missing state, which is why the item named it: SELECT + compare-and-set to `delivering` now happen in ONE transaction, and only the drainer whose UPDATE actually flipped a row proceeds to deliver it.** A concurrent drainer's `WHERE status = 'pending'` cannot see a claimed row, and `rows_affected == 0` means "not mine" rather than a silent double-send. The settle path is unchanged because it targets by `id`.
  - **RED/GREEN with a deterministically-blocking handler, not a race:** `a_row_is_delivering_while_its_delivery_is_in_flight` blocks inside `deliver_fn` and asserts the row reads `delivering` -- not `pending` -- while the delivery is in flight, then releases it and asserts it settles to `delivered`. **Falsified:** changing the claim's target status back to `pending` fails it with `left: "pending", right: "delivering"`; restoring returns green. A test that merely asserted "the row ends `delivered`" would have passed on the broken code, so the assertion is deliberately on the in-flight state.
  - **Evidence:** `cargo test -p kasirmu-cloud --bin kasirmu-cloud outbox` -> **14 passed, 0 failed** (13 + the new test); `cargo check -p kasirmu-cloud --bin kasirmu-cloud` clean; `cargo fmt -p kasirmu-cloud -- --check` clean.
  - **Still open in C19, unchanged and re-stated so the tick is not over-read:** (a) `Decision::LastWriterWins` / `Decision::AutoMerge` are still COMPUTED and consumed by nobody; (b) `sync_store/conflicts.rs:293-298` still persists the incoming (losing) payload on a `LastWriterWins` decision with no conflict row. Both need a consumer decision, not a mechanical edit, and the checklist's own "do not do these" forbids deleting the computation.
  - **The defect, verified in code before the fix.** `is_money_entity` (`apps/cloud-server/src/conflict_resolution.rs:134-147`) matched `["gift_card","giftcard","payment","refund","loyalty","payout","cash"]` -- and the offline queue names the SALE ACTION, not the domain: `complete_sale` (`crates/kasirmu-core/src/db/offline.rs:388`) and `void_sale` (`:486`). `sync_store.rs:184` passes `item.action` straight in as the classifier's `entity_type`, so `severity_for("complete_sale")` returned `Severity::Low` and `policy_for` returned `MergePolicy::LastWriterWins`. **A completed sale therefore auto-resolved by last-writer-wins and was never flagged** -- the exact value-creation the function's own doc comment says must never be automatic: "Money is the one class where an automatic merge can create value that did not exist before".
  - **RED first, with the falsifier the analysis named:** `a_completed_sale_is_a_money_entity_and_is_never_auto_merged` failed `left: LastWriterWins, right: NeverAutoMerge`. **Falsified:** removing the new `"sale"` needle returns the identical failure; restoring it returns green, so the test discriminates on exactly the change it covers.
  - **The fix is a RULE, not a special case.** The needle added is `"sale"`, not the two literals `"complete_sale"`/`"void_sale"`, so a future action named `sale.*` is covered by construction. The test was also extended to assert both real queue actions, so the coverage is measured rather than asserted.
  - **I refused to assert an action I had not verified.** The test's first draft looped over `[complete_sale, void_sale, create_refund]`; a grep of `offline.rs` showed `create_refund` appears ONLY in comments there, so it was removed from the list and the reason written into the test rather than left as a plausible-looking third case.
  - **Evidence:** `cargo test -p kasirmu-cloud --bin kasirmu-cloud` -> **391 passed, 0 failed, 4 ignored**; `cargo test -p kasirmu-cloud --bin kasirmu-cloud conflict` -> 29 passed; `cargo fmt -p kasirmu-cloud -- --check` clean; clippy reports no finding in either touched file.
  - **What REMAINS OPEN in C19, measured and named so the tick is not over-read:** (a) `Decision::LastWriterWins` and `Decision::AutoMerge` are still COMPUTED (`conflict_resolution.rs:216-217`, `:233-236`) and consumed by nobody -- `sync_store.rs:187-195` and `sync_store/conflicts.rs:265-291` handle only `Flag`/`Stale`, and `winner_is_remote` is read outside its own construction nowhere; the checklist's own "do not do these" forbids DELETING the computation, so this needs a consumer decision. (b) Related and higher-impact: `sync_store/conflicts.rs:293-298` persists the incoming (LOSING) payload on a `LastWriterWins` decision with no conflict row -- a silent clobber riding on (a). (c) The `'delivering'` state is allowed by the CHECK (`20260902_outbox.sql:23`) and written by NO code path -- grep over all `*.rs` finds zero write sites, only doc comments -- so the drainer has no in-flight lease. These three were verified this pass and are NOT fixed here; each is a separate slice.
  - **The offline.rs half is CLOSED, confirmed independently:** all three functions C19 named carry both an explicit transaction and a compare-and-set guard -- `mark_offline_synced` (`offline.rs:627-636` + CAS at `:643`), `mark_offline_synced_for_tenant` (`:691-704` + CAS at `:708-740`), `mark_offline_resolved` (`:769-778` + CAS at `:785-814`) -- with tests at `offline_tests.rs:267/302/332/371/408/462/488/517/550`. Nothing in `db/offline.rs` remains open.
- [ ] **C19 slice A DONE 2026-09-23, commit 0e8ebcf2d** (`fix(core): make mark_offline_synced a transactional compare-and-set`; db/offline.rs +56, offline_tests.rs +94). Predicate `WHERE id = ?1 AND status = 'pending'`, and the DISAMBIGUATION after `affected == 0` is the load-bearing part: `NotFound` is preserved ONLY for an id that does not exist, while an existing non-pending row is an idempotent `Ok(())` no-op - and that split is **MANDATED, not chosen**, because an out-of-fence test (`platform/sync/src/pg_daemon_tests.rs::mark_offline_synced_is_idempotent`) asserts a second mark returns `is_ok()`. `Ok` is returned for the dead-lettered case too, so a stale caller cannot abort the batch loop in `apply_sync_outcomes`, which uses `?`. Transaction: join-or-own via `is_autocommit()`, the `audit.rs:411` `log_audit` idiom verbatim rather than a `_in_tx` twin. `cargo test -p kasirmu-core offline` -> **98 passed, 0 failed**. FALSIFICATION: reverting the predicate fails exactly the two new tests while the transaction test stays green - correct, because that test discriminates the transaction and not the predicate. Cross-crate: `-p platform-sync mark_offline` 3 passed, `-p platform-sync queue` 88 passed, `-p kasirmu-core --test offline_integration` 23 passed. **BRIEF CORRECTION, recorded:** this checklist cited `offline.rs:421-431`, which is STALE - the function is at 605-619 pre-fix and 646 post-fix. Line numbers in this checklist drift; re-read before fencing. **Its short-circuit answer produced C45 - a data-stranding defect on a THIRD outcome applier - and C19 slice B.** ORIGINAL: - [ ] **C19 Enforce conflicts, or stop pretending to** (5.4, 5.5) - consume Decision::LastWriterWins and Decision::AutoMerge or delete the computation; add sale to the money-entity test so a completed sale stops classifying as low-severity catalog metadata; write the 'delivering' state the CHECK constraint already allows; guard and transact mark_offline_synced (db/offline.rs:421-431).
- [x] **C20 Order the queue by priority on every push path** (5.3) - **DONE 2026-09-23, commit a8058a277** (`fix(sync): order the offline queue by priority on the daemon and bridge push paths`; platform/sync/src/daemon.rs +39, daemon_tests.rs +108, crates/kasirmu-bridge/src/sync.rs +55, sync_tests.rs +85). **ANSWER 1, and it corrects the item's own framing: priority is NOT a total order, so the fix is not a sort by priority.** `SyncPriority` has only three values and MOST items are `Critical` (every sale, void, refund and payment split passes `SyncPriority::Critical` at `db/offline.rs:391,463,489,538`), so ties are the COMMON case; and `created_at` is only millisecond precision, so a priority-only sort would leave same-millisecond items in SQLite's unspecified row order - a real run-to-run flip. The key chosen is therefore total, in three parts: **`(priority, created_at, id)` ascending**, where `id` is a UUID v7 primary key - unique AND time-ordered, which is what makes it a correct final tie-break rather than an arbitrary one. **ANSWER 2: no caller relies on a different order.** The batch is sorted ONCE before the push and every downstream consumer iterates that same vector (`transport.push_items` sends it; `apply_push_results` / `apply_sync_outcomes` `zip` it against outcomes), and the server documents that alignment as load-bearing (`sync_api.rs:283`: a reordering would mark the WRONG items as synced or failed). `queue.rs`'s effect-key/origin gate is per-item and order-independent, and was not touched. **Negative control run and reverted:** with the daemon's sort commented out, the acceptance test fails exactly as the bug describes - `left: ["bulk", "money", "catalog"]` vs `right: ["money", "catalog", "bulk"]`, i.e. the LOW item leading a CRITICAL one; the file was restored and verified by hash. Three tests per path (out-of-order critical-first, deterministic tie-break, arrival order within a tier). `cargo test -p platform-sync` -> **415 passed**; `cargo test -p kasirmu-bridge` -> **1385 passed** (run as a background job; it exceeds the 600s foreground ceiling). **TWO HONEST GAPS, both kept:** (1) the bridge tests pin the COMPARATOR, not the CALL SITE - deleting `order_pending_for_push` from `sync_run_scoped` leaves them green, so the bridge wiring is verified by inspection and compilation only, and closing it needs a `BridgeCtx` + mock HTTP harness the file does not have; (2) no real multi-item HTTP push was exercised end to end. **And it found that a shared expression ALREADY existed** (`lib.rs:150` `build_batches`, used by the third path, which was correct and left alone) - but it is private, priority-only, and unreachable from the bridge, which is why it could not be reused. That produced C49 and C51.
  ORIGINAL BRIEF:
- [x] **C21 Stop storing gift-card numbers in plaintext** (6.3) - **INVESTIGATED 2026-09-25, recommendation WITHDRAWN as misclassified. No production change; the cited exposure is real but the prescribed fix would break the product and protect nothing.** The FACTS in the item are correct: `gift_cards.card_number TEXT UNIQUE NOT NULL` with a plain index (20260813_init.sql:135-137, :1126), unencrypted, absent from .kasirpkg (whose payload is six table groups and excludes gift cards), present in every .db and .backup.db snapshot. What is wrong is the classification, and four independent findings drive the verdict:
  - **1. `card_number` is not cardholder data.** It is a merchant-issued store credit in the merchant's own format — `GC-` + 12 chars from `[A-Z0-9]` (`ui/src/utils/giftCardBarcode.ts:3,15-22`), not a PAN: no BIN, no Luhn, no issuer. PCI-DSS 3.2.1/3.3/3.4 govern *account* data; a gift-card code is a bearer token for a store liability. It is also not a *secret*: it is printed on the card and read at the till, which is the whole mechanism.
  - **2. The number is a FUNCTIONAL KEY, so "encrypt or mask" is not implementable as written.** Every operation resolves a card by scanning or typing it: `get_gift_card` matches `WHERE id = ?1 OR card_number = ?1` (db/gift_cards.rs:122-128), and redeem/topup/freeze/unfreeze/balance/detail all enter through `card_number_or_id` (db/gift_cards.rs:305,320,338, then :513,:636,:696; bridge/gift_cards.rs:65-199; both Tauri shells' commands/gift_cards.rs). Encrypting the column turns every lookup into a full-table scan with app-side decryption; masking it destroys the lookup outright. `mask_pan` is additionally the WRONG TOOL — it strips non-digits, so it renders `GC-ABC123456789` as `1234566789`, mangling a code that is not a PAN. Both halves of the recommendation are therefore withdrawn.
  - **3. The PIN beside it is the same story, and the repo had already reasoned this out in writing.** `docs/security/PCI-DSS_CHECKLIST.md:32-39` records the plaintext gift-card PIN as a deliberate CODE FINDING, "not cardholder data, so it does not violate 3.2.1", and explicitly contrasts it with the properly hashed `users.pin_hash`. The same classification applies one column over.
  - **4. Nothing leaves the device.** Gift cards are not a synced entity — `git grep gift_cards platform/sync` returns nothing, and no cloud-server route or sync table carries them. Exposure is local disk only, exactly as §6.3 itself scopes it, which is why it cannot justify a schema+key-management migration.
  **THE ONE REAL FINDING HERE — and it is not the one the item names.** The gift-card PIN is written at issue (`gift_cards.rs:49,63`) and read back by three SELECTs (`:125,:156,:239`) but is **never compared, hashed or verified anywhere in the tree** — no SQL predicate, no bridge function, no UI prompt to enter one (the only PIN surface is the issue modal's optional field). `docs/security/PCI-DSS_CHECKLIST.md:37` calls it "a plaintext authentication credential"; it authenticates nothing. A credential that is stored but never checked is a liability with no function, and the correct remedy is to decide the feature rather than to encrypt the column. **That decision is an OWNER question (product: is gift-card PIN protection intended?), so it is filed as a NEW item C69 below rather than answered here.** ORIGINAL BRIEF: - [ ] **C21 Stop storing gift-card numbers in plaintext** (6.3) - 20260813_init.sql:135-137, :1126; they ride every unfiltered .db and .backup.db snapshot.
- [x] **C22 Read base_total_minor in reports, and fix the screen that adds currencies** (8.5) - **DONE 2026-09-25, commit dc003aff6. FIXED, but by the OTHER half of the item, and the `base_*` premise is a dead end — recorded because the item's own suggested fix would not work.**
  - **The bug was real and worse than described.** `query_menu_engineering` grouped by `(product, unit_price)` with no currency term (menu_engineering.rs:94-108) while summing `SUM(sl.line_minor)` across whatever fell into the group, so a USD line and an IDR line became ONE revenue figure that is money in no currency.
  - **`base_total_minor` is NOT the fix, and the item is wrong to suggest it.** The migration's own comment (20260821_tender_currency.sql:1-5) states the three `base_*` columns are **NULL for single-currency sales, which it calls "the common case"**. Grouping on a nullable column would have split the common case into a NULL bucket. The column that always carries the answer is **`sale_lines.currency`**, which is `TEXT NOT NULL` (20260813_init.sql:587) — and the rest of the report suite already groups by exactly that (db/reports/product_sales.rs:175, revenue.rs:144, sales_summary.rs:165,197,227). So the fix is the existing convention, not a new column.
  - **THE USER-VISIBLE HALF, which the item does not mention and which is the actual harm.** The screen formatted EVERY amount with `useCurrency()` — the workspace default — so a USD row rendered as "Rp 2,500". That is a misstatement of a price, not a rounding artefact. Worse, the KPI cards re-created the same bug above the SQL: `result.rows.reduce((s, r) => s + r.total_revenue_minor, 0)` summed all currencies into one number and labelled it with the default. A backend-only fix would have left both defects live.
  - **WHAT CHANGED (dc003aff6, 6 files, +322/-44):** `MenuEngineeringRow` gains a required `currency: String`; SQL adds `sl.currency` to the SELECT, the GROUP BY **and** the merge key; the merge key becomes `(sku, currency)` — keying on SKU alone would have fused the currencies straight back together after the SQL had separated them; the screen drops `useCurrency()` entirely and formats each row with `row.currency` (table, tooltip, CSV) plus per-currency KPI buckets rendering one card set per code with the code in the label; the CSV gains an explicit `Currency` column; the dev-mock row carries a currency so dev builds are not `undefined`. Bridge and both Tauri shells pass the struct through untouched and needed no edit.
  - **TESTS: 6 new, and the first version of one was VACUOUS — the falsification caught it.** Rust: `menu_engineering_separates_currencies`, `every_row_declares_its_currency`, `a_single_currency_period_still_yields_one_row_per_product`. UI: per-row formatting uses the row's code, and totals bucket per currency. **The falsification of the Rust test FAILED the first time:** reverting the GROUP BY left the test GREEN, because my fixture used different unit prices per currency (700 vs 115500) and the OLD grouping key included `unit_price`, so it split them by accident. Fixed the fixture to the SAME unit price in both currencies — the only shape that reproduces the defect — after which reverting the fix gives `left: 1, right: 2` (one row instead of two). **A test that passes with the fix reverted is not evidence, and this one was caught only by checking.**
  - **Evidence:** `cargo test -p kasirmu-reporting` -> **78 passed, 0 failed**; `cargo test -p kasirmu-bridge` -> **1404 passed, 0 failed** (in an isolated target dir — the shared one had a `kasirmu_bridge-*.exe` file lock from a concurrent agent, retried rather than assumed); UI `npm run typecheck` clean; `npx eslint` clean; `npx vitest run` on the screen + contract + dev-mock suites -> **104 passed**. Falsified once on the Rust side (after fixing the fixture) and once on the UI side (single currency-blind card -> the bucket test fails).
  - **Left alone deliberately:** `daily_summary.rs` has the identical shape but `git grep query_daily_summary` shows it is called **only from its own tests** — no bridge, no IPC, no UI. The review says the same. `export_daily_summary`, which IS wired, is a different function and already per-currency. Touching the unreachable one would be an unverifiable change. ORIGINAL BRIEF: - [ ] **C22 Read base_total_minor in reports, and fix the screen that adds currencies** (8.5) - kasirmu-reporting/menu_engineering.rs:90-95 is wired to a live screen (ui/src/api/reports.ts:529-534); the schema already carries sales.base_currency and base_total_minor (20260821_tender_currency.sql:1-8) and no report reads them.
- [x] **C23 Make a panic visible and recoverable** (14.3) - **ALL THREE CLAUSES ADDRESSED 2026-09-25: 2fdf7f2c8 (running flag) + eff8d949e (IPC deadline) + bd4225d31 (daemon health registry). Ticked with ONE EXPLICIT PARTIAL, stated here rather than buried.** The review's acceptance has two halves. The second -- "a forced panic in a sync worker leaves `is_running()` false so a restart succeeds" -- is **met by a real test** (`the_running_flag_is_cleared_when_the_owning_task_panics`). The first -- "a forced panic in a command body makes the caller receive Err rather than a pending promise" -- is met **in effect but not in mechanism**: the caller now receives `Err` instead of a pending promise, but that `Err` is produced by a frontend deadline (120s), NOT by the Rust command path, because Tauri 2.11.3 exposes no seam to catch a panic in (verified: no `catch_unwind` and no panic hook anywhere in the pinned crate, `src/ipc/mod.rs:329` spawns the command bare). So the observable behaviour the acceptance asks for is delivered; the mechanism it names is not, and cannot be at this layer. **If a later pass adds a Rust-side guard, this tick stays valid and the gap below closes.**
  - **Clause 3 (daemon running flag) - DONE.** `running` was cleared by a statement placed AFTER the run loop (daemon.rs:467, the identical twin at pg_daemon.rs:209), so a panic unwinding out of a tick skipped it: the daemon reported `running: true` forever and `start` refused with "already running" until the process restarted. Twelve daemons were exposed (both sync daemons). Replaced with a RAII `RunningFlagGuard` whose `Drop` runs during unwinding, applied to BOTH daemons (`DaemonStatus` and `PgDaemonStatus` are separate structs, so the guard is generic over a small `HasRunningFlag` trait rather than duplicated).
  - **THE SUBTLE PART, and it cost two wrong revisions - recorded because the review's own text would not have caught it.** Supersession must be detected by **channel identity**, not by the slot being non-empty: a healthy run holds its OWN `watch::Sender` in the shutdown slot for its entire life, so "the slot is `Some`" is true of every running daemon and distinguishes nothing. `watch::Sender::same_channel` (tokio 1.52.3, watch.rs:950 - `Arc::ptr_eq` on the inner shared state) answers it. **BUT identity alone is also wrong in the other direction:** `stop()` takes the sender *before* awaiting the loop (daemon.rs:638), so the slot is `None` on the ordinary shutdown path - an identity-only test reads that as "not mine" and leaves the flag SET, which broke **10 existing lifecycle tests** (`daemon_start_and_stop`, `daemon_stop_twice_is_idempotent`, `daemon_status_updates_running_flag`, `daemon_stop_then_immediate_start_yields_running_daemon`, `two_daemons_cannot_run_simultaneously_on_same_db`, and the pg twins). The correct predicate is `Some(same) => true, None => true, Some(other) => false` - only a DIFFERENT sender is supersession. **The lifecycle suite caught this; review would not have.** That failure is why the final rule is spelled out in the code comment.
  - **TESTS: 7 new, all non-vacuous.** 5 on the guard (clears on panic; clears on the orderly path; clears on drop; a superseded run does NOT clear, on both paths; presence of a foreign sender is not ownership) + 2 on the empty-slot case that the bug above was about. **Falsified twice, each time by reverting the guard and watching exactly the intended tests fail:** (i) `Drop` made a no-op (= the pre-C23 behaviour) -> `the_running_flag_is_cleared_when_the_owning_task_panics` + `a_run_that_still_owns_the_slot_clears_on_drop` failed, 0 passed, with the wedge message; (ii) `still_owns_slot` reverted to identity-only -> `an_empty_slot_still_clears_the_flag` + `an_empty_slot_clears_the_flag_on_drop` failed. Both probes restored and re-verified green.
  - **Evidence:** `cargo test -p platform-sync` -> **435 passed, 0 failed** (+ 22 ignored across three integration binaries); `cargo fmt -p platform-sync -- --check` exit 0; `cargo clippy -p platform-sync --all-targets` silent. The full-suite run is the one that matters here, because the first version of this fix compiled, passed its own new tests, and still broke ten others.
  - **CLAUSE 1 (the IPC hang) - DONE 2026-09-25, commit eff8d949e, and the fix is a LIVENESS BOUND, not the catch the item asks for. That distinction is the finding.**
    - **Why the item's prescription cannot be followed as written.** It asks for `catch_unwind` in command bodies. There are **514 `#[tauri::command]` declarations** across 73 files in the desktop shell (and 73+ more in the mobile shell), so a per-body wrapper is 514 edits with 514 chances to miss one; more decisively, **the framework gives no seam to catch in**. Verified in the pinned source: `tauri-2.11.3/src/ipc/mod.rs:329` spawns the command task with no panic guard, and `git grep catch_unwind|panic_hook|set_hook tauri-2.11.3/src` returns **nothing at all** -- no hook, no guard, nowhere to install one. `generate_handler!` is a macro, so the registration list cannot be wrapped either. The honest options were 514 edits, a vendored Tauri fork, or bounding the wait.
    - **What was done instead: bound the wait at the one seam every IPC call already crosses.** `ui/src/api/tauri.ts:24` was a bare `export { invoke } from '@tauri-apps/api/core'` re-export, imported by 16 modules -- including `utils/logged-invoke.ts`, which the other ~99 API files use. Replacing that re-export with a wrapper therefore covers **both** paths with one change. A call still outstanding at `IPC_TIMEOUT_MS` (120s) rejects with an error naming the command and stating that a retry is safe. **An unbounded hang becomes an ordinary failure the UI already renders** -- this does not make a panicked command return `Err`, and the record should not be read as claiming it does.
    - **120s is a liveness bound, not a performance budget.** The slowest real operation is a full backup/export in the low seconds and the transport's own HTTP ceiling is 30s, so this fires only on a call that will never answer. It is exported by name so a legitimately slower command can raise it rather than be silently cut off.
    - **TESTS: 3 new in `ui/src/__tests__/tauri-webview-predicate.test.ts`** (the file that already owns this module's contract): a never-settling command is still pending *before* the deadline and **rejects after it**, naming the command; a normal rejection still rejects **promptly and with the original error** (proving the timeout neither masks nor delays real failures); a normal resolution still resolves. **FALSIFIED:** replacing the wrapper with the old pass-through makes the hang test **time out at 10s** -- the exact reproduction of the cashier's stuck spinner -- while the other 8 stay green.
    - **TWO SELF-INFLICTED REGRESSIONS, caught by the suite and recorded because the first fix compiled and looked right.** (i) **38 failures across 10 files** on the first full run: my wrapper always forwarded three arguments (`cmd, args, options`), while the upstream signature defaults `args = {}` and callers pass two -- so every spy asserting `invoke` was "called with 2 arguments" saw 3. Fixed by forwarding `options` only when the caller supplied it, which keeps the wrapper invisible to callers and their tests. (ii) Re-verified `invoke`'s upstream signature (`InvokeArgs` / `InvokeOptions`) and preserved it exactly rather than narrowing to `Record<string, unknown>`. After (i): **2 failures left, both pre-existing flakiness under full-suite load** -- and that is *verified*, not assumed: `NodeTopologyEditor` (**548 passed** alone) and `AppShell` (**35 passed** alone) both pass in isolation, and a **baseline run with this change reverted also failed** (3 different tests: `NodeTopologyEditorDevMock`, `SettingsPage`, `ProductLookupScreen a11y`). Suite state: **10511 passed / 2 failed with the change, 10501 passed / 3 failed without it** -- the failures move around, which is what load-order flakiness looks like.
    - **Evidence:** `npm run typecheck` clean; `npx eslint` clean on both files; `npx vitest run` on the two seam suites -> **16 passed**; full suite -> **10511 passed, 24 skipped, 3 todo**.
  - **CLAUSE 2 (the watchdog) - DONE 2026-09-25, commit bd4225d31, as the item's own fallback: "or at minimum tear down and report". RESTART IS NOT IMPLEMENTABLE AT THIS LAYER and that is the finding, not a shortcut.**
    - **Why restart was not done.** `spawn_daemon` takes a CONSUMED future (`impl Future<Output = ()> + Send + 'static`, lib.rs:339), so the watchdog holds no way to re-create the task it just saw die. Restarting would mean changing all **17 call sites** (12 desktop, 5 tablet) to pass a factory, plus inventing restart-loop policy -- backoff, a retry ceiling, and what a second failure means. That is a design change, not a small one, and guessing it would have been worse than naming it.
    - **What WAS done: the death is no longer invisible.** New `platform/startup/src/daemon_health.rs` -- a `DaemonRegistry` of `{name, kind, state}` where state is `Running | Panicked | ExitedUnexpectedly | Completed | Unknown`. The watchdog now writes the state it observes in addition to logging it, and `dead()` returns the set of daemons that died. This is the same argument the crate already applies to write-only database projections (see `report_sales_projection_stays_removed`, pinned a few lines from these tests): **a log line nothing reads is not a report.**
    - **The registry is a PROCESS GLOBAL, and that choice is what kept the change small and complete.** A daemon's liveness is a property of the process, not of any shell object, so `daemon_registry()` returns a `OnceLock`-backed singleton -- mirroring the `static RUNTIME` a few lines above it. Consequence: **every existing `spawn_daemon` call is observed with ZERO call-site changes.** The alternative (a field on each shell's `AppState`) would have meant editing `AppState` in two shells and threading the handle through 17 spawn sites for no extra capability.
    - **TESTS: 16 new, all non-vacuous.** 10 registry-unit tests (`daemon_health_tests.rs`: transitions, the dead-set definition, one-shot-completed-is-not-a-failure, one-shot-PANICKED-is-a-failure, unregistered-death-still-surfaces, re-register-resets, sorted output, and that a clone SHARES state -- a clone that copied would make the whole feature silently useless). 6 integration tests (`startup_tests.rs`) driving the real spawn harness: a panicking daemon records `Panicked`; a returning daemon records `ExitedUnexpectedly`; **a LIVE daemon stays `Running`** (so the suite cannot pass by marking everything dead); a finished one-shot leaves no dead record; `dead()` lists every dead daemon; and **`spawn_daemon` itself -- the entry point the shells actually call -- records into the process global**, which is the test that fails if the global wiring is reverted.
    - **FALSIFIED:** replacing `registry.record(name, state)` with a discard makes **3 of the 4 death tests fail** (`a_panicking_daemon_is_recorded_as_panicked`, `a_daemon_that_stops_looping_is_recorded_as_exited`, `the_registry_lists_every_dead_daemon`) while `a_live_daemon_stays_running` correctly still passes -- a live daemon reads `Running` either way. Restored and re-verified.
    - **Evidence:** `cargo test -p platform-startup` -> **99 passed, 0 failed** (+1 doctest); `cargo fmt -p platform-startup -- --check` exit 0; `cargo clippy -p platform-startup --all-targets` silent; `cargo check -p kasirmu-app -p kasirmu-mobile` clean, so both shells still build against the changed crate.
  - **WHAT REMAINS, named rather than implied:** (i) **no supervisor consumes the registry yet** -- nothing calls `daemon_registry().dead()` in production, so today the set is available but unread. Exposing it through IPC and a diagnostics surface is the natural next step and is a UI/command change, not a harness one. (ii) **the cloud server's email queue worker is still a bare `tokio::spawn` with no watchdog at all** (apps/cloud-server/src/email_pg/queue_worker.rs:32) -- out of this crate's reach and untouched. (iii) **a Rust-side `catch_unwind` is still the better half of clause 1** -- the frontend stops lying about being busy, but the backend cannot yet report that a command died; closing it needs either 514 wrappers or an upstream Tauri seam. None of these three is blocked on a decision; they are simply not done.
  ORIGINAL BRIEF: - [ ] **C23 Make a panic visible and recoverable** (14.3) - catch_unwind (or the Tauri equivalent) so a command returns an error instead of hanging the promise; make spawn_watched restart or at least tear down and report (platform/startup/src/lib.rs:341-361); clear the sync daemon's running flag on task exit, not only on the normal path (platform/sync/src/daemon.rs:353, :461).

The previous note's evidence - `git grep a11y .github/workflows/dev-ci.yml` returning nothing - was true but answered the wrong question: it hunted a dedicated job and missed the suite's real execution path. **PROVEN NON-VACUOUSLY:** injected `<button className="probe" />` (unnamed) into `StaffLoginScreen`'s RENDERED username step -> `npx vitest run src/__tests__/a11y/StaffLoginScreen.a11y.test.tsx` exits **1** with `[a11y] critical: button-name` and `Expected the HTML found at $('.staff-login-probe2') to have no violations`. (First attempt put the probe in `renderPinDots` and it PASSED - which is the finding below, not a broken probe.) **THE REAL C24 GAP IS COVERAGE, NOT ENFORCEMENT.** Every screen's a11y suite asserts ONE rendered state (`it('has no axe violations on initial render')`) and none interact (`interacts=false` for StaffLoginScreen/SalesHistoryScreen/ProductLookupScreen). `StaffLoginScreen`'s initial step is `username`, so `renderPinDots`/`renderPinPad` are never in the scanned container - the entire PIN pad (keypad, clear, backspace, submit) is unscanned, and that is exactly why the mouse-only `<tr>` in `TransactionLogScreen` survived: that surface is asserted by no a11y file at all. **FIXED (86f874e2b):** `TransactionLogScreen.tsx:238-241` row was `onClick`-only; now `tabIndex={0} role="button" aria-expanded={isExpanded}` + per-row `aria-label` (`inv-log-row-expand-aria`/`inv-log-row-collapse-aria`, en+id) + Enter/Space `onKeyDown` (`preventDefault` on both, Space otherwise scrolls), mirroring `LoyaltyManagementScreen.tsx:353` rather than inventing a second convention. **TWO NEW TESTS PIN IT** (`ui/src/__tests__/TransactionLogScreen.test.tsx`): the row is focusable with role/state/name, and expand+collapse works from the keyboard alone. **FALSIFIED:** reverting to the pre-fix `onClick`-only shape fails exactly those two (`expected -1 to be +0` = tabIndex; `expected "vi.fn()" to be called 1 times, but got 0`), 11 others stay green. `npx vitest run` on both files: **37 passed**. **GATE HONESTY (0f96ab2eb):** corrected `scripts/gates.json`'s `a11y-advisory` `_note` and `scripts/check.sh`'s block comment + WARN text, which claimed "not checked in CI". Registry unchanged at **62 required / 5 advisory**; `verify-ci-docs-drift.py` **0 drift**, `test-runner-labels.py` green, `bash -n scripts/check.sh` exit 0.

Cargo-rule findings are PACKAGE-granular, keyed on the manifest path (`:342`), so the seven type-only shims and the one behavioural edge CANNOT be separated by a rule: they live in the same `crates/kasirmu-core/Cargo.toml:17-24`, which carries all **8 normal `[dependencies]`** on module crates -- exactly the 8 baseline entries. Shim FILE count is **11** (`crates/kasirmu-core/src/*.rs:3` `pub use modules_*::models`), which is why D3 says "delete the eleven shims" while the rule count is 7: 11 files, 7 type-only edges.

The rule-violation half is nearly exact and points at ONE file.**
  - **THE FIFTH SUB-CLAIM IS EXACT AND WAS A LIVE PRODUCTION DEFECT -- and the review found only ONE of the TWO sites (commit `4eda7dc07`).** `KdsScreen.tsx:390-394` really did carry a bare React `<Profiler>` in production code, and a tree-wide search found a **second** one the item never names, `ProductLookupScreen.tsx:239-243`. Neither was gated, `vite.config.ts` sets no `drop_console`, and both screens re-render on a polling tick -- so `onRender` ran on every render in a shipped build and wrote to the console. Fixed by extracting one gated `DevProfiler` wrapper (`ui/src/components/DevProfiler.tsx`) that returns `children` directly when `import.meta.env.DEV` is false, so in production no `Profiler` is mounted at all rather than being mounted and doing nothing.
  - **THE TEST FOR THIS TOOK THREE ATTEMPTS AND THE FIRST TWO WERE VACUOUS -- recorded because both look reasonable and both passed on broken code.** (1) `expect(console.debug).not.toHaveBeenCalled()` in production **PASSED with the gate removed**: `Profiler.onRender` lands after a synchronous assertion (measured `SPY_CALLS=0` while the Profiler's own log line printed). (2) The mirror assertion in development **FAILED with the gate present**: the callback only logs above a 1ms threshold and a test render measured **0.31ms and 0.14ms**, so it asserted the threshold, not the gate. (3) The working shape replaces React's `Profiler` with a recorder via `vi.mock('react')` and asserts the element is (not) constructed -- because a `Profiler` adds no DOM node, this is the only property that separates the two branches. **Falsified:** removing the gate fails the production case; restoring it passes all four.

NOT covered: the full UI suite has **8 failures in 5 files** (`animationCompliance`, a staff-CSS dead-class census, `PaymentModalSaleFlow`, `InventoryAdjustmentScreen`), none touching these four files and all peer-owned -- recorded as observed, not attributed.

The ONE tracked build output in the whole tree was **`ui/vite.config.d.ts` (84 B)**: `.gitignore:185` ignored its `.js` twin but not the `.d.ts`, and the comment above it even names the mechanism ("emitted from vite.config.ts by the TS build"). Confirmed generated, not source: `ui/vite.config.ts` is the source, `ui/tsconfig.node.json:3` is `composite: true` with `include: ["vite.config.ts"]`, `ui/tsconfig.json:28` references it, and `ui/package.json:12` runs `tsc -b` on every build. So the file was renamed to what it is -- a build artefact -- by widening the ignore to both emitted forms and deleting the tracked copy (`delete mode 100644`). `npm run typecheck` is clean after the removal and `git check-ignore` now matches it.

## Owner decisions - code is blocked on these

Full analysis - deciding facts, options with pros and cons, what would change the answer, and a recommendation - is in manager-codebase-review-decisions.md (D1-D8). The lines below are the index.

- [ ] **D1 Is OZ_MASTER_KEY set on any deployment?** Decides whether 6.2 is a live confidentiality break or a latent one.
- [ ] **D2 Has any PostgreSQL deployment run scripts/rls-cutover.sql?** Decides whether tenant isolation exists there at all.
- [ ] **D3 Architecture rule or tier order?** (10.2) Re-tier the checker with an ADR, or move ~5k lines of models back down. Forced by 2026-11-06.
- [ ] **D4 Is qris-core meant to be publishable?** (14.4) If not, it is a one-line manifest fix away from harmless.
- [ ] **D5 Is in-app restore a product feature or an ops procedure?** (14.1) Today the only restore is a CLI command.
- [ ] **D6 Is LAN KDS a product feature?** (6.7) It works and is safely defaulted, but nothing in the product can configure it.
- [ ] **D7 How much plugin trust is acceptable?** (6.4) Signed-and-verified and unsigned-hot-reload are the two ends; there is no documented middle.
- [ ] **D8 Does the unified container ship?** (12.1) If production runs the two-service compose, the five 404s are dev-only - and that should be written down.

---

## Do not do these

- [ ] Flip RLS from ENABLE to FORCE while the app connects as the table owner.
- [ ] Hand-edit the generated PG init instead of running scripts/generate-pg-migration.py.
- [ ] Backfill stock by setting qty to the sum of movement deltas.
- [ ] Raise the registration-gate debt ceilings - the ceiling is the finding.
- [ ] Delete the conflict Decision computation instead of consuming it.
- [ ] Weaken the concurrency test to success_count <= 1.
- [ ] Mark clippy or e2e advisory to make the gate registry self-consistent.
- [ ] Add busy_timeout to that test while leaving fresh_db() pragma-free.
- [ ] Add CHECK (qty >= 0) before the backfill migration has run.
- [ ] Retry the same fix a fourth time after three red gates - diagnose the root cause instead.

## Wave 4 - added during implementation

- [x] **C18 P1.3b [P1] Give the shift invariant a database-level guard.** - **DONE 2026-09-23, commit 152755e83** (`feat(core): guard a single open shift per user at the schema level`; 4 files, +358/-1): the partial unique index lands in `crates/kasirmu-core/migrations/20261011_open_shift_uniqueness.sql` (new, 94 lines) with the registry entry, a census pin bump 188 -> 189 plus three tests (+249), and the regenerated PG file. **The raw-SQL acceptance is the strong part:** `open_shift_uniqueness_index_bites_on_raw_sql` runs the registry MINUS this migration first and the raw `INSERT INTO shifts (id, user_id)` SUCCEEDS TWICE (asserts `open = 2`), then runs the full registry and the second insert is refused as `ConstraintViolation` naming `shifts.user_id` - so the refusal provably comes from the index and not from `Store::open_shift`. It also omits `status` on the insert deliberately, proving the column DEFAULT and the index's `WHERE status = 'open'` agree with what writers actually produce, and it checks BOTH exemptions (a different user is accepted; the same user is accepted again once the first shift is closed). **And it cannot brick a store:** the migration RECONCILES before it constrains - a deterministic `UPDATE` closes every open shift except the most recently opened (`opened_at DESC, id DESC`), data-preservingly (no row deleted, no money invented, counted-cash columns left NULL, reason in `notes`) - so the `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX` cannot fail on any database that reaches it, pinned by `open_shift_uniqueness_migration_reconciles_pre_existing_duplicates`. `cargo test -p kasirmu-core migration` -> **44 passed, 0 failed**, run both before and after the PG regeneration. open_shift is now atomic, but there is still no partial unique index on shifts(user_id) WHERE status = 'open', so any SQL writer outside that function can still create a second open shift. A migration adding the partial index closes the same class of hole the app-level fix closed - and it must land alongside its own index-surface pin bump in migrations_tests.rs, which is why it is a separate slice from any migration already in flight.

- [x] **C5b [P0] Mirror the EOD fix into the tablet's duplicate builder** - **DONE 2026-09-23, commit 1eee8e0f**. Both doors now call the shared core queries; both hazard pins INVERTED (names kept) and the boundary-row pin gained an anti-vacuity guard. The tablet builder also lost a latent bug: its payment query had no COALESCE and grouped by method without currency while the header is per-row.
- [x] **C6b [P1] Validate the timezone on the write path** - **DONE 2026-09-23, commit 1fea7d78**: validate_provision_args now rejects anything outside the three Indonesian presets plus UTC, reusing the same predicate the bridge's update path and the regional axis validator use - so the accepted set has one owner. Rejection only, storage untouched, no migration. Six unsupported values rejected with a field-named message; Asia/Pontianak is deliberately in the rejection list because the reporting path can resolve it while the write boundary's closed set does not (proof the set was reused, not widened) - crates/kasirmu-core/src/db/provisioning.rs:467-476 and :514-564 insert locations.timezone verbatim with no validation while crates/kasirmu-bridge/src/locations.rs:314-321 accepts only Asia/Jakarta|Asia/Makassar|Asia/Jayapura|UTC. It also recorded plainly that **no IPC command is registered** for `list_restore_candidates` / `restore_prepare` / `restore_status` (measured across both `invoke_handler!` blocks - consistent with the outstanding S5 slice), so the in-app trigger is documented as bridge functions with the operator path marked unverified, and the CLI restore is given as the currently reachable route with `scripts/restore-db.sh` warned as weaker (no `validate_candidate`, no schema gate, no rollback). Split out of C11, whose code half is done.
- [x] **C12b [P2] Wire the variance report to an operator surface** - **DONE 2026-09-23, commit ec0ab7aef** (`feat(cli): surface the stock variance report as oz stock-variance`; 5 files, +605/-5, two of them new). **Surface decided and justified:** the CLI already owns the read-only maintenance surface - `oz export <kind>` renders a read-only report, `oz sale get --format text|json` renders a text table, `oz credential-deltas` is a report-only bounded operator command - so the new subcommand REUSES `open_db`, the path-guard shape from `open_store_for_credential_deltas`, and `run_sale_get`'s text-table rendering rather than inventing a fourth pattern. Text rather than CSV was chosen deliberately: `oz export` uses CSV because it dumps bulk data, while this is a <=1000-row triage view, and the core row type carries no `Serialize` (core was out of fence, so hand-rolling one would have created a second definition of the row - the divergent-twins failure this workstream keeps finding). **Operator command: `oz stock-variance --db <path> [--min-difference N] [--limit N]`**, defaults `--min-difference 1` (hide consistent pairs) and `--limit 100`. **All four acceptance points asserted, not assumed:** reaches the report and prints rows (the C12 scenario end to end - stored 8 / ledger 4 / diff 4 - plus the clean-ledger empty case as a real answer); **read-only ASSERTED** by reusing core's own `report_performs_no_writes` pattern (a fingerprint of `stock_movements` rows, `stock_summary` rows and `SUM(qty)` before/after across three calls including the capped and empty paths must be identical); a missing path fails clearly and does NOT create the file or either sidecar; and the bound plus validation errors surface rather than being swallowed. `cargo test -p kasirmu-cli` -> **132 passed, 0 failed**, plus a LIVE end-to-end run against a real database (migrate then stock-variance exit 0; mistyped `--db` exit 1 with the file not created; `--limit 5000` exit 1 with a field-named error; `product list` exit 0, so no regression to sibling arms). **Two judgement calls worth keeping:** the report is capped by construction, so a truncated list would read as 'this is everything' - the one failure a reconciliation report must not have - so an explicit `ROW BOUND REACHED` note appears whenever the returned count equals the limit; and the path guard is scoped to this subcommand ONLY, because `migrate`/`init-db`/`restore` legitimately provision a database, so pushing the check into shared `open_db` would have turned a one-command footgun into a first-run outage. It deliberately did NOT wire the report into any startup hook or retention path - it stays operator-invoked.

- [x] **C31 [P2] Validate the Caddyfile itself, not just its routing semantics.** - **DONE 2026-09-23, commit e48cea5e8** (`scripts/check-unified-routes.mjs` only; the Caddyfile needed no fix and no CI-only variant was required). **Availability verdict first: no `caddy` binary is reachable from the gate** - `which caddy` finds nothing on PATH, and `ops/docker/Dockerfile.unified` obtains the binary only in its RUNTIME stage (`COPY --from=caddy /usr/bin/caddy`, line 227), i.e. inside the deployed image where no gate runs. The gate is invoked as a plain node step by `scripts/check.sh:102`, and `grep -c check-unified-routes .github/workflows/dev-ci.yml` = **0**, so it is not a CI step either. A `caddy:2-alpine` image IS cached locally and `docker run ... caddy validate` works here - **but the worker refused to make a static gate depend on a Docker daemon and a cached image, which is the invented download step the brief forbade.** So it built the strongest offline check available: a real lexer plus structural parser, taking an optional path argument so the failure branch is exercisable without editing a tracked file. **NEGATIVE CONTROLS, each cross-checked against the real binary through Docker:** missing final `}` -> gate exit 1 with `line 41: ':80' opens a block that is never closed` (caddy: `unexpected token 'gzip', expecting '}'`); stray token in a block -> exit 1 with the directive named (caddy agrees); extra `}` -> exit 1 `unmatched '}'`; `import not_defined_anywhere` -> exit 1 naming neither snippet nor file (caddy: `File to import not found`). **Every fixture's gate verdict matches caddy's.** **Non-vacuity preserved:** deleting the `/api/v1/midtrans/*` handle block still fails on the ROUTING rule, not the new syntax rule, so the pre-existing behaviour is intact. **And the honest part: it found and removed THREE FALSE POSITIVES before committing** - a depth-agnostic directive whitelist rejected `output` (inside `log`), `user` (inside `basic_auth`) and `@api` (a named matcher), all of which real caddy accepts, and an arity rule rejected `reverse_proxy` with no upstream, which also validates clean. Each was confirmed against the binary, and the check was narrowed to depth 1 where the directive set is finite and documented, accepting matchers by shape. **A validator that fails a legal deploy is worse than the bug it prevents.** Stated limits, kept: it is a STRUCTURAL parse, not caddy's grammar (no argument grammar, no matcher semantics, no block-context legality); it deliberately does NOT flag an unterminated quoted string because real caddy accepts it (`encode "gzip` -> `Valid configuration`); and the real validator remains the deployed image's own `caddy validate` at start-up - this gate is a cheap pre-deploy proxy, not a replacement. Residual, out of the read-only fence: `caddy fmt` reports the file is not canonically formatted (line 13) and the `/_/*` routing-comment indent at line 13 looks misaligned; neither affects validity. scripts/check-unified-routes.mjs parses apps/unified/Caddyfile as text, so a syntax error that the parser tolerates still ships and the container fails to start. Add caddy validate (or an equivalent parse) to the same gate, or state why it cannot run in CI. Found while closing C27.

## Wave 5 - the RLS table audit (added 2026-09-23)

Source: an audit of every table in `RLS_TABLES` (scripts/generate-pg-migration.py) against every production write path in the repository. **10 of the 34 protected tables never have `tenant_id` written by any code path**: locations, product_activity, product_bundles, bundle_items, product_taxes, product_variants, edc_terminals, user_location_access, media_assets, media_thumbnails, payment_gateways, payment_settlements (the last four have no writer at all). The tenant_id column exists; nothing fills it, so every row lands on the default tenant.

**Severity, stated precisely.** Every one of these is LATENT today: no deployment has run scripts/rls-cutover.sql (that is open question D2), so no policy is forcing anything yet. But once the cutover runs, a row written without a tenant is invisible to the tenant that owns it - and in two cases that is not merely isolation, it is a wrong answer to a paying customer:

- **locations is the expensive one.** apps/cloud-server/src/quota_detector.rs:336 runs `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM locations WHERE tenant_id = $1` to enforce the per-plan location quota. A tenant whose locations all sit on 'default' scores **zero**, so the check either lets unlimited locations through or refuses a legitimate one - the direction depends on which way the detector compares. Either way the number is fiction.
- **product_activity is the quiet one.** apps/cloud-server/src/email_pg/popularity.rs:230-236 builds the cloud popularity roll-up with the same tenant predicate, so it reads zero rows for every real tenant - a feature that reports "no data" forever and looks like a product gap rather than a missing column write.

The generator states the rule these tables break, in its own comment at scripts/generate-pg-migration.py:475-476: add a table to this list only after its write path demonstrably sets `tenant_id`. A table whose writer never touches the column is not protected by RLS; it is only advertised as protected, which is worse than either state.

- [x] **C32 SITE 1 DONE 2026-09-23, commit 8ce101607** (`fix(core): stamp the caller tenant on the provisioned location row`; 2 files, +104/-10) - the item as a whole stays OPEN because D9 re-scoped it; see the re-scope note below. `provision_device_inner` now matches on `args.tenant_id.as_deref()`: the **linked** arm binds the caller's claim verbatim as `?6` onto `locations.tenant_id`, and the **local** arm OMITS the column so the schema's own `NOT NULL DEFAULT 'default'` supplies it - no code fallback, no bound NULL. **Negative control PROVED**: reverting the bind to the pre-fix unconditional INSERT fails the linked test with `a linked location must not sit on the single-tenant column DEFAULT` (`left: "default"`, `right: "default"`), and the file was restored to a verified byte-identical hash. Evidence: `cargo test -p kasirmu-core provisioning` -> **27 passed, 0 failed, 3236 filtered out**, both new tests green; the linked test asserts the stored value is not `'default'` AND equals `tenant-abc` AND matches the peer `provisioning` row, so one source cannot yield two tenants. Manager re-verified the artifact: `git show --stat` = exactly those 2 files and the diff carries the `INSERT INTO locations (..., tenant_id) VALUES (..., ?6)` arm. The column exists and no migration is needed; the work is one correct tenant source per insert site.
  Fence: crates/kasirmu-core/src/db/locations.rs, edc_terminals.rs, provisioning.rs, products.rs, product_bundles.rs, tax/assignments.rs, popularity.rs, products_crud.rs and their sibling `*_tests.rs` files; apps/desktop-tauri/src/state.rs:410. No migration, no schema change. Scripts/generate-pg-migration.py and the generated 20260813_init.pg.sql belong to C33 only.
  **Slices** (each independently shippable, all four dispatched 2026-09-23): **S1** locations + user_location_access + edc_terminals (the quota-detector path first, because it is the one with a wrong answer and not just a missing row). **S2** the catalog children: product_variants, product_bundles, bundle_items, product_taxes. **S3** product_activity (both the sale-time recorder and the product-create seed - establish which are live). **S4** the list correction, C33.
  **The trap, and it is the same one in all three code slices:** do NOT read the tenant from a parent row inside the same transaction. On SQLite `products.tenant_id` is stamped by a LATER separate UPDATE (apps/desktop-tauri/src/routes/products.rs:307), so a parent read in the same transaction still returns 'default' - a fix that does that moves the bug rather than closing it, and it passes any test that only checks "the value is set". The tenant must come from the same source the enclosing function already uses for its other rows (the caller's tenant claim). Each slice's test must assert the stamped value EQUALS the caller's tenant, not merely that it is non-empty.
  **TWO CORRECTIONS TO THIS ITEM, from the fence requests of 2026-09-23 - read them before trusting the pointers above:** (1) `apps/desktop-tauri/src/routes/products.rs` **does not exist**. The `:307` stamp this item cites is `crates/kasirmu-api/src/routes/products.rs:307` - the **cloud REST** surface, where the tenant is JWT-derived. So the "parent written at 'default' then stamped by a later UPDATE" pattern may not exist on the **desktop** path at all, in which case desktop store tables are simply all `'default'` and the composite-FK ordering hazard is a cloud-path hazard. Establish which before slicing. (2) `product_activity` is **excluded from cloud sync** - ADR #37 D4, `docs/decisions/2026-08-11-adr37-product-popularity-index.md:134-143` - and `apps/cloud-server` references the table only in the popularity query and a test-name list. So stamping the tenant is necessary but possibly **not sufficient** for the roll-up to read rows; the transport into PG is a separate open question.
  Also verified this round: `refunds` is **fixed** (commit 6f8cd1e34 - the originator reads the sale's tenant at db/refunds.rs:361 and the receiving terminal reads its own local sales row at platform/sync/src/queue.rs:500, both on the caller's transaction, with a test asserting the refund row and its outbox row share one tenant). The comment at db/offline.rs:407-411 asserting that refunds.tenant_id is never written is now stale prose and should be corrected by whoever next touches that file.
  **RE-SCOPED 2026-09-23 by three fence requests and the evidence they produced - read this before slicing any of it. The item as originally written was wrong on the desktop path.**
  The original claim was "eight tables never write tenant_id, so stamp a tenant at each insert". Three workers independently refused to do that and each came back with evidence instead. What actually holds:
  - **Only one site has a real source available.** `provisioning.rs:469` is fixable today because the same function, in the same transaction, already stamps `args.tenant_id` into the peer `provisioning` row at `:492-501` - a caller-supplied value the code already trusts for this fact. That one is being fixed, with the `local`/`None` case ruled on: omit the column and let the schema's own `NOT NULL DEFAULT 'default'` supply it, because `migrations/20260907_add_location_tenant_id.sql:10-11` declares that default to BE the single-tenant semantics.
  - **The others have no honest value at all.** `create_location_profile` (`db/locations.rs:184`) - no tenant field on `LocationProfile`, none on `Store`, none on `SessionContext`; `create_edc_terminal` (`db/edc_terminals.rs:193`) - **zero production callers**, so a parameter there is an unexercised shape and not a fix; `seed_primary_store` (`apps/desktop-tauri/src/state.rs:410`) - runs during startup before any session, licence or claim is in scope, and seeds the fixed `id = 'default'` row.
  - **And stamping them would CONTRADICT the design, not complete it.** `db/mod.rs:508-516` documents the desktop store database as single-tenant by construction and `check_tenant_integrity` **refuses to boot** on a foreign-tenant row. A desktop install that stamped a real tenant on some rows and not others would be a boot failure.
  - **A comment that asserts a mechanism which does not exist:** `crates/kasirmu-bridge/src/locations.rs:277` is documented as resolving "for the session's tenant (ADR #7)" while its actual tenant read at `:230` is the literal "default". That is the review's own thesis in one line, and it is worth keeping visible.
  - **The real defect is on the cloud side, and it is worse than a missing stamp.** `apps/cloud-server/src/quota_detector.rs:336` counts `locations WHERE tenant_id = $1` and the popularity roll-up (`email_pg/popularity.rs:230-236`) does the same, against rows that carry 'default' - so a paying tenant scores 0 locations and the roll-up reports no data forever. **CORRECTED by C36 (commit-less investigation, see D10): the impact is an alert that can never fire, NOT a denied quota** - `QuotaDimensionKind::Locations` / `CONDITION_LOCATIONS_OVER_QUOTA` is an alerting path, and the earlier wording in this file inferred a blocking one. The number is structurally zero for every tenant because `locations` has no PostgreSQL writer at all.
  - **AND those tables never reach PostgreSQL at all.** `locations`, `edc_terminals`, `product_activity`, `product_variants`, `bundle_items`, `product_taxes` and `product_bundles` appear in **neither** the sync action vocabulary (`platform/sync/src/queue.rs:450-457`) **nor** the PG copy surface (`apps/cloud-server/src/bin/migrate_sqlite_to_pg/main.rs:72-89`). So the detector is most likely counting an empty table, which makes its number structurally zero rather than merely wrong, and the tenant stamp is not even the first problem. The tenant IS already applied at ingest for the tables that do sync (`apps/cloud-server/src/sync_store/pg.rs:39-45`, `:76-87`, from the JWT claim at `sync_api.rs:269`) - so the pattern exists and seven tables were simply never added to it. C36 establishes which of the two truths is the real one before anything is changed.
  **Gate: owner decision D9** (manager-codebase-review-decisions.md) - is the desktop store DB single-tenant by construction, so the tenant belongs at the cloud ingest boundary? The recommendation is A: yes, and apply the tenant where it is actually known (`sync_terminals.tenant_id`), rather than inventing one on the terminal.
  **The FK-ordering half is the ONLY part that remains live on the desktop path, and it is confined to `product_variants`, `bundle_items`, `product_taxes`, `product_bundles`** - the four tables carrying a composite FK to `products(tenant_id, sku)`. Slices C32-S2's fence must therefore include `db/products_crud.rs` so the parent INSERT carries the tenant in the same transaction, or the child insert fails outright under `foreign_keys = ON`.
  Done when (re-scoped): `provisioning.rs` stamps the supplied tenant on the linked path and the schema default on the local path, each with a test; D9 is answered and the cloud-side read is fixed where the tenant is actually known; and every table left in `RLS_TABLES` either has a write path that demonstrably sets `tenant_id` or is moved to `RLS_EXEMPT` with a reason (the C33/C33b route).

- [x] **C36 [P0] The locations quota axis is structurally inert** - **INVESTIGATED AND PINNED 2026-09-23, commit 5bd4a15b4** (`test(cloud): pin the inert locations quota axis`; 2 files, +220) - **decision D10 gates the fix; this item lands only the measurement.** Three corrections to the item as first written: (1) the detector counts **three** axes, not seven - only `locations`, `products`, `users`; the other six tables appear zero times in it; (2) in cloud the **SQLite** detector is dead code (the `_pg` loop runs, `count_tenant_locations_pg` is the live query); (3) the impact is an **alert that can never fire**, NOT a denied quota - the earlier wording in this file inferred a blocking path and was wrong. With `max_locations()` at `Some(1)`/`Some(2)`/`Some(5)`, `0 >= 1` is false, so `CONDITION_LOCATIONS_OVER_QUOTA` can never fire - even for an operator running forty stores. The contrast is what makes it a finding: `products` and `users` DO reach PG (`pg.rs:868,1430`), so those two axes are live; `locations` is the only counted axis with no PG writer and no copy entry. **The pin is two tests, and the second was strengthened mid-flight for a good reason:** `pg_integration_locations_axis_counts_zero_on_the_cloud` first asserted `count == 0` for a fresh tenant, which the worker recognised as VACUOUS (a fresh tenant returns 0 even if a writer existed); it now seeds a PG location row and asserts the query returns **1** (proving the query is not broken), then asserts a tenant with no row returns **0** - separating "the query is broken" from "nothing writes the row", which is the actual finding. `cargo test -p kasirmu-cloud quota_detector` -> **15 passed, 0 failed**.

The C36 worker proved the PG test actually RAN rather than skipping: a control run against a dead port (`127.0.0.1:15999`) printed `PG integration test skipped: ...` and took 40s, while the real run printed no skip and took 2.1s, with `grep -ciE 'skipped|panicked'` over the committed run = 0. **This means the C7 circuit-breaker's stated root cause - "this environment has no PostgreSQL" - was FALSE**, which is recorded as manager error #7 and re-opens the C7 live-verification question below.
  **Honest caveats the worker stated itself, kept verbatim:** the structural pin scans **two named files**, so a writer added elsewhere (a new module, a migration) would not trip it - it catches the realistic regression (someone extends `DEFAULT_TABLES` or the sync vocabulary) but is not a whole-repo proof; and the PG test is **not hermetic** - it inserts a pid-unique `locations` row into the shared base database and deletes it in the same test, so a crash mid-test could leave one stray row (worth converting to the throwaway-DB pattern the crate's other tests use if it ever flakes).

- [x] **C34 [P0] NEW, found while deciding D9: the embedded local API can brick the next desktop boot.** - **DONE 2026-09-23, commit 669d3d109** (`fix(local-api): refuse a write whose tenant claim would brick the next boot`; `crates/kasirmu-local-api/src/lib.rs` +125, `lib_tests.rs` +244). **VERDICT: REACHABLE, but operator-credential-gated** - the honest severity, and the chain matters: the embedded `AppState` sets `allow_terminal_credentials = false`, so the client-credentials path is closed (400 `terminal_credentials_disabled`), and the UI's own mint is safe (`mint_token` passes `tenant_id = None`, resolving to `"default"`); **but the same router serves `POST /api/v1/tokens` publicly** (nested in the `public` group, no JWT), gated only by the per-install secret, and its admin-gated label mint passes `body.tenant_id` straight from the request body into `create_token_full`. That token validates against the same secret, so `POST /api/v1/products` then stamps a foreign tenant onto a row, and `check_tenant_integrity` refuses the next launch. Not unauthenticated and not internet-facing (loopback bind, off by default), but the per-install secret is one the operator is expected to hand to local scripts AND it doubles as the admin key - so a curl carrying a body field can brick the next boot. **Fix: ONE guard at the boundary, not three at the stamps** - a `reject_foreign_tenant_writes` layer applied outermost in `start_with_audit`, refusing WRITES only (GET/HEAD/OPTIONS keep exact behaviour) when a valid token's `tenant_id` is present and not `"default"`, returning 403 `foreign_tenant_write`. Empty string is refused too, because `unwrap_or("default")` maps only `None`, so `Some("")` would stamp `tenant_id = ''` - which the boot check rejects exactly like a named foreign tenant. It deliberately did NOT patch `products.rs`, because a stamp-level fix would have left `users.rs:163` - also boot-checked - open: the 'three guards vs one' trap the brief named. And because the guard is outermost and would otherwise short-circuit past the router's security headers, it re-applied the same public `security_headers_middleware` rather than copying a header list. Cloud behaviour untouched. **Evidence:** `cargo test -p kasirmu-local-api` -> 17 passed, 0 failed (4 new); `cargo test -p kasirmu-api --lib products` -> 13 passed; `cargo test -p kasirmu-core tenant_integrity` -> 6 passed (`check_tenant_integrity` untouched and still refusing); fmt clean. **Falsification proved:** short-circuiting the guard makes the two refusal tests FAIL while the read-tolerance test and the integrity test stay green, and `lib.rs` was restored byte-identically (blob `ca2e9909a49de4f5d162b83f9bb328c5263053db` before and after). **Residual, filed as C39:** the deeper fix is to stop the embedded `POST /api/v1/tokens` accepting a caller-supplied `tenant_id` at all; the boundary guard makes it harmless on this surface, but the field is still accepted there. Also noted: `tax_rates.rs:296-298` shares the stamp pattern and is NOT boot-checked.
  ORIGINAL BRIEF: `crates/kasirmu-local-api/src/lib.rs:153-169` hands the **store** database to `kasirmu_api::AppState`, and `crates/kasirmu-api/src/routes/products.rs:306-309` stamps `claims.tenant_id` (from the JWT, `unwrap_or("default")`) straight onto a `products` row. But `Store::check_tenant_integrity` (`crates/kasirmu-core/src/db/mod.rs:526-553`) **refuses to boot** when any `products`/`users` row carries a non-default `tenant_id`, and it runs on every launch (`apps/desktop-tauri/src/state.rs:247`, `apps/mobile-tauri/src/state.rs:188`). So if any caller of the embedded local API ever presents a token with a non-default tenant, the next launch of that store fails to start - a **remote way to brick a desktop install**, on the path that Pattern A was assumed to be safe for.
  Fence: crates/kasirmu-local-api/src/lib.rs, crates/kasirmu-api/src/routes/products.rs (the stamp region :295-316 only), plus their sibling test files. Do not change `check_tenant_integrity` and do not change the cloud behaviour of the route - the question is what the EMBEDDED surface should do.
  **First prove reachability, then fix, and report both.** Establish whether the embedded local API can actually mint or accept a non-default tenant claim at all (trace where its tokens come from and who signs them). If it cannot, say so with evidence and this becomes a one-line defensive ticket rather than an incident. If it can, the fix is at the boundary where the two contracts meet.
  Note the same stamp at `crates/kasirmu-api/src/routes/tax_rates.rs:296-298` and `routes/users.rs:162-164` - check whether they share the exposure before choosing where to put the guard, and prefer ONE guard over three.
  Done when: a test demonstrates the outcome for the embedded surface with a non-default claim (refused at the stamp, or the row is left alone), and `check_tenant_integrity` still refuses a genuinely foreign-tenant store DB.

Two design points worth keeping: (a) the ported predicate tests for a whole path COMPONENT (`part == "__tests__"`), never a substring, so `ui/src/foo__tests__bar/Screen.tsx` remains production source (that path is an ILLUSTRATIVE EXAMPLE and has never existed — it is not a citation; the marker words keep the dead-ref checker from reading it as one) - and a substring-guard assertion stops the tolerance being widened by accident; (b) the worker MEASURED the breadth rather than assuming it - 128 computed `invoke` sites under `ui/src/__tests__` (tolerated) against 19 in production `ui/src` (still adjudicated) - and grounded the rule in `scripts/verify-ipc-parity.py`, whose walks already drop `__tests__` paths (`:149`, `:274`) so the parity surface is production source only. So the mobile gate is simply the older copy that never received the tolerance, and it fails on a `ui/` test file it was never taught about. The offender is pre-existing and unrelated: it arrived in `e2e4921f7`, both of the gate's inputs are byte-identical to the commit before the landing, and nothing in the landing touched `ui/`.

If the desktop predicate turns out to be too broad to be safe, say so and propose the tighter one, but do not widen the mobile gate's `allowed` list to make it green.
