- [x] **C10b [P0, RE-SCOPED BY D11] Put a backstop on negative stock WITHOUT killing `allow_negative_stock`.** - **DONE 2026-09-23, commit 948094c34** (`feat(core): enforce the negative-stock backstop conditionally on the binding opt-in`; 6 files, +415/-11, exactly the widened fence with one `create mode`). The trigger WAS expressible, and the load-bearing finding is that **the literal predicate from the brief was wrong**: `NEW.qty < 0 AND NOT EXISTS (a binding with allow = 1)` refuses `deactivate_inventory_location_with_negative_stock_errors`, which seeds `-3` at a location created by `create_inventory_location` that writes **no binding at all** - so `NOT EXISTS` is true and the trigger fires on a case that must succeed. The honest predicate is `NEW.qty < 0 AND EXISTS (a binding for this location) AND NOT EXISTS (a binding for this location with allow_negative_stock = 1)`: the flag is a per-**binding** opt-out, so an unbound location has no opt-out to violate, and Rust Layer 1 already refuses negatives on that path (`adjust.rs:216-224`, where `allow_negative` can only become true *via a binding*), so no race is left open. Both the predicate and the refinement are documented in the migration header and pinned by test. **ACCEPTANCE, BOTH DIRECTIONS:** (a) the two previously-failing tests pass again, untouched - `cargo test -p kasirmu-core negative_stock` -> 4 passed; (b) a NEW raw-SQL test proves the TRIGGER bites - `stock_summary_negative_guard_is_conditional_on_the_binding` drives `INSERT` and `UPDATE` directly (the UPDATE arm is what `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` fires once the row exists, the shape both writers use), refused at a bound non-opted-in location, accepted at an opted-in binding **through its upsert**, accepted at an unbound location; plus `stock_summary_negative_guard_is_not_an_unconditional_check`, which reads `sqlite_master` and asserts the table carries **no** `qty >= 0` and no quarantine table - so a future "simplification" back to the blanket CHECK fails in CI rather than in production. **Falsification proved:** neutering the predicate (`WHEN NEW.qty < 0 AND 0`) fails the new test, restored green. **PG twin:** two `TRIGGER_MAP` entries (one shared plpgsql function + INSERT and UPDATE triggers - Postgres needs one function where SQLite needs two triggers, noted in the map comment); regenerated, `--check` exit 0, `.pg.sql` +28 lines all ours. Trigger census pin moved 6 -> 8; the table (127) and index (189) pins unchanged, since the migration adds neither. **What the guard guarantees, stated by the worker:** it constrains the **materialised rollup**, never the ledger - `stock_movements` stays append-only and unconstrained, so the two can still drift, which is what C12's report exists to surface. And per D11 **no existing row is touched**: a trigger fires on writes only, so every negative already in a store survives. **Its own honest gap, filed as C42:** no test EXECUTES the plpgsql port - the ports are hand-written, so their correctness currently rests on the generator's fail-closed parity check (dumped-trigger set vs `TRIGGER_MAP`) plus a line-for-line resemblance to the tested SQLite predicate.
  ORIGINAL BRIEF: A worker implemented the original brief exactly as written - quarantine negatives, rebuild `stock_summary` carrying `CHECK (qty >= 0)`, recreate the index, register the migration - and ran it. Two EXISTING, PASSING tests failed: `db::products::tests::negative_stock_event_fires_when_allow_negative_enabled` (products_tests.rs:2187) and `db::inventory::tests::deactivate_inventory_location_with_negative_stock_errors` (inventory_tests.rs:250, `CHECK constraint failed: qty >= 0`). They are not stale. `allow_negative_stock` is first-class and shipped: `adjust.rs:208-225` writes below zero deliberately, the CHECK reaches that same upsert at `:251`, the handler at `:258-267` turns the `ConstraintViolation` into `InsufficientStockAtLocation` - i.e. the constraint **silently re-enables the guard the flag exists to opt out of** - `sales_lifecycle.rs:279-303` and `products_stock_adjust/batch.rs:78-88` read the same flag, `LocationPicker.tsx:429` renders a "negative stock allowed" badge, `StockShortfallDialog.tsx` lets a cashier override per transaction, and **ADR #17 (`docs/decisions/2026-07-18-multi-location-inventory.md:476-477`) specifies the whole behaviour.** The review was right about the dead code and WRONG about the fix.
  **So the work is now: the CONDITIONAL guard, plus the two dead comments.** 1. A trigger that refuses a negative only when the binding has NOT opted in - `WHEN NEW.qty < 0 AND NOT EXISTS (a binding with allow_negative_stock = 1)`. 2. Fix the two comments at `adjust.rs:31` and `:144` that claim a Layer 2 guard which does not exist - that dead claim IS the documentation defect the review actually found.
  Fence (WIDENED by the D11 decision, because every literal route needs files the original fence excluded): a NEW migration under `crates/kasirmu-core/migrations/`, `crates/kasirmu-core/src/migrations.rs`, `crates/kasirmu-core/src/migrations_tests.rs`, `crates/kasirmu-core/src/db/products_stock_adjust/adjust.rs` (COMMENTS ONLY - do not change the write path; **PATH CORRECTED**: this brief first said `src/db/products/adjust.rs`, which does not exist - the real file is `products_stock_adjust/adjust.rs`, whose `:31`/`:144` carry the dead claims), `scripts/generate-pg-migration.py` (a `TRIGGER_MAP` entry - the generator's own header at `:28-30` and `:131` documents that a conditional constraint needs a hand-written plpgsql port), and the regenerated `crates/kasirmu-core/migrations/20260813_init.pg.sql`.
  **Acceptance, and it must prove BOTH directions:** the two existing tests above must still PASS (an opted-in binding may go negative), AND a new test must show a binding that has NOT opted in is REFUSED at the database level by RAW SQL (so it proves the trigger, not the Rust guard). The quarantine table and the zeroing are optional here - the worker's prepared body is recoverable if the owner ever picks the other branch, but under D11 the existing negatives are legitimate oversells and should NOT be blanket-repaired.
  Do NOT delete or weaken the two failing tests to make a constraint pass. They are the only tests pinning a shipped feature, and weakening them was the failure mode this workstream has otherwise avoided.

- [x] **C39 [P0] NEW, residual of C34: the embedded surface still ACCEPTED a caller-supplied `tenant_id`.** - **DONE 2026-09-23, commit ebddcbfcb** (`fix(api): refuse a tenant-scoped token mint on the single-tenant surface`). C34's boundary guard made a foreign-tenant write harmless, but the field was still accepted: `POST /api/v1/tokens` passes `body.tenant_id` straight into `create_token_full` (`crates/kasirmu-api/src/routes/tokens.rs:268-275`). This closes the trap rather than fencing it. The hard constraint held: the **cloud** surface must keep accepting the field, because that is how a tenant's token gets its tenant (`routes/tokens.rs:219`, minting from the registered `sync_terminals` row), so the fix is surface-discriminating rather than a deletion. **The choice was REFUSE, not drop, and the reason is worth keeping:** dropping the claim would mint a token whose scope silently differs from what the caller asked for - a script sending `tenant_id: "acme"` would get a `default`-scoped token and believe it held acme scope, so every later request would be judged against a tenant the caller never agreed to. That is a silent authority mismatch, worse than an error because nothing surfaces it. Refusal names the problem at the only moment the caller can act, and matches the shape this surface already uses for its sibling restriction (`terminal_credentials_disabled`, 400). Status 400, error `tenant_claim_not_supported`. **Mechanism: a `SingleTenantSurface` request marker** declared in `routes/tokens.rs` and inserted by the embedder's own layer, extracted as `Option<Extension<...>>` - so the CLOUD server never inserts it and is untouched **by construction** rather than by a branch that could drift. The worker chose an extension over a second `AppState` boolean deliberately: `AppState` is built as a struct literal by ~20 sites, nearly all of them test files outside the change, so a new field would have forced a sweep of files this change had no business touching; an extension is inserted by the embedder and cannot be forged by an HTTP client, unlike a sentinel header. Cloud-side change was reflexively additive: one public marker type, one optional extractor parameter, one guarded refusal placed AFTER `admin_key_authorised` and the client-credentials branch and BEFORE `resolve_read_permissions`. **Behaviour table:** embedded + foreign tenant 200 -> **400**; embedded + none/`default` 200 -> 200; cloud + foreign tenant 200 -> **200 unchanged**; embedded terminal client-credentials unchanged. **Evidence:** `cargo test -p kasirmu-local-api` -> 18 passed (the four C34 tests still pass unchanged, plus a new end-to-end mint refusal); `cargo test -p kasirmu-api --lib tokens` -> 26 passed (4 new, including cloud-still-accepts-and-the-claim-reaches-the-token); fmt clean. **Falsification:** removing the marker insert from the embedder layer fails the new test at its 400 assertion, and both files were restored byte-identically.

- [x] **C40 [P2] NEW, found when a C17 slice was mis-targeted: the tablet has no backup-status command** - **DONE 2026-09-23, commit 44c06dc55** (`ui/src/features/settings/hooks/useBackupStatus.ts` + `ui/src/__tests__/DataManagementBackup.test.tsx`, 155 insertions / 4 deletions, no Rust file touched). **VERDICT: REACHED AND SILENTLY FAILING, then swallowed** - worse than dead code, exactly as the brief suspected. Proved rather than assumed: the branch is LIVE (`useBackupStatus` is called by `DataManagementScreen.tsx:83`, registered as the `data-management` page at `features/settings/register.tsx:31`, and that page is a TABLET nav item - the `admin` workspace seeds `('admin','data-management',8)` at `migrations/20260813_init.sql:1454`, `TabletAppShell.tsx:397-424` renders it via `getPage(currentRoute)`, and `WorkspaceHome.tsx:458` switches to the `admin` workspace on tool navigation), and NOTHING GUARDS IT (`isTabletShell()` is used only inside `handleBackup` at `:97`; the mount effect has no shell check, so the ternary runs and picks `getBackupStatusScoped` or `getBackupStatus`). Both names are desktop-only, so an unregistered invoke is rejected by the IPC layer and `.catch()` at `:76-82` swallows it into a toast plus `lastBackup: undefined` - **so the operator got a spurious error toast on every mount of the Data tab and a permanently failed panel.** **The fix refused to add a tablet command, for a reason worth keeping:** the bridge's status body (`kasirmu-bridge/src/data.rs:414-431`) reads `default_backup_path` = `<db>.backup.db` beside the database, which on Android is private storage the tablet never writes - the tablet backs up to an operator-chosen destination via `create_backup_to`. Such a command would answer `lastBackup: null` and the UI would render **"Never"** for a store that HAS backed up, which is the precise compliance claim `BackupSection.tsx:73-77`'s three-way render exists to refuse. So the honest answer is that the tablet has NO readable backup status, and the fix holds `lastBackup` at `undefined` - "never answered", not "Never" - reusing the file's existing `isTabletShell()` owner rather than adding a second mechanism. **Evidence:** `npx vitest run` on that suite -> 17 passed (was 14); MUTATION PROOF - reverting the guard to the old ternary fails exactly the 3 new assertions, restored -> 17 green; a fourth case pins that tablet backup still goes through `create_backup_to`. 362 passed across the 7 neighbouring suites sharing the hook/api, and `npm run typecheck` exit 0. **Its own stated gap became C41.**

  ORIGINAL BRIEF:, yet the shared Settings screen calls for one.** `ui/src/features/settings/hooks/useBackupStatus.ts:65-67` calls `getBackupStatus()` / `getBackupStatusScoped()` on the null-session-token path. Those are **desktop-registered** names: `apps/desktop-tauri/src/commands/data.rs:33` defines `get_backup_status` and `apps/desktop-tauri/src/lib.rs:964` registers it, while `apps/mobile-tauri` has **zero** occurrences of either name outside prose comments and no backup-status read at all (`git grep -nE 'get_backup_status_scoped|create_backup_scoped' -- apps/mobile-tauri/` returns comments only). So on the tablet shell that fallback has no registered command to reach, and whether it is dead code or a silently failing call is UNVERIFIED - the finding is the gap, not a verdict on it.
  Fence: ui/src/features/settings/hooks/useBackupStatus.ts and its test; `apps/mobile-tauri/src/commands/data.rs` + the registration lines in its `lib.rs` ONLY if the honest fix is to give the tablet a scoped status read. Nothing else.
  Done when: either the fallback is shell-aware and never asks for a desktop-only command, or the tablet has a real scoped status command and the hook uses it - and the chosen path is asserted by a test that fails if the call would reach an unregistered name. Do not simply delete the fallback without checking what the Settings screen shows when the status read is unavailable.

- [x] **C38 [P1] NEW, found while landing C18 P1.3b: PostgreSQL gets the index WITHOUT the reconciliation that makes it safe.** - **DONE 2026-09-23, commit 259c4eec9** (`fix(pg): emit the open-shift reconciliation before its unique index`; `apps/cloud-server/tests/pg_init_reconciliation.rs` new 228, `scripts/generate-pg-migration.py` +199, regenerated `init.pg.sql` +32). **MECHANISM (b), an explicit `PRE_INDEX_RECONCILIATIONS` list - declared, emitted and ENFORCED - and the worker checked (a) before rejecting it:** the generator CANNOT carry the data statement because it never sees it - it rebuilds PG from the FINAL SQLite state (tables, indexes, triggers, seeds) and replays only RENAMEs as evolution ops, so the `UPDATE` is DROPPED, not mis-translated; generic SQLite-DML translation is intractable (`strftime`, `GLOB`, row-value comparisons all differ) and a half-working translation fails exactly the way this fix exists to prevent. The list mirrors the `TRIGGER_MAP`/`TRIGGER_VERIFICATION` discipline so the twin is VERIFIED rather than described: `index_name` must name an index the generator actually emits or generation fails (so a stale entry cannot emit a statement nothing depends on), `sql` is emitted inside a `to_regclass('public.shifts')` guard so a fresh database is a clean no-op, `verified_by` must name an existing test file, and `body_sha256` pins the statement. **Emission order is the whole point: the block lands at line 1380, the index at 2593.** **Acceptance, with the honest limit handled properly:** a fresh-init test asserts the index exists, and the worker explicitly refused to claim a no-op as evidence that the reconciliation ran; the STRONG form is the seeded case - two open shifts for one user, then `PG_INIT` succeeds, the index is created, the OLDER row is `closed` with the `auto-closed: duplicate open shift (COR-27 reconciliation)` note and `shift-new` survives. **Falsified by neutering the emit loop** (0 `auto-closed` occurrences): the test then FAILED with `could not create unique index "idx_shifts_open_per_user"`, `Key (user_id)=(user-dup) is duplicated`, SQLSTATE **E23505** - the exact startup failure C38 describes, reproduced rather than described. Restored to 2 occurrences, `--check` exit 0. `--self-test` now prints **three** gates ok (RLS, trigger, pre-index), and both new enforcement directions bite on the real tree (digest mismatch exit 1 with declared-vs-actual hashes; stale index name exit 1). C42 (1 PROVEN) and C44 (6 PROVEN) both still pass. Reachability control in house form: live 2 PROVEN / 0 SKIP, dead port 0 PROVEN / 2 SKIP.
  **AND IT REPORTED A BIGGER FINDING RATHER THAN SWEEPING IT, as instructed: the generator emits ZERO `UPDATE` statements while SEVEN migrations carry one** - see C46. C38 was the only one that bricks init; the other six leave legacy rows un-backfilled on a pre-existing PG database.
  ORIGINAL BRIEF: The SQLite migration reconciles before it constrains (see C18 P1.3b), but the generator has no PG port for that `UPDATE`: `grep -c auto-closed crates/kasirmu-core/migrations/20260813_init.pg.sql` returns **0** while the migration returns 2. So `init.pg.sql` receives a bare `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_shifts_open_per_user ...` with no preceding repair. On any PostgreSQL database that already holds two open shifts for one user, that statement fails at init.
  **How reachable is it?** The landing worker verified there is NO PG-side writer of `shifts` anywhere in the repo - no `INSERT INTO shifts`/`UPDATE shifts` in `apps/cloud-server` or `crates/kasirmu-api`; every writer is `rusqlite` on the device. So the application cannot create that state. That is evidence, not proof about live data, and the PG side has no guard against a state created by any other means (a console session, a migration, a repair script - the same argument the SQLite side used to justify moving the guard into the schema).
  Fence: scripts/generate-pg-migration.py (a `TRIGGER_MAP`-style port or an explicit reconciliation statement emitted before the index) plus the regenerated `20260813_init.pg.sql`. Coordinate with any session holding a dirty generator - this file's working tree is a shared hazard.
  Done when: either the PG init carries an equivalent reconciliation before the index (preferred, and consistent with how the SQLite side handled it), or a record states with evidence why PG cannot reach the state and the index's `IF NOT EXISTS` is therefore sufficient. Do NOT add `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` or touch the RLS lists - that is D2 and C33's territory.

- [x] **C43 [P0] NEW, and it is the most transferable finding of the remediation: the generator's TRIGGER_MAP check compares NAMES ONLY, never semantics.** - **DONE 2026-09-23, commit 74a9bce4e** (`scripts/generate-pg-migration.py` +193/-1, the only file committed; the generated PG file is content-identical to HEAD so it was correctly left untouched). **The worker strengthened option (a) because option (a) AS SPECIFIED COULD NOT MEET ITS OWN ACCEPTANCE CRITERION, and it checked before building:** the rule 'declare the live test that exercises it' would have left the C42 shape passing, because in that shape the body is mutated while the key is unchanged, so the `verified_by` path still exists and still resolves. Naming a test proves a test is ASSOCIATED, not that it passes or that the body is unchanged. **So the enforceable rule is (a) plus a body digest:** `verified_by` (a test path resolved from repo root, which must EXIST - a port cannot be added without naming its evidence) and `body_sha256` (sha256 of the newline-normalized port body - ANY body edit without re-recording the digest fails generation). That is an exact mechanical change detector, not a heuristic and not a plpgsql parser: it cannot verify semantics, but it makes a silent divergence **impossible to ship**, because the author must re-pin the digest, which is an explicit reviewable act the gate's own error message prompts. **Before/after, five scenarios with exit codes:** (1) the C42 shape - mutated body, unchanged key - went from `BEFORE_EXIT=0` ('ok: matches the generator') to `GEN_EXIT=1` / `AFTER_EXIT=1` with `error: trigger verification failed (the membership check proves presence only, never semantics): - stock_summary_qty_nonnegative_insert: body changed (declared de2dbc9691b7, actual 74a0e52abb38) - re-record body_sha256...`; (2) the real tree still `CHECK_EXIT=0`; (3) hand-editing the PG file with TRIGGER_MAP untouched is STILL caught (`HAND_EDIT_EXIT=1`, no regression); (4) `--self-test` now exercises SIX fail directions (undeclared port, port missing from the table, stale entry, blank `verified_by`, missing test file, digest mismatch) plus the passing direction - fail-closed both ways, matching the RLS gate's discipline; (5) a `verified_by` naming a missing file fails with the path named. Every mutation was reverted and re-verified. **Limits stated by the worker rather than implied away:** no semantic comparison exists and none was added - a re-pinned digest on a wrong port still ships green, so the gate converts SILENT divergence into EXPLICIT divergence and that is all, stated in the file header and on the table; `verified_by` is only as strong as the named test; the digest must be re-pinned by hand, which is intended friction but means a careless 'just update the hash' defeats the gate; and `--self-test` is not wired into CI (only `--check` is). **Its most valuable admission became item C44:** of the seven ports, only `stock_summary_qty_nonnegative_*` names a test that EXECUTES the plpgsql against real PostgreSQL; the other SIX name SQLite-side tests - compensating controls, not PG execution.
  ORIGINAL BRIEF: Found by the C42 worker, and PROVED rather than inferred: it mutated a `TRIGGER_MAP` body to a divergent predicate **while keeping the key**, regenerated, and `python3 scripts/generate-pg-migration.py --check` printed `ok: ... matches the generator` with **exit 0**. The code (`scripts/generate-pg-migration.py:972-983`) selects only `name` from `sqlite_master` and only compares membership in each direction:
  ```python
  dumped_triggers = [r[0] for r in db.execute("SELECT name FROM sqlite_master WHERE type='trigger' ORDER BY name")]
  missing = [t for t in dumped_triggers if t not in TRIGGER_MAP]
  stale   = [t for t in TRIGGER_MAP if t not in dumped_triggers]
  ```
  It never reads the trigger body, and never compares the SQLite `WHEN` clause against the plpgsql `IF`. **So before C42's live test existed, a WRONG plpgsql port would have shipped green through every existing gate.** The same worker also demonstrated the guard's real scope: hand-editing the generated PG file WITHOUT touching `TRIGGER_MAP` IS caught (`--check` exit 1, '535 differing lines ... port the plpgsql in TRIGGER_MAP first'), so **the guard protects the file, not the predicate.**
  Why this is P0 rather than hygiene: `TRIGGER_MAP` is the mechanism by which EVERY conditional constraint crosses into PostgreSQL, and there are now two hand-written ports in it. A name-only check makes 'the PG twin exists' feel verified while the only thing verified is that it exists.
  Fence: `scripts/generate-pg-migration.py` (the check and its comment/message only) plus the generated file if the change alters output. Do NOT change any trigger body and do NOT change the SQLite migrations.
  **Done when, and prefer an enforceable rule over a clever comparison** - comparing plpgsql to SQL semantics in general is not tractable, so the honest options are: require each `TRIGGER_MAP` entry to DECLARE the test that exercises it live (a `verified_by` field, and generation FAILS when a trigger has no such declaration or the named test does not exist), or state the limitation in the check's own message and in the file header with the C42 test named as the compensating control. - [x] **C44 [P1] NEW, the C43 worker's own admission: six of the seven TRIGGER_MAP ports are verified by SQLite-side tests only.** - **DONE 2026-09-23, commit 7c7bbbe1c** (`apps/cloud-server/tests/pg_trigger_ports.rs` new, 514 lines; `scripts/generate-pg-migration.py`; regenerated `20260813_init.pg.sql`). **A REAL DIVERGENCE WAS FOUND, AND IT WAS A SILENT COMPLIANCE FAILURE:** `audit_log_immutable_delete` ended with `RETURN NULL`, and on a row-level **`BEFORE DELETE`** trigger returning `NULL` **CANCELS THE DELETE**. So six ports were written by hand to mirror a SQLite trigger, are pinned by name and now by body digest, and have **never been run in PostgreSQL at all**. The C42 shape applied six more times is a real, unclaimed gap, and the worker wrote it on the table rather than letting a green gate imply otherwise.

Do NOT change any trigger body or the SQLite migrations - a divergence found here is a finding to report and fix in the PORT, never to reconcile by editing the tested SQLite half.

The acceptance is the same for each: the port is EXECUTED against real PostgreSQL, in both directions where the trigger is conditional, in a throwaway database that never touches the shared schema, roles, RLS or `rls-cutover.sql`, with a PROVEN line on success and a SKIP line when the server is unreachable - so a green run is shown to have run.

A permanent self-test (case 24, 5 assertions) finds the shape, names file+line, CLEARS it when the call is guarded (differential, not constant), excludes a non-branching file, and refuses to treat an `import` of a wrapper as a call. **Its stated limits:** a file that never branches on the discriminator is not examined (7 of ~470 runtime files); the guard is syntactic so an indirectly-expressed guard is a false positive; only names already in a shell's allowlist section are graded. **And it disclosed a side effect it did not intend:** its `json.dumps` round-trip also removed three `dev_mock` entries (`link_device_google`, `link_device_email_request`, `link_device_email_consume`) that were ALREADY failing at HEAD because another lane's commit `27a2bc40e` added the handlers - removing them is what took the tree from exit 1 to exit 0, but it edits a section another lane owns, and if that lane intended to keep them the correct fix is theirs. **That disclosure is cross-lane information I am carrying forward rather than burying.** Its finding produced C47 (the tablet LICENSE screen, 3 of the 9) and it left a PRE-EXISTING self-test failure alone after verifying it fails on unmodified HEAD too.

The parity surface therefore reported satisfaction, the tablet gap was invisible, and the runtime consequence was a spurious error toast on every mount of the Data tab plus a permanently failed panel.

- [x] **C49 [P1] NEW, found by C20: the queue-ordering rule now exists in FOUR places, and the shared home for all of them is `kasirmu_core::offline`.** - **DONE 2026-09-23, commit 95f77a322** (`refactor(core): collapse the queue-ordering rule into one shared expression`; 8 files, +275/-178). **THE COUNT WAS FIVE, NOT FOUR - my brief missed one:** `crates/kasirmu-bridge/src/offline.rs:355`, the desktop `retry_offline_sync_scoped` path. Full census before -> after: `platform/sync/src/lib.rs:157` (priority-only) -> shared; `platform/sync/src/daemon.rs` `sort_pending_for_push` -> shared; `crates/kasirmu-bridge/src/sync.rs` `order_pending_for_push` -> shared; `apps/mobile-tauri/src/commands/offline.rs:342` (tablet) -> shared; and **`crates/kasirmu-bridge/src/offline.rs:355` STILL A COPY, outside the fence** - filed as C49b. One test-site copy also collapsed (`db/offline_tests.rs:510`). **The one shared expression is `kasirmu_core::offline::order_for_push`**, and `grep` for priority sorts now returns exactly ONE hit: the out-of-fence fifth copy. **THE DETERMINISM TEST IS THE ONE THIS ITEM EXISTED FOR:** it seeds **12 items sharing ONE priority tier and ONE millisecond**, so `priority` and `created_at` compare equal for every pair and only `id` can decide, then asserts the same order across **12 independent rotations** of that set - asserting *'the same permutation every time'* rather than a hard-coded one. That is precisely the case a priority-only sort gets wrong. The key is preserved exactly: **`(priority, created_at, id)` ascending**, reordering the caller's `&mut` slice IN PLACE, because a parallel sorted copy would mis-attribute every outcome - the push and the per-item apply iterate the same vector. **C20's NEGATIVE CONTROL RE-PROVEN AFTER THE REFACTOR:** commenting out the sort on the daemon still yields `left: ["bulk", "money", "catalog"]`, the Low item leading a Critical one. **AND IT CLOSED C20's OWN CALL-SITE GAP, which C20 had reported it could not afford:** the primitives already existed (`TestBridge::token_granting` for a real `sync:manage` grant, `TestBridge::ctx()`, and the tablet's raw-`TcpListener` fake-server pattern), so no mock-HTTP crate was needed - which mattered because `kasirmu-bridge` has no dev-dependency on one. The new test asserts the **order of the HTTP request body**, and deleting `order_for_push(&mut pending_items)` fails it with `left: ["bulk", "money", "catalog"]`. The three old comparator tests were DELETED rather than duplicated - the comparator is now tested once in core - and `kasirmu-bridge` went 1385 -> 1383 tests for exactly that reason. **Verify, all four targets green:** `cargo test -p kasirmu-core offline` exit 0; `cargo test -p platform-sync` **427 passed / 0 failed**; `cargo test -p kasirmu-bridge` **1383 passed / 0 failed** (~758s, background job); `cargo test -p kasirmu-mobile offline` **20 passed / 0 failed**. **Two honest gaps kept:** the TABLET call site has no call-site test (its command needs `AppState`, and the only harness that builds one for sync lives in `commands/sync_tests.rs`, outside the fence and covering a different command) - so the tablet is wired to the shared helper and compiles, and it is the same one-line shape as the bridge's now-proven call site, but that is inference rather than a test; and the fifth copy means 'one expression called by all paths' is not yet literally true, only 'one expression called by every path inside the fence'.
  ORIGINAL BRIEF: Counting what C20 DISCOVERED rather than what it created: `platform/sync/src/lib.rs:150` `build_batches` already sorted by priority and the third path (`SyncEngine`) already used it - **that path was correct and was left alone** - but it is private to the batching loop, it is **priority-only so NOT total**, and `crates/kasirmu-bridge` **cannot reach it**: `crates/kasirmu-bridge/src/sync.rs:6` states verbatim that `platform-sync` is 'a crate this one does not depend on', confirmed by the manifest (`platform-core`, `platform-kernel` only). So C20 added `sort_pending_for_push` (daemon) and `order_pending_for_push` (bridge), each the identical comparator, and the tablet has a third copy - four in total, three of them now the same one-line expression.
  **The correct shared home is `kasirmu_core::offline`**, the one crate all three paths already depend on. Moving it there collapses the copies AND upgrades `lib.rs`'s priority-only sort to the total key, so it is a consolidation and a correctness fix in one change rather than a tidy-up.
  **The total key, as C20 established it and which must survive the move:** `(priority, created_at, id)` ascending. Priority alone is not total - `SyncPriority` has only three values and MOST items are `Critical` (every sale, void, refund and payment split passes `SyncPriority::Critical` at `db/offline.rs:391,463,489,538`), so ties are the COMMON case, and `created_at` is only millisecond precision, so same-millisecond items would fall back to SQLite's unspecified row order and flip run to run. `id` is a UUID v7 primary key - unique AND time-ordered, which makes it a correct final tie-break rather than an arbitrary one.
  **ORDERING IS LOAD-BEARING BEYOND SORTING:** the batch is sorted ONCE before the push and every downstream consumer iterates that same vector - `transport.push_items` sends it and `apply_push_results` / `apply_sync_outcomes` `zip` it against per-item outcomes. The server says so explicitly (`sync_api.rs:283`: a reordering would mark the WRONG items as synced or failed) and reassembles outcomes in request order for that reason. A shared helper must therefore reorder the same vector, not build a parallel one.
  Fence: `crates/kasirmu-core/src/db/offline.rs` + its sibling test file, `platform/sync/src/daemon.rs` + `daemon_tests.rs`, `platform/sync/src/lib.rs` (the batching sort), `crates/kasirmu-bridge/src/sync.rs` + `sync_tests.rs`, and the tablet path in `apps/mobile-tauri/src/commands/offline.rs` - the ONE slice where touching the third shell is the POINT rather than scope creep. Do NOT touch `platform/sync/src/queue.rs` (per-item, order-independent, settled).
  **ALSO CLOSE C20's OWN STATED GAP, because the same test work covers it:** the bridge tests pin the COMPARATOR and not the CALL SITE - deleting `order_pending_for_push(&mut pending_items)` from `sync_run_scoped` leaves them green. The daemon test DOES cover its wiring because it goes through `read_config_and_pending`. Closing that for the bridge needs a `BridgeCtx` plus a mock HTTP harness the file does not have; use it if a shared helper makes the call-site assertion cheap, and if not, say so again rather than leaving the gap unstated.
  Acceptance: one shared expression called by all three paths; the total key proven by a test seeding same-priority same-MILLISECOND items and asserting a DETERMINISTIC order across runs; and C20's negative control preserved - commenting out the sort must still make a Low item lead a Critical one.

- [x] **C50 [P2] NEW, from C48's enumeration: the duplicate-id classification rule now has a FIFTH expression, on the server, as a hand-written literal.** - **DONE 2026-09-23, commit ab9f00fc9** (`apps/cloud-server/src/sync_api.rs` + its test file). **The conditional decision rule resolved to ONE-SOURCE-OF-TRUTH, with the condition settled by hard evidence rather than assumption:** `apps/cloud-server/Cargo.toml:20` already declares `kasirmu-core = { workspace = true }`, `sync_api.rs` already calls `kasirmu_core::` in that same file (`:224`, `:263`, `:285`, `:422`), and `is_duplicate_id_rejection` is `pub` and NOT feature-gated - so the honest minimal fix was to CALL THE EXISTING FUNCTION, with no duplication, no comment-as-contract and no new shared constant. The constant, the producer and the client predicate were all left untouched, as fenced. The classification was extracted into a private `push_outcome_label(&PushOutcome) -> &'static str` for the same structural reason as the three client-side appliers: an inline match arm is unreachable from a unit test, and the classification is the thing under test. **Both directions asserted on STRUCTURED values** (the label, not a formatted exposition string, and the classifier is called directly rather than through `/metrics` text parsing): a reason built FROM the constant labels `conflict`; ALL THREE non-duplicate reasons the handler can actually produce label `rejected` (`invalid id:`, `missing batch outcome for`, `pg insert failed:`); the ANCHORING case (`pg insert failed: duplicate id: retried` -> `rejected`) because the predicate is `starts_with`; and `Accepted`/`Conflict` labels unchanged. **ON THE 'prefer structured values' INSTRUCTION, IT MADE THE SHARPEST POINT OF THE SLICE:** the sharing itself has no RUNTIME value to assert - two independently-written literals that are equal today behave identically to one call and diverge only on the next reword, which is exactly the failure being closed, so a runtime assertion provably cannot catch it. It pinned the coupling at the SOURCE instead (`include_str!`) and then PROVED THE PIN HAS TEETH: corrupting the expected value failed the test, and restoring the hand-written `starts_with("duplicate id:")` classifier also failed it - **while all three behavioural tests kept passing, which is precisely the silently-desynchronised scenario.** **Honest limitation and the remaining half:** the producer side (`sync_store/sqlite.rs:102`, `sync_store/pg.rs:107`) still builds its reason with `format!("duplicate id: {}", item.id)`, which C50's fence forbade changing, so the two ends are PINNED rather than UNIFIED - a reword of the constant now fails this test and NAMES the files that must move with it, instead of silently splitting the metric from the thing it measures. **That remaining half is queued as C50b.** Fence integrity: `git status` on `sync_client.rs` and `sync_store/` is empty. **Contention handled per the rule:** its first `cargo test --bins` died in the linker (`rust-lld.exe`, exit `0xffffffff`); it confirmed via `cargo check --all-targets` that there were NO compile errors before retrying once, which succeeded - it did not retry blindly and did not widen the target.
  ORIGINAL BRIEF: `apps/cloud-server/src/sync_api.rs:342-345` classifies `Rejected` for Prometheus labels with `reason.starts_with("duplicate id:")` instead of using `DUPLICATE_ID_REJECTION_PREFIX`. It is NOT a defect - it mutates no row, it only labels metrics - but it is the same 'three copies of a classification rule' divergence one layer up, and it is worse than the client copies because the prefix's PRODUCER and its classifier live in different files (`apps/cloud-server/src/sync_store/pg.rs:107` constructs the `duplicate id:` string; `sync_api.rs:333/337` constructs `invalid id:` and `missing batch outcome for`), so renaming the constant would silently desynchronise them.
  Fence: `apps/cloud-server/src/sync_api.rs` (the metric label region only). Done when the classifier uses the shared constant (or a server-side equivalent documented as such), with a test that the label still appears for a duplicate-id rejection and does not for a genuine one.

- [x] **C51 [P1] NEW, from C48's enumeration, and it is a genuine short-circuit of exactly the kind this review was written about: a function that PRETENDS SUCCESS.** - **DONE 2026-09-23, commit 6347f3ee3** (`fix(core): make the sync-http-disabled push stub fail instead of faking Accepted`; sync_client.rs +82, sync_client_tests.rs +73). **THE CHOICE IS THE ERROR, AND THE STRONGEST EVIDENCE FOR IT WAS INSIDE THE SAME FILE:** four sibling disabled stubs ALREADY return `Err(SyncHttpError::Client("sync-http feature is disabled"))` - `ack_memo_on_server` (:657), `fetch_active_memos_from_server` (:812), `qris_charge_on_server` (:922), `qris_status_from_server` (:978) - plus `sync_pull::fetch_snapshot_from_server` (:247). So this was not a new shape imposed on the crate; it is the ESTABLISHED HOUSE RULE, and the push path was the LONE EXCEPTION. **Worse, and it is this review's thesis in miniature: `fetch_active_memos_from_server`'s own doc comment already NAMED the divergence in prose** - 'a read has no honest success stub, unlike the push path's pretend-accepted' - so the anomaly was documented, understood, and left standing. **NO CALLER RELIED ON PRETEND-SUCCESS, checked rather than assumed:** all seven `send_items_to_server` call sites match on the `Result` and already have an `Err` arm that does the right thing unchanged (mobile `offline.rs:351`, bridge `offline.rs:365`, bridge `sync.rs:694` and mobile `sync.rs:260` route `Err` to `mark_all_failed`, leaving the row in the queue as `failed` with the reason in `last_error` and counted in `failed_count`; mobile `lib.rs:466` logs and leaves rows `pending`). The error therefore converts a SILENT LOCAL DESTRUCTION into the existing loud, diagnosable path, and no test asserted the fake `Accepted`. **BOTH DOORS CAN NO LONGER DIVERGE:** rather than giving the two stubs the same body, the disabled-path decision became a single function `push_outcomes_without_http` that both delegate to - two identical bodies is the copy-drift this workstream keeps finding; one callee is not. **AND IT HANDLED THE UNTESTABLE HONESTLY:** the feature genuinely cannot be disabled to test this - verified, not assumed: `cargo check -p kasirmu-core --no-default-features` fails with `E0433: cannot find module or crate 'reqwest'` (ungated reqwest in `license_verification.rs`/`sync_auth.rs`, which the crate documents itself at `attestation.rs:206`). So it used a `#[cfg]`-mirrored SHARED-CALLEE test rather than a copy: the helper is `#[cfg(any(not(feature = "sync-http"), test))]`, so the test build compiles and exercises THE SAME FUNCTION the disabled build calls. A second test pins the DELEGATION ITSELF by reading the source through `include_str!` and asserting the disabled async stub contains `push_outcomes_without_http()` and does NOT contain `PushOutcome::Accepted` - so a future edit reintroducing a local `Ok(...)` fails even though the stub body can never execute in a runnable build. **Evidence:** `cargo test -p kasirmu-core sync_client` -> **44 passed, 0 failed**; falsification restored the pre-fix body and BOTH new tests failed; `cargo check -p kasirmu-core` clean with no warnings (the helper's cfg gating was added specifically to clear a `never used` warning under `-D warnings`); `grep -rn 'Pretend all items' crates/` now returns nothing. **Follow-up filed as C53:** the sibling stubs still inline the message literal (five copies, six counting sync_pull's variant).
  ORIGINAL BRIEF: `crates/kasirmu-core/src/sync_client.rs:463` and `:524` - the `#[cfg(not(feature = "sync-http"))]` stubs of `send_items_to_server{,_blocking}` - return `Ok(vec![PushOutcome::Accepted; items.len()])` **without performing any network call**, with the comment `// Pretend all items were accepted when HTTP is compiled out.` Fed to any of the four appliers, that marks every queued mutation `synced` and destroys it locally, silently.
  **Reachability, checked rather than assumed: LATENT.** `sync-http` is a DEFAULT feature (`crates/kasirmu-core/Cargo.toml:47`) and no workspace member disables default features (`kasirmu-core = { workspace = true }` in desktop, mobile and bridge), so it is compiled out of every shipping build. It is a footgun, not an active data-loss bug - and it is one `--no-default-features` build away from being the latter.
  **Why it is P1 anyway: the failure mode is silent and total.** A stub whose job is to pretend success is indistinguishable, to every caller, from a real successful push - there is no error, no log, no metric. This is the same shape as the audit-retention divergence (C44) and the inert quota axis (C36): a mechanism that reports success while doing nothing.
  Fence: `crates/kasirmu-core/src/sync_client.rs` (the two stub bodies only) plus its sibling test file. Do NOT change the feature wiring or the manifest.
  Done when the stub either returns an ERROR (so a build without HTTP fails loudly instead of destroying queued items) or is documented at the call site as unusable, with a test asserting the chosen behaviour. Prefer the error: a compile-time configuration that cannot sync should not be able to report that it synced. State which you chose and why.

- [x] **C52 [P2] NEW, a workflow hazard that has now bitten twice: rewriting `scripts/ipc-parity-allowlist.json` through Python re-introduces CRLF.** - **DONE 2026-09-23, commit 85786cce4** (`scripts/verify-ipc-parity.py` only). **MY PREMISE WAS WRONG AGAIN, AND THE WORKER CHECKED IT BEFORE BUILDING:** the by-construction fix was ALREADY IN HEAD before either incident - `write_allowlist_payload` has opened `NamedTemporaryFile("w", encoding="utf-8", newline="\n", ...)` since `baa025f77` (2026-09-13), while C41 (78e55701d) and C47 (464782f53) are 2026-09-23. **The writer never re-introduced CRLF; the two lanes hit it by rewriting the file through AD-HOC Python that BYPASSED the helper.** So 'fix it by construction' was already satisfied, and the genuinely missing half was the READ-BACK ASSERTION the brief also demanded. **Direction (1), byte-neutral rewrite observed rather than assumed:** running the real writer flag against the real file gave before = after = `69baa65691e48c761ede20bca063e2ce6341556b`, `git hash-object` == `git rev-parse :path`, `git diff --quiet` exit 0, 35545 bytes / **0 CR** / 268 LF. **Direction (2), the pre-fix path reproduced:** `Path.write_text(text, encoding="utf-8")` with no `newline=` on a temp copy produced **35813 bytes / 268 CR** against the fixed 35545 / 0 CR - `byte-identical: False` with `differing TEXT lines: 0`, i.e. a whole-file byte difference invisible to every payload assertion. Independent corroboration: the stale index stat for the real file recorded `size: 35813`, exactly the CRLF length. **The guard is proven END TO END rather than argued:** `assert_allowlist_bytes(path, written)` is called TWICE - on the sibling temp BEFORE `os.replace` (fail-safe) and on the target AFTER it (proof) - and sabotaging a copy's write path to `newline="\r\n"` makes the real CLI print `... did not come back as the bytes this run wrote: 35545 bytes written, 35813 on it now (268 CR there, 0 CR written)` and exit **2** with the target's hash UNCHANGED, no sibling temp left, and 0 CR. So a regressed write path is refused BEFORE the rename lands, which means the shared file another lane owns cannot be corrupted - and it refuses rather than normalises, which is the post-pass the brief forbade. Self-test grew 24 cases/167 assertions -> **26 cases/177 assertions**, all 10 new ones passing; `verify-ipc-parity.py` exit 0, `verify-scoped-reads.py` exit 0. **THREE THINGS IT INSISTED MUST NOT BE LOST, all filed:** (a) **TWO PRE-EXISTING SELF-TEST FAILURES are not its own and are not fixed** - `fallback the real tablet tree exposes the shape the leg was written for [n=0 names=[]]` and `case 24 real tree: the leg still sees the nine shared-UI calls it found first run`; it ran HEAD's unmmodified script from a temp probe and got the IDENTICAL 2 failures, so they are pre-existing in the C41 tablet-reachability leg - **and this self-test is NOT WIRED INTO CI** (`dev-ci.yml:694`, `scripts/check.sh:71` and `run-pre-push.py:252` all invoke it WITHOUT `--self-test`), which is why it can sit red unnoticed. Filed as C55, including the decision that wiring it would fail CI today. (b) **`--write-dev-mock-gaps` REORDERS the `dev_mock` section - a real CONTENT bug, not a line-ending one:** `merge_section_entries` returns `sorted(merged)` while the committed section is not in sorted order, so a 'no-op' reseed relocates `create_backup_to` and its 3-line reason for a 4-line content diff. It caught this mid-proof, restored the file BYTE-IDENTICALLY from a backup and re-verified the hash, and deliberately did NOT fix it because canonical ordering is a separate owner decision and another lane rewrote those entries on purpose. Filed as C56. (c) A ` M` on the allowlist with identical bytes was a `text=auto` STAT-CACHE artifact (`git diff-files --raw` reporting worktree hash `000...0`), cleared with `git update-index --refresh` - worth knowing because it is the same phantom-dirty class that cost a worker a round earlier this wave.
  ORIGINAL BRIEF: The C41 worker disclosed it as a side effect the first time; the C47 worker hit it again and had to detect and normalize it. Both were caught, both by a worker who checked `git hash-object` against `git rev-parse :path` rather than trusting the write. The file is tracked LF; `json.dumps` on Windows with default newline handling is the cause. This matters beyond tidiness: a CRLF-only diff on a shared JSON file is the same artifact that made a generated PG file look dirty across several scans this wave, and one of those checks cost a worker a round.
  Fence: `scripts/ipc-parity-allowlist.json` or the script that writes it, plus whichever parity script performs the rewrite. Done when the rewrite path preserves LF BY CONSTRUCTION (e.g. open with `newline=""` and write exact bytes) with an assertion that the produced bytes match on re-read. Do NOT reformat the file's contents - this is about the bytes of the write, not the shape of the JSON.
- [x] **C53 [P2] NEW, from C51: the `"sync-http feature is disabled"` message is inlined in FIVE stubs, six counting sync_pull's variant.** - **DONE 2026-09-25, commit a0b368499. The count was LOW: it was SIX in `sync_client.rs` alone, seven in the crate, and a SEVENTH SITE-CLASS outside the fence.**
  - **The census, measured rather than quoted.** `git grep 'sync-http feature is disabled'` finds **6** in `sync_client.rs` (:521, :786, :940, :1053, :1106, plus the shared helper added by C51), **1** in `sync_pull.rs` (:250), and **5** more in `sync_auth.rs` (:116, :238, :334, :442, :679) where the same string is a `status` field rather than an error. The item said five/six; the true figure inside C51's own fence is six. `sync_auth.rs`'s five are OUTSIDE this item's fence and are left standing deliberately -- they are status payloads, not error messages, so unifying them is a different question (whether a status string should share an author with an error string) and would widen the blast radius past what was asked.
  - **What changed: one expression, six delegations.** `sync_http_disabled_error()` is now the single author of the message and the variant; `push_outcomes_without_http` and all four siblings in `sync_client.rs` delegate to it, and `sync_pull::fetch_snapshot_from_server` does too. The four siblings each carried the identical three-line `Err(SyncHttpError::Client(...))` body -- a change to wording, error type, or operator advice had to be made six times, and the sixth would be missed. That is C51's own copy-drift trap, one layer up from where C51 applied it.
  - **THE ITEM'S EXPLICIT QUESTION -- should sync_pull's variant unify or stay distinct? IT UNIFIED, and the why is the evidence, not a preference.** It returned `SyncHttpError::Network` with a longer pull-specific message where the other five returned `Client` with the plain one. Two facts decided it: **(1)** a build with no HTTP is not a network failure -- nothing is attempted, so `Network` is simply the wrong variant; **(2)** **no caller branches on it.** The only variant-naming match on the pull result is `matches!(snapshot, Err(AuthExpired))` (bridge/sync.rs:954); the error is otherwise consumed by a catch-all `Err(e) => Some(e.to_string())` (:1056-1061). So nothing loses behaviour by agreeing -- but the MESSAGE does reach an operator through that `to_string()`, which is exactly why it now has one author instead of two phrasings.
  - **Note the item's fence was slightly wrong, harmlessly:** it files `sync_pull.rs` as though it were a sibling of `sync_client.rs`; `fetch_snapshot_from_server` is DEFINED in `sync_pull.rs` (:248) and merely re-exported by `sync_client.rs`, which is why the delegation crosses the module boundary as `crate::sync_client::sync_http_disabled_error()`.
  - **TESTS: 3 new, extending the C51 `include_str!` pin to the whole family.** The shared rule names the cause; **every sibling stub must DELEGATE rather than reimplement** (asserted on the SHAPE, because a re-inlined body would satisfy a behavioural test -- same message, same variant -- while restoring the drift); and the pull stub must delegate **and not report Network**. **FALSIFIED TWICE, one per mechanism:** re-inlining `ack_memo_on_server`'s body fails the sibling pin naming that stub, and restoring the pull stub's `Network` form fails the pull pin. Both restored.
  - **A TEST BUG FOUND BY ITS OWN FALSIFICATION, recorded because it would have hidden the defect:** the sibling pin first split the body on a `newline-brace` pair, which matched a nested brace and truncated the body to NOTHING -- the failure message printed an empty body, and a literal re-inlined further down the stub would have been invisible. Fixed to split on the closing brace at column 0; the falsification now prints the offending lines.
  - **Evidence:** `cargo test -p kasirmu-core --lib` -> **3345 passed, 0 failed**; the three new tests pass and each was falsified. **Formatting note:** `cargo fmt -p kasirmu-core -- --check` reports 5 diffs, and all 5 are PRE-EXISTING debt in lines outside this change (211, 289, 1115, 1130, 1240 against my region at 990-1098) -- verified by running the checker against the file at HEAD, which reports the same 5. They were left alone rather than swept into this commit. Clippy is silent on these files (its one warning is in another lane's uncommitted `product_bundles_tests.rs`). ORIGINAL BRIEF: - [ ] **C53 [P2] NEW, from C51: the `"sync-http feature is disabled"` message is inlined in FIVE stubs, six counting sync_pull's variant.** Same class as C50 - one rule expressed in many places - and C51 showed the mechanism that fixes it: make the disabled-path decision a single callee the stubs delegate to, rather than identical bodies. The four siblings (`ack_memo_on_server`, `fetch_active_memos_from_server`, `qris_charge_on_server`, `qris_status_from_server`) plus `sync_pull::fetch_snapshot_from_server` each carry their own copy, so a change to the wording, the error type or the advice given to an operator must be made five or six times - and one of them will be missed.
  Fence: `crates/kasirmu-core/src/sync_client.rs` and `crates/kasirmu-core/src/sync_pull.rs` (the stub bodies and their messages only) plus their sibling test files. Done when a single expression produces the disabled-capability error and every stub delegates to it, with the C51-style `include_str!` delegation pin extended to the siblings so a future edit cannot re-inline one. Note `sync_pull`'s message is a VARIANT - decide whether it should unify or stay distinct, and say why.

Because the grep count IS the acceptance, it REPHRASED THE COMMENT to describe the removed rule rather than reproduce it - and it said why: *'the alternative, adding an exclusion for my own file, would have been rigging the measurement.'* That is the difference between a green result and a true one, and it was one exclusion away from the wrong side. It also swept `.sort(`, `.sort_by(` and `.sort_by_key(` over the queue paths and found only a CRDT Lamport-clock comparison and test-local `Vec` sorts, none of which order the transmit queue. **(2) The existing request-body ordering test still passes** (`sync_run_scoped_pushes_critical_before_an_earlier_low_item` -> 1 passed, exit 0). **(3) The C20 negative control had to be PROVEN on this path, which had no call-site test at all, so one was added** - `retry_offline_sync_scoped_pushes_critical_before_an_earlier_low_item` asserts the ORDER OF THE HTTP REQUEST BODY captured from a raw loopback socket (same pattern as the sibling test; this crate has no mock-HTTP dev-dependency), enqueuing Low -> Critical -> Normal so the un-sorted `ORDER BY created_at ASC` read puts Low first. Restored and re-verified: offline filter 10 passed, sibling filter 1 passed. **A REAL BEHAVIOUR CHANGE ON TIES, stated rather than slipped in:** the bridge path now orders by `(priority, created_at, id)` instead of `priority` alone - intended, because `created_at` is millisecond-precision and most items are Critical, so the old copy left same-millisecond items to SQLite's unspecified row order. The in-place `&mut` call is preserved so the push and the per-item apply keep iterating the same index-aligned vector. **AND THE TABLET QUESTION WAS ANSWERED (b), PLAINLY, WITHOUT MANUFACTURING A TEST:** the tablet's `retry_offline_sync_scoped` (`apps/mobile-tauri/src/commands/offline.rs:298`) is **NOT** a delegate to the bridge function - it is an INDEPENDENT ~80-line body talking to `AppState` directly, while the desktop's command (`apps/desktop-tauri/src/commands/offline.rs:105`) is the one that delegates. So a test here covers the desktop wiring for free and does NOT cover the tablet, and the tablet's call site remains untested because its harness lives outside the fence. **It did not widen the fence and did not file a Fence_Request, correctly noting the conversion itself is complete.** It also flagged, as an observation rather than a defect it touched, that the tablet ALREADY calls the shared helper (`:348`) - so it is already CORRECT, merely UNVERIFIED. **That observation is a real finding and is filed as C54.** No linker failure occurred, so no `cargo check` fallback was needed; no two cargoes ran concurrently; fmt clean on both files, and the acceptances were re-run AFTER formatting rather than before.

One line, the same change as the other four. Fence: that file plus its sibling test file. Done when `grep` for a priority sort in the queue paths returns zero copies outside the shared helper.

- [x] **C54 [P1] NEW, from C49b's (a)/(b) answer: the tablet's `retry_offline_sync_scoped` is a SECOND IMPLEMENTATION of the desktop retry path.** - **DONE 2026-09-23, commit dc2b4dd8c** (`test(mobile): pin the tablet retry path's push order and its twin divergence`; `apps/mobile-tauri/src/commands/offline.rs` +7 doc-only, `offline_tests.rs` +147). MANAGER-RE-VERIFIED: git show --stat is exactly those two files. **THE DIFF WAS DONE FIRST, AS ORDERED, AND IT FOUND A LIVE BUG - see C59.** Verdict: they differ for a REAL reason and one difference is a defect, so DO NOT COLLAPSE. The step-by-step table: Phase 1 read is the same file on both paths; the permission gate is the same rule through a different seam; the config-missing error is the same message in a different error type; **Phase 3 WRITE IS A DIFFERENT FILE - the tablet locks `state.db`, the GLOBAL identity database, while Phase 1 read the STORE database**; the bridge re-resolves the store scope after its await (because its conn guard is not `Send`); ordering is the shared `order_for_push` on both; the return type is the same. So the tablet pushes successfully and then writes its outcomes to a database where those ids do not exist - `mark_offline_synced` returns NotFound, the `?` in `apply_sync_outcomes` aborts, the store's rows stay `pending`, and **every retry re-sends the same items forever**. **The worker REFUSED TO COLLAPSE, and the reasoning is the right call:** collapse is mechanically available (`AppState::bridge_ctx()` at `state.rs:450`, used by 20+ tablet commands) but changing which DATABASE a live bug writes to is a behaviour change on that bug's blast radius, not a refactor. **So it PINNED the behaviour instead: the new test asserts the command returns `AppError::Core { sub_kind: NotFound }` AFTER the request body proves the push succeeded - i.e. the outcomes went to a database where the ids do not exist - so when the collapse lands THAT assertion fails and whoever lands it deletes it deliberately.** It chose `NotFound` over a bare `is_err()` so the test names the mechanism rather than any error. **ACCEPTANCE, both proven:** the tablet CALL-SITE test (the one the bridge's test file cannot make, because the tablet command needs `AppState` and the bridge harness builds a `BridgeCtx`) binds a raw `TcpListener` loopback socket, captures the push body, and asserts the parsed array is `["money", "catalog", "bulk"]` - Critical before Normal before Low - from a queue seeded Low -> Critical -> Normal; `cargo test -p kasirmu-mobile offline` -> **21 passed, exit 0**. And the ordering key IS the shared rule, proven by the C20 control RUN rather than asserted: commenting out `order_for_push` fails it with `left: ["bulk", "money", "catalog"]`, restored -> 21 green. **NO FENCE REQUEST, AND IT EXPLAINS WHY:** the `AppState`-building harness lives in `sync_tests.rs` outside the fence, but the pattern was reproducible inside the FENCED sibling `offline_tests.rs` (mounted at `offline.rs:474`, already importing `AppState`/`migrations`), so the acceptance was achievable in-fence and no widening was warranted. **AND IT ATTRIBUTED THREE UNRELATED FAILURES TO ANOTHER SESSION BY PROOF RATHER THAN ASSERTION:** `cargo test -p kasirmu-mobile --lib` exits 101 with three `drift_pin_*` failures naming new debt `desktop_link::{login_with_email_password, request_email_login_code, verify_email_login_code}`; those three names appear **0 times at HEAD** and once each in the working tree (uncommitted registrations in `apps/mobile-tauri/src/lib.rs`, a dirty file from another lane), and it confirmed independence by reverting its OWN doc edit and re-running the gate - same three failures, exit 101. The ceiling arithmetic corroborates: 92 ungated against 89, a delta of exactly +3. It did NOT regenerate the ledger or raise the ceiling, because that would commit another session's in-flight work under its message.

The rendering is therefore extracted into a pure `render_fallback_info(shell, fb)` -- pure over its inputs, which is this file's own stated design principle (its self-test docstring says the parsers are pure "so the self-test can hand it a fabricated tree") and what makes the fallback leg's output testable at all, since `main()` itself is not.

Restored.

MANAGER-RE-VERIFIED: git show --stat is exactly those two files. **THE FIX re-resolves the STORE scope for Phase 3, mirroring the bridge reference (`crates/kasirmu-bridge/src/offline.rs:371-375`) and this shell's own already-correct `sync_run_scoped` (`apps/mobile-tauri/src/commands/sync.rs:253-258`)**, replacing `state.db.lock().await` (the global identity db) with `state.resolve_scope(&session_token)` + the store connection guard. It re-resolves rather than carrying the Phase 1 guard across the HTTP await, for the reason `sync_run_scoped` documents: the store manager's `std::sync::Mutex` guard is not `Send`. after, Phase 3 writes the store db, the guarded CAS lands, the command returns `Ok(SyncResult { synced_count: 3, .. })`, and `list_pending_offline` no longer offers those rows - **loop closed**. **AND THE 'BEFORE' WAS PROVEN RATHER THAN QUOTED:** reverting Phase 3 to `state.db` fails the new test with exactly the documented mechanism - `a successful push must no longer abort on Phase 3: Core { sub_kind: NotFound, message: "not found: offline_queue 01a0cd1d-..." }`, exit 101 - and restoring it returns exit 0. **THE DIVERGENCE PIN WAS DELETED DELIBERATELY, WITH THE COMMIT BODY SAYING SO:** *'This is the fix the C54 divergence pin was waiting for: that pin, a NotFound assertion over a successful push, is deleted deliberately in this commit.'* That is the handoff working exactly as designed - the pin existed so that whoever closed the bug had to do it on purpose, and the replacement assertion is the positive one (rows `synced`, pending count 0). **ACCEPTANCE, three pieces, `cargo test -p kasirmu-mobile offline` -> 22 passed, exit 0:** success now marks store rows `synced` with `synced_count == 3` and `pending_offline_count() == 0`, PLUS an assertion that the GLOBAL queue stays empty so the fix cannot silently re-introduce the old write target; the ordering test still passes unchanged (body `["money", "catalog", "bulk"]`); and a NEW failure-path test drives a real HTTP 500 through the same path and asserts the command does not abort, `synced_count == 0`, `failed_count == 1`, the row is `Failed` (never `Synced`) with `retry_count == 1` and a recorded `last_error` - deliberately NOT weakened to 'an error occurred', because the hazard of moving a write target is OVER-MARKING, so the test pins that a failed push is recorded on the store row rather than reported as success. **AND IT LEFT THE OTHER SESSION'S WORK ALONE:** `apps/mobile-tauri/src/lib.rs` remains dirty with the uncommitted `desktop_link::*` registrations, the same three `drift_pin_*` failures remain (gate filter shows 8 passed / 3 failed), grepping the disagreement for `offline::` returns nothing, and it neither regenerated the ledger nor moved the ceiling. **C60 filed:** the audit record for this bug is still marked OPEN and cites pre-rebrand paths that do not exist.

The chain: `apps/mobile-tauri/src/commands/offline.rs:298` `retry_offline_sync_scoped` is three-phase - **Phase 1** reads the pending queue from the STORE database (`state.resolve_scope(&session_token)` -> `db_manager.open_store` -> `<data_dir>/store-<id>.sqlite`), **Phase 2** pushes successfully, and **Phase 3** writes the outcomes to `state.db` - which is the **GLOBAL IDENTITY database** (`AppState::new` -> `resolve_db_path` -> `<app_data_dir>/kasir.db`, `state.rs:164-171`), NOT the file Phase 1 read. Those ids do not exist there, so `mark_offline_synced` returns `CoreError::NotFound` (`crates/kasirmu-core/src/db/offline.rs:643-666`) and the `?` inside `apply_sync_outcomes` ABORTS the command. The desktop path is correct: `crates/kasirmu-bridge/src/offline.rs:317` re-resolves the STORE scope for its Phase 3 write.

So this is the opposite of an ungated checker: **the red C63 saw is what a newly-built gate looks like when it works** -- it found real drift the day it was switched on, and a peer then fixed the data. - [x] **C64 CLOSED 2026-09-25 - investigation shows the ordering is UNREACHABLE, and both guards are now pinned (`0adb58ddf`).** C64's arithmetic is real and was REPRODUCED: with the clamp removed, the refund-before-accrual case yields `-350`; with it, `0`. So the floor does swallow the reversal, exactly as C64 predicted. But the ordering cannot occur: (1) `process_refund_unchecked` refuses any sale whose status is not Completed (kasirmu-bridge/src/refunds.rs:109-114) and the accrual runs on the transition TO completed, so a sale is always accrued before it can be refunded; (2) the sync path refuses it independently - a refund arriving for an absent sale takes `credit_refund_effect_without_sale` (platform-sync/src/queue.rs:751), which credits STOCK only and never touches customer spend. **The real gap C64 surfaced was that neither guard was tested:** nothing pinned the status check (every case in refunds_tests.rs called `store.create_refund` directly, which has no status predicate), and nothing recorded the arithmetic. Both are now covered and BOTH FALSIFIED by neutering the guard they protect. No production code changed, which is the correct outcome for a P1 whose check found the defect class already closed. Original finding follows: 
  [ ] **C64 [P1] NEW, found by C61's re-derivation: `customers.total_spent_minor` has TWO writers with OPPOSITE SIGNS in DIFFERENT transactions, and a zero floor hides a double-subtraction.** `crates/kasirmu-core/src/db/sales_lifecycle.rs:59` ADDS to it and `crates/kasirmu-core/src/db/refunds.rs:1027` subtracts via `MAX(total_spent_minor - ?1, 0)`. That is a multi-writer balance by construction - the same shape as the `loyalty_accounts.points` / `customers.loyalty_points` defect the review named and that has since been fixed into a single transaction - but this one was NOT fixed, because the review's named instance was the loyalty pair. **And the `MAX(..., 0)` floor is what makes it dangerous rather than merely untidy:** if a refund is applied before its sale is counted, the subtraction clamps at zero, so the under-count is silently swallowed and a later sale adds on top of a wrong base. A floor on a derived balance converts a visible negative into an invisible wrong number.
  Fence: `crates/kasirmu-core/src/db/{sales_lifecycle,refunds}.rs` plus their `*_tests.rs` siblings. Done when either both writers run inside ONE transaction with the other's work visible, or the value is derived rather than stored (a SUM over the sales/refunds rows it is supposed to represent) - and the discriminating test is a refund BEFORE its sale, which today should show the clamped floor swallowing the difference. If the second option is taken, say what the migration does with existing rows, because a derived value and a stored value disagree for every historical record the moment they diverge. If investigation shows a path that keeps the two writers ordered so the clamp cannot be reached, show it and close the item with that evidence instead.

The item also said EIGHT functions; it is **six** -- the other two files in that commit were test siblings.

  - **The replacement targets the only path where the wrap changes an outcome: AUTOCOMMIT, part-way through.** `soft_delete_user_rolls_back_the_update_when_the_marker_refresh_fails` drops `over_quota_markers` so step 2 fails with step 1 already written, then asserts the row is NOT in the trash. **FALSIFIED: with the wrap removed it FAILS** -- "a failed delete must leave the member out of the trash", the autocommitted UPDATE surviving as a half-deleted row. With the wrap it passes. That is the statement the verdict was making, now measured.
  - **The verdicts stand unchanged.** Nothing needed reverting or relabelling: no wrap is unobservable, and the one place a proof was missing now has one that fails without the code it covers. **Evidence:** `cargo test -p kasirmu-core --lib` -> **3361 passed, 0 failed**; `cargo fmt -p kasirmu-core -- --check` clean in the touched file. **What is NOT covered, stated so the tick is not read as more than it is:** the other four wrappers still carry verdicts with no falsifying test each -- this pass supplied one for the function where the join-versus-autocommit distinction is sharpest, not four. The code compiles and every filter is green (assignments 41, kds 166, loyalty 53+84, profile 90, staff 90), so nothing is broken - but some of those eight wraps may be no-ops on a single shared connection, exactly like the eight-of-nine non-discriminating rollback tests in the sibling slice. Fence: the six source files above plus their test siblings. Done when each wrapped function carries a one-line verdict in its doc comment - the observable invariant it protects, or an explicit 'contract improvement, not a defect fix' - and any wrap whose invariant turns out to be unobservable is either reverted or relabelled. **This is a documentation-quality item, not a correctness one: the risk is a future reader treating a no-op wrap as a guarantee.**

- [x] **C66 [P2] DONE 2026-09-26. The 120s was NOT the fixture cost -- it was a SELF-DEADLOCK on the test's own DDL lock, and it is gone: 140.3s -> 18.6s on a real Postgres.**
  - **The item's premise did not reproduce as stated, and measuring first is what found the real defect.** The item reads the 120s as the cost of a slow test ("a test taking 120s+ against a 3-try budget", "per-tenant setup a shared fixture could do once"). Two measurements say otherwise. **(a)** A whole `PG_INIT` apply -- 127 CREATE TABLE, 188 CREATE INDEX, 5 DO blocks -- takes **1.76s** on a fresh database (timed with `psql -f` in the container), so the per-throwaway-DB schema apply is not 120s of anything. **(b)** On a clean cluster the test still took **140.3s** and nextest still marked it `SLOW [>120.000s]`.
  - **The cause was found by watching `pg_stat_activity` while the test ran, not by reasoning about it.** Polled every 12s during the run, two sessions sat on the same advisory key the whole time: **one `idle` holding `pg_advisory_lock(5717975333627777873)`, one `active` waiting on it in `Lock/advisory`.** `5717975333627777873` is `SCHEMA_LOCK_KEY` -- so the test was blocked on *itself*.
  - **THE BUG IS AT `pg_tests.rs:832` + `:849`: the test took the DDL guard TWICE in a row.** `let _ddl = pg_ddl_guard(&url).await;` took the session-scoped advisory lock for the role-drop cleanup and deliberately **held it to the end of the test** (its own comment said so: "it releases when `_ddl` drops at the end of the test"). Two statements later `let ddl = pg_ddl_guard(&url).await;` took the *same non-reentrant key on a different connection* for `DROP DATABASE`. The second acquisition blocked for the full `SCHEMA_LOCK_TIMEOUT` of **120s** and then -- by the guard's own documented fallback -- **ran the window unlocked**. So the one window the guard exists to protect was the one window it did not protect, and the suite paid 120s for the privilege.
  - **FALSIFIED BY KILLING THE HOLDER, which is the experiment that settles it.** With the unfixed code running and already blocked, `pg_terminate_backend` on the single `idle` holder dropped the test from a projected 140s to **PASS [22.2s]** -- so the other 120s really was that wait, and not load, not the fixture, not the container.
  - **The fix is one guard instead of two.** The lock is taken once (`let ddl = pg_ddl_guard(&url).await;`), held across BOTH cleanup steps, and released explicitly at the end, so the `DROP DATABASE` now genuinely runs inside the lock. **Falsified the other way too:** re-introducing the second take exactly as it was, on the same cluster with everything else identical, put it back to **SLOW / PASS [141.4s]**; removing it returned **PASS [18.6s]**. Both directions run in one sitting, so the comparison is like-for-like.
  - **No other guard site in the file repeats the pattern.** All 14 acquisitions were audited: every one is a single take with an explicit `release()` before any re-take (`:1350`/`:1403` releases before the `:1413` re-take), except `:1508`, a lone `let _ddl` held to test end -- which is correct by design, because nothing takes it a second time. C66's test was the only double-take.
  - **The item's P2 worry -- "a test quietly retried green forever" -- was real but its FLAKE reading was wrong in a way worth stating.** The test never actually flaked on correctness: it passed every attempt, at 140s, inside a 3-try budget and under a `slow-timeout` that only *reports*. It was one busier runner away from `terminate-after = 3`, which is the latent red the item predicted; it was not one. The fix removes the wait rather than documenting it, which is the item's first accepted outcome.
  - **Evidence, all against a real `postgres:17-alpine` on :15432 (nothing skipped):** `cargo nextest run -p kasirmu-api` -> **342 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped**; whole `pg::tests` filter -> **14 passed in 19.8s** with no test SLOW; the same filter under `--profile ci` (90s slow-timeout, the stricter one) -> **14 passed, RLS test 25.4s**; `python scripts/verify-pg-tests-ran.py --nextest-junit target/nextest/default/junit.xml` -> **PASS**, 342 cases, no `<flakyFailure>`, no `<failure>`; `cargo clippy -p kasirmu-api --all-targets -- -D warnings` silent; `cargo fmt -p kasirmu-api -- --check` clean. The 140.3s -> 18.6s figure is the same test, same cluster, same commit otherwise, both measured this session.
  - **What is NOT covered, stated so the tick is not read as more than it is:** the fix removes the 120s self-wait. The test still costs **~19-25s** of real work (10 throwaway databases across the PG suite, each applying the full 127-table schema), and the item's other suggestion -- sharing one schema-applied database across the 8 `throwaway_test_pool` call sites -- was **not** taken: at 1.76s per apply it buys little, and those tests take a throwaway DB precisely because sharing the base DB was the flake source the fixture's own doc comment documents. Nothing here changes that trade-off.
  ORIGINAL BRIEF: - [ ] **C66 [P2] NEW, found in the PR 113 CI logs: `kasirmu-api pg::tests::pg_integration_rest_rls_non_owner` is FLAKY and SLOW, and it failed once already.** Across two consecutive runs: TRY 1 exceeded **120s** and was reported SLOW, TRY 2 passed at **123s**, and in the earlier run it panicked at `crates/kasirmu-api/src/pg_tests.rs:820:10` while the summary counted `1 flaky`. It did not fail either run, but a test taking 120s+ against a 3-try budget is a latent red that will fail on a busier runner or a slower PostgreSQL container - and when it does, it will look like an unrelated regression. Fence: `crates/kasirmu-api/src/pg_tests.rs` (that test only) plus whatever fixture or RLS setup it depends on. Done when either its cost is brought under the retry budget with the reason recorded (e.g. per-tenant setup a shared fixture could do once), or its slow-and-flaky character is written down where a future reader will find it - because the wrong outcome is a test quietly retried green forever, which teaches everyone to ignore a red.

- [x] **C67 [P2] NEW, found while fixing the tablet pins: the tablet's floor pin carries a slack allowance that SWALLOWED a three-command registration entirely.** - **DONE 2026-09-25, commit ebdca2758. The slack was NOT deleted -- it was moved to the comparison it was written for, and the fix immediately caught a SECOND registration hidden the same way.**
  - **The defect reproduced with numbers rather than the item's figures.** A probe printed the pin's own operands: **parsed=343, floor=342, slack=24, ledger=342**. So the tree was already ONE above the floor with no red, and the pin tolerated up to **366** -- twenty-three further silent registrations. The item's "339 == 339 plus slack 24" was the state when it was written; the floor has since been raised to 342, and the defect survived that raise.
  - **The root cause is the COMPARISON, not the constant, and the desktop pin proves it.** The desktop twin holds the SAME slack 24 (`desktop-tauri/.../registration_gate_tests.rs:144`) but applies it at `:804` as `measured.abs_diff(debt::REGISTERED_TOTAL) <= REGISTERED_SLACK` -- the ledger's distance from the TREE -- and separately asserts the floor against the tree by **exact equality** at `:793-802`. The tablet inverted this: it applied the slack to the tree-vs-floor comparison (`:846`) and had **no equality leg at all**. So the item's framing ("its size is the bug") is half right -- the size makes it worse, but the SHAPE is what let a registration through.
  - **The slack's real purpose was preserved, not discarded.** The generated-ledger leg documents it: `REGISTERED_SLACK` "allows the ledger to lag the tree between regenerations" (`:1069-1072`). That is a legitimate claim about the LEDGER, so it now governs exactly that, while the floor reads the tree by equality to match the desktop contract. Deleting the constant would have removed a real allowance.
  - **THE FIX IMMEDIATELY FOUND A SECOND, LIVE OCCURRENCE.** The new equality went red on the first run: **lib.rs registers 343, the floor said 342.** Traced rather than assumed: `af77f0144` ("feat(mobile-tauri): register the role re-seed door on the tablet") added `commands::setup::seed_default_roles_scoped` and touched `commands/setup.rs` and its tests but **NOT this file or the generated ledger** -- the same shape as `fc2ea1938`, which the file's own floor comment already documents. So the lag was real and unattributed, not a mis-parse. Floor raised to 343 with the provenance recorded in that comment, and the ledger regenerated (its `REGISTERED_TOTAL` rose by exactly one, with **no row changes** -- confirming a genuinely new registration rather than a re-classification).
  - **FALSIFIED BY RESTORING THE EXACT PRE-C67 STATE, and the result is the whole argument for the change:** with floor 342, ledger 342 and tree 343, and ONLY the old slack comparison in place, the pin **passes green** -- the defect is invisible. Add the equality and it fails naming the drill. Both forms were run in that same probe, so the comparison is like-for-like rather than two separate experiments.
  - **Evidence:** `cargo test -p kasirmu-mobile --lib registration_gate` -> **12 passed, 0 failed**; `cargo clippy -p kasirmu-mobile --lib --all-targets` silent; `cargo fmt -p kasirmu-mobile -- --check` reports one diff in this file, a **trailing newline at line 1823**, present at HEAD too (verified by checking the file out and re-running) and far from both edited regions (the floor comment ~193-210, the pin body ~836-905) -- pre-existing debt left alone rather than swept into this commit. ORIGINAL BRIEF: - [ ] **C67 [P2] NEW, found while fixing the tablet pins: the tablet's floor pin carries a slack allowance that SWALLOWED a three-command registration entirely.** `apps/mobile-tauri/src/commands/registration_gate_tests.rs`'s `drift_pin_registration_floor_is_met` asserts `REGISTERED_FLOOR == REGISTERED_TOTAL` (339 == 339) **plus** `parsed <= floor + REGISTERED_SLACK` where the slack is **24**. Commit `fc2ea1938` registered three commands, the tree parsed 342, and 342 sits inside 339 + 24 - so **a stale floor and a stale ledger total agreed with each other while both lagged the tree by three, and the pin stayed GREEN.** It only surfaced via the ceiling pin, because the ceiling counts ungated doors and had no slack. **The slack is documented as intentional and it does have a purpose (it lets registrations land before the floor is re-pinned), but its size is the bug: at 24 it can absorb twenty-four registrations - a whole slice - and the pin that exists to notice registrations is the one pin that cannot notice them.** Contrast the DESKTOP floor pin, which is an outright equality against the tree and therefore went red immediately: the two shells have different guarantees for the same-named pin.
