The substitution therefore excludes stamp lines by construction, and the result proves the guard worked: **both website pages came out BYTE-IDENTICAL**, because their only crate reference sat inside a stamp. A blind replace would have rewritten a published page's audit record.

  - **Evidence:** `check-dead-refs.py` on the three most-edited specs -> "LIVE DOCS: every path reference resolves", 0 unresolved; `verify-ci-docs-drift.py` 0 drift; `node scripts/generate-records-index.mjs --check` exit 0. ~~**the structural half**~~ -- **CLOSED 2026-09-25, commit f9cd8f5c3.** The rule the previous paragraph predicted was built to the shape it proposed: `renamed_crate_target()` in `check-dead-refs.py` reports an exempt path when the named directory is gone AND the rebranded twin exists. **The distinguisher was MEASURED before it was written, and two candidate heuristics were rejected on the evidence:** "does the last path segment exist anywhere?" returns 0 for BOTH a pre-rename `ozpkg.rs` and a planned `XXX_*.sql`, so it separates nothing. What does separate them is the **deepest existing prefix** -- a PLAN sits inside a crate that exists (3 of 4 segments resolve), while a RENAME takes the crate directory itself away (1 of 4) and leaves a same-named twin. **SCOPE, and this is where the first attempt was wrong.** Rescuing across all eight exempt prefixes produced **213 findings in 57 docs** -- almost all in ADRs, dated records and finished plans, which are records OF a past state and must not be rewritten. The exemption's own comment names the tell ("_active/ is the tell: a spec that is still open has not finished moving the tree"), so the rescue is scoped to `docs/specs/_active/` alone. That is the "gate that cries wolf" this file warns about, avoided by narrowing rather than by tolerating noise. **IT IMMEDIATELY FOUND DRIFT THE ROUND-21 PASS HAD MISSED: 14 references in 5 specs, and most were RUNNABLE COMMANDS.** A `grep -rn 'env::set_var|env::remove_var' apps/desktop-client/src/` and three siblings are reproduction recipes a reader is meant to run, naming a shell directory that no longer exists; they were missed because the round-21 sweep searched `crates/oz-*` patterns and these are `apps/*-client`. All repointed, and the checker now reports **0**. **FALSIFIED LIKE-FOR-LIKE, one injected reference, two runs:** with the rescue DISABLED, a live `_active/` spec citing `crates/oz-core/src/exchange_rate.rs` reports **0 unresolved -- the silent hole C70 described**; re-enabling it reports that path and exits 1. Same input, same command, only the rule differing. **The rename table is VERIFIED against the tree, not trusted:** eight self-test cases cover the rescue, including one asserting every table entry's target directory exists in this checkout and every entry's OLD path is gone, so a future un-rename cannot make the rule invent findings. Self-test **26 cases, no files touched**; all five auditor self-tests OK; docs drift 0. ORIGINAL BRIEF: - [ ] **C70 [P2] NEW, found while fixing C21's doc: 19 LIVE docs still cite pre-rebrand crate paths that NO LONGER EXIST, and the checker that should catch them cannot see them.** Correcting `docs/security/PCI-DSS_CHECKLIST.md` turned up `crates/oz-security`/`crates/oz-core` across eight sites in that one file. Widening the search: **19 non-archived docs** name `oz-core`/`oz-security`/`oz_core` — `docs/specs/_active/` (13 files incl. 0043/0044/0045, c1, c2, c4, c5, p1-p3), `docs/specs/testing/tdd-testing-strategy.md`, and **two published pages, `website/src/content/docs/en/shifts.md` + `id/shifts.md`**. Measured with `git grep -l -E 'oz-security|oz_security|oz-core|oz_core' -- 'docs/specs/**' 'website/**' | Where-Object { $_ -notmatch 'archived|_done' }` -> **19**.
  **Why no checker caught it.** `check-dead-refs.py` works and is honest — run against the tree it reports **7 unresolved references in 1 live doc** (`manager-codebase-review-checklist.md`). It has a **deliberate, documented** exemption at `check-dead-refs.py:152-156`: `docs/specs/_done/`, `_archive/` and **`_active/`** are skipped because "plans and ACTIVE specs describe files still to be created ... Neither is drift." That reasoning is sound for a path that is *planned*. It cannot distinguish a planned path from a *pre-rename* one, so a live spec's acceptance command can name a crate that was renamed away years ago and the tool stays silent by policy.
  **The cost is concrete, not cosmetic.** Every Phase/acceptance command in those specs is a `cargo test -p oz-core` form that prints "package ID specification did not match any packages" — which reads like *gone*, not *renamed*. `todo-open-debt-program.md:154` already records this exact failure mode hitting the debt programme ("not one acceptance command in this file runs verbatim in this tree"), and it was fixed there by hand. The same rot is live in 19 more docs. The website pair matters most: those are reader-facing pages whose audit stamp credits sources by path.
  **Do NOT simply run a find-and-replace.** `oz-core` -> `kasirmu-core` is right for a path, but the two names also appear as legitimate *history* in those same files (rename narratives, "was `oz-security`" notes) and as `oz-*` strings in `deny.toml` skip entries the rebrand checklist deliberately keeps. The decision is per-site, which is why this is an item and not a sweep.
  Fence: `docs/specs/_active/**` (13), `docs/specs/testing/tdd-testing-strategy.md`, `website/src/content/docs/{en,id}/shifts.md`, and `.agents/skills/docs-auditor/scripts/check-dead-refs.py:152-156` (the exemption that hides them).
  Done when: (a) each of the 19 is either corrected to the real path or marked as history, and (b) the checker gains a narrow rule that a path under its exempt set is still reported when the **containing named crate directory exists under a different name** — so a rename can never hide behind the planned-file exemption again, with a planted fixture proving the rule fires.
- [ ] **C69 [P2] NEW, found while investigating C21: the gift-card PIN is stored in plaintext but is never verified anywhere, so it is a credential in name only.** `gift_cards.pin TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT ''` (20260813_init.sql:138) is written verbatim from `input.pin.unwrap_or_default()` (db/gift_cards.rs:49, inserted :63) and read back by three SELECTs (:125, :156, :239), with **no hashing, no comparison, and no verification site**: a tree-wide search for a PIN predicate, a bridge verify function, or a UI prompt to enter a gift-card PIN returns nothing. The only PIN surface is the optional field on the issue modal (`ui/src/features/gift-cards/IssueGiftCardModal.tsx:149-161`). `docs/security/PCI-DSS_CHECKLIST.md:32-39` records this as a CODE FINDING and calls it "a plaintext authentication credential ... in the same database file as `users.pin_hash`, which is hashed by name and design" — but the paragraph stops one step short: it never asks whether anything checks it, and the answer is that nothing does.
  Why it still matters at P2: unlike `card_number` (a printed bearer identifier, see C21), a PIN that a cashier might reasonably *believe* protects a card is stored recoverable in every snapshot. If the feature is intended, the field must be hashed like `users.pin_hash` before it is ever compared; if it is not, the column and its field are dead surface and should go. Either way a reader currently cannot tell which world they are in.
  **Owner question (product, not technical): is gift-card PIN protection intended?** This is the same shape as D5/D7 and is deliberately NOT answered here.
  Fence: `crates/kasirmu-core/src/db/gift_cards.rs` (the `pin` field of `IssueGiftCardInput`, the INSERT at :57-63, the three SELECTs), `ui/src/features/gift-cards/IssueGiftCardModal.tsx` (the optional PIN input), `docs/security/PCI-DSS_CHECKLIST.md:32-39` (the note whose conclusion this amends).
  Done when: the owner picks hash-or-delete, and the outcome is pinned by a test — either `pin` is stored as a PHC hash and a `verify_gift_card_pin` path exists with a test that a wrong PIN fails, or the column, the input and the three SELECT fields are removed with a migration.
  - **CORRECTION ADDED 2026-09-26 (C69 verification pass): the item and the PCI note it amends both UNDERSTATE what the code already does, and that is now written down.** The claim "never verified anywhere" is TRUE and I re-confirmed it by census: `gift_cards.pin` is written verbatim (`db/gift_cards.rs:49`, inserted `:63`) and read back by three SELECTs (`:125`, `:156`, `:239`), with no PIN predicate, no `verify_gift_card_pin`, and no UI prompt anywhere in the tree. But the PIN **is** already treated as a secret at the SERIALIZATION boundary by a prior fix, MSL-10: `modules/loyalty/src/models.rs:105` carries `#[serde(default, skip_serializing)]` (so no JSON response or log dump emits it, while older clients still deserialize), and a manual `Debug` impl at `:127-132` prints `.field("pin", &"<redacted>")`. It is pinned by `gift_card_pin_is_never_serialized_or_debugged` (`:530-567`). The struct's own doc calls it "PIN for balance checks" (`:103-104`) — an intended control. So the accurate characterisation is **protected on the wire and in memory, recoverable at rest, never verified** — not "a credential in name only". Fixed in `docs/security/PCI-DSS_CHECKLIST.md`, which is the doc the item asked to amend.
  - **Still an owner question, and deliberately unanswered by me.** The product decision (hash like `users.pin_hash`, or delete the column and the modal field) is unchanged by the correction and remains the item's blocker. What changed is that the owner now knows the PIN is already redacted at two of three boundaries, which is material to the hash-or-delete call: hashing closes the at-rest gap only.
  Fence: `apps/mobile-tauri/src/commands/registration_gate_tests.rs` (the slack constant and its comment). **The decision, not the number: is `REGISTERED_SLACK` meant to absorb a working session or a single commit?** If a session, 24 is defensible and the honest fix is to document that the ceiling pin is the one that catches registrations; if a commit, shrink it to something that cannot hide three. **Do not just shrink it to make CI green - that is not the failure mode here; the pin is green.** Done when the slack's purpose and its size are reconciled in writing, and the comment states which pin is the backstop for a registration that lands without the floor moving.

- [ ] **C35 [P1, DEFERRED - do not start until D9 is answered] Parameterize the four catalog children so the FK wall cannot fire.** Gated because YAGNI now applies: the parameterization only earns its keep once a sync action for these tables exists to carry a real tenant into them, and the finding recorded above says none does. The analysis is kept so the slice is ready the moment that changes. This is the surviving half of C32-S2, and it is a PREPARATION slice, not a live-defect fix: today `product_variants` omits `tenant_id` (`db/products.rs:248-258`) so it takes the column default, and the parent's tenant is also `'default'`, so the composite FK at `migrations/20260831_per_tenant_unique_rebuild.sql:173,199,216,239` is satisfied. The defect is that the wall is *reachable*, not that it is being hit.
  Fence: crates/kasirmu-core/src/db/products_crud.rs (`create_product_with_attributes` :251 and the `create_product` wrapper :224), db/products.rs:248, db/product_bundles.rs:112/:129/:182, db/tax/assignments.rs:44, plus the sibling `*_tests.rs` of each. A second slice carries the value from crates/kasirmu-bridge/src/products.rs:662-735, bundles.rs:135-176, product_variants.rs:266-313.
  Shape, decided by the D9 analysis and NOT to be re-litigated: an explicit `tenant_id: &str` PARAMETER on the writer, never a field on `Store`. There are already **54** `tenant_id: &str` parameter sites in `crates/kasirmu-core/src/db`, and `db/offline.rs:305-310` states why: the parameter exists because `enqueue_offline_priority` hardcoded `"default"` and "enqueues another store's sale under `default` - a real pre-existing bug". `Store::new(` occurs 1,560 times, so a field is either a 1,560-site edit or a silent default, and a silent default is exactly the bug the parameter was invented to prevent.
  The `create_product` wrapper keeps passing `"default"` explicitly so roughly 160 legacy callers and their `'default'` assertions stay green - parameterization must NOT change desktop behaviour, because a real tenant value on desktop bricks the next boot per C34.
  Done when: a test calls `create_product_with_attributes(.., "tenant-a")` on `fresh_db()`, then the variant/bundle/bundle-item/tax writers, and asserts all four child rows carry `tenant-a` **and that no `ConstraintViolation` occurred** (that is the ordering-by-construction proof); a second test pins that `create_product` still writes `'default'`. media_assets, media_thumbnails, payment_gateways and payment_settlements have no production writer at all - they are declared tables. Keeping them in `RLS_TABLES` makes the list's length a claim rather than a fact and hides the four tables that genuinely do need work. Move them to `RLS_EXEMPT` with a reason naming the absence of a writer, then regenerate `crates/kasirmu-core/migrations/20260813_init.pg.sql` (generated, never hand-edited).
  Fence: scripts/generate-pg-migration.py, crates/kasirmu-core/migrations/20260813_init.pg.sql.
  The premise is now WITNESSED, not assumed: the worker on this slice proves the absence of a writer itself, per table, and reports the greps. A wrong exemption is worse than the current state - a table that IS written must stay in `RLS_TABLES`.
  Done when: the generator's two lists are consistent with its own rule, the regenerated SQL is idempotent (running the generator twice is byte-identical), and the PG schema-drift gate passes.


## C17 work list (from the IPC gate-debt inventory; ceilings measured 75 desktop / 95 tablet during remediation)

**Slice 2 finding (recorded):** three of the four tablet settings reads are dead, but `get_hardware_settings` is NOT - `ui/src/hooks/useTerminalHardware.ts:240` calls it from a production fallback arm whenever the session token is null, and the null initial state makes that reachable. So it is queued as **C17b**: retire that fallback (wait for the token, or fail the card closed visibly) and only then delete the command and its ledger row, with an acceptance that proves the UI still resolves hardware settings with a session. The IPC parity gate independently confirms the split - it lists the three dead doors among the 15 commands named by no shipped UI file, and omits hardware.

The ordered, lowest-risk-first list is in the inventory recorded in the journal: (1) delete the tablet's five ungated history reads and their registrations, because the scoped twins are already live and the UI already prefers them; (2) the tablet's four ungated settings reads; (3) the three ungated branding setters; (4) the ungated create_backup and get_backup_status; (5) six vendor/value wrappers; (6) five more; (7) set_setting's renderer-supplied user_id (needs a decision first: the ungated door writes the GLOBAL identity database while the scoped twin writes the store database, so they are not interchangeable); (8) five tablet wrappers; (9) hoist the tablet pos/refunds permission checks out of private run_* helpers into the wrapper bodies so the sweep can see them; (10) route the tablet promotions wrappers through the bridge shims that already enforce PROMOTIONS_*. Per deletion: remove the command and its registration, lower REGISTERED_FLOOR, DEBT_CEILING and the matching class count together, regenerate with KASIRMU_REGENERATE_GATE_LEDGER=1, then run both registration_gate_tests files. Ceilings may only fall.

**Owner-blocked, each waiting on one question (do not guess these):**
- **C1 stage 2** (per-install keychain key + `oz rekey`): is `OZ_MASTER_KEY` set on any deployment, and is whole-file .db credential portability a requirement? The reader is already branch-tolerant, which was the precondition.
- **C2 signature verification + operator grant store**: D7 - how much plugin trust is acceptable. The silent hot-swap is already removed, so the remaining risk is a first load of a dropped file.
- **C7 role cutover**: has any PostgreSQL deployment run scripts/rls-cutover.sql? Detection now reports the posture at boot, so the answer is one HTTP call away.
- **C26 architecture**: rule versus tier order, deadline **2026-11-07** (the grandfathered entries expire then and the checker treats expired as blocking).
- **C17 slice 3** (desktop create_backup): does the pre-login updater keep a session-less door as a documented exemption? And does set_setting's global-identity-vs-store-database split allow a straight repoint to its scoped twin?

**Two operational lessons from implementation, worth keeping:**
- **Verify worker artifacts, not the roster.** Four workers in this wave stopped silently - two produced nothing, one produced complete green work it never committed, one wrote nothing at all - while still reporting running or ready. A file mtime, a grep for the expected marker, or running the acceptance yourself is the reliable signal, and a commit SHA is the only tick worth recording.
- **A worker that cannot demonstrate a failing test should say so.** Three slices here landed with a proven RED: the concurrency test failed against the unfixed SQL first (sale-status CAS, cash payout, open_shift). Two others correctly reported that no discriminating test exists for their change (the fiscal sequence wrap, the C13 tax bounds) rather than shipping a test that would pass either way.

A tick without a command in the log is not a tick. If a command here fails, the item it belongs to is NOT done, whatever its checkbox says.
